# u4.43.01 Operator DEFI_MATERIAU

## 1 Purpose

Define the parameters or physical characteristics of a material, which will be used for the expression of the constitutive laws, or the parameters associated with fatigue, damage, or simplified methods.

The physical parameters currently accepted by this operator concern the following domains: **Mechanics** and **Thermal**, linear or non-linear, **Metallurgical** for the modelling of steels and Zircaloy, **Hydration** and **Drying** for concretes, **Fluid** for acoustics, **Thermo-Hydro-Mechanics** for the modelling of saturated porous media in coupled thermo-mechanics, and **Soil Mechanics**.

If necessary, one and the same material can be defined in a call to DEFI_MATERIAU with several series of physical parameters.

Produces a data structure of type `mater`.

## 2 Syntax

```text
mater = DEFI_MATERIAU(
    ◇ reuse = <objet de MATER>,
    ◇ MATER = mater,
    ◆ | TABLE = _F(
           ◆ TABLE = table,
           ◆ NOM_PARA = / "IRRA",
                        / "TEMP",
           ◆ COMPOR = / "ELAS_COQUE_FO",
                      / "ELAS_FO",
                      / "ELAS_ORTH_FO",
                      / "THER_FO",
                      / "THER_NL",
                      / "THER_NL_ORTH",
           ◇ INTERPOL = / "LIN" (par défaut),
                        / "LOG",
           ◇ PROL_DROITE = / "CONSTANT",
                           / "EXCLU" (par défaut),
                           / "LINEAIRE",
           ◇ PROL_GAUCHE = / "CONSTANT",
                           / "EXCLU" (par défaut),
                           / "LINEAIRE",
        ),
      | ELAS = _F(
           ◆ / E = float,
             / K = float,
             / CELE_P = float,
           ◇ & NU = float,
           ◇ & MU = float,
           ◇ & CELE_S = float,
           ◇ & RHO = float,
           ◇ ALPHA = float,
           ◇ AMOR_ALPHA = float,
           ◇ AMOR_BETA = float,
           ◇ AMOR_HYST = float,
           ◇ K_DESSIC = float (défaut: 0.0),
           ◇ B_ENDOGE = float (défaut: 0.0),
           ◇ LONG_CARA = float,
           ◇ COEF_AMOR = float (défaut: 1.0),
        ),
      | ELAS_FO = _F(
           ◆ E = fonction / formule / nappe,
           ◆ NU = fonction / formule / nappe,
           ◇ RHO = fonction / formule / nappe,
           ◇ & TEMP_DEF_ALPHA = float,
             & ALPHA = fonction / formule,
           ◇ PRECISION = float (défaut: 1.0),

           ◇ AMOR_BETA = fonction / formule / nappe,

           ◇ K_DESSIC = float (défaut: 0.0),
           ◇ B_ENDOGE = float (défaut: 0.0),
           ◇ LONG_CARA = float,
           ◇ COEF_AMOR = float (défaut: 1.0),
        ),
      | ELAS_FLUI = _F(
           ◆ E = float,

               | D_VISC_TEMP = fonction / formule / nappe,
          ),
    ◇ INFO = / 1 (par défaut),
             / 2,
)

◆ : obligatoire
◇ : optionnel
⟐ : présent par défaut
& : ensemble
/ : un seul parmi
| : plusieurs choix possibles
```

## 3 Characteristics from a table

Definition of the constant or function linear elastic characteristics.

The keyword TABLE simplifies the writing of DEFI_MATERIAU for certain constitutive behaviours (COMPOR) by reading the data from a table. The TABLE is used to create functions.

The parameters are stored in columns, with an additional column giving the abscissa of the functions that are created (NOM_PARA). For example, the following table makes it possible to define an elastic constitutive behaviour.

| TEMP | E | NU | RHO | ALPHA | TEMP_DEF_ALPHA |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 20 | 200 | 0,3 | 1000 | 1,E-5 | 20,0 |
| 50 | 190 | 0,3 | 1000 | 1,E-5 | 20,0 |

## 4 General elastic characteristics

### 4.1 Factor keywords ELAS, ELAS_FO

Definition of the constant or function linear elastic characteristics.

Most of the functions may depend on the command variables (see [U4.43.03](doc:u4.43.03)) or on other parameters such as time ('INST' or the geometry 'X', 'Y', 'Z')

'TEMP', 'HYDR', 'SECH', 'NEUT1', 'NEUT2', 'X', 'Y', 'Z',…

Warning! For the geometry, there are two cases:

- The direct variables 'X', 'Y', 'Z', which work only in a few particular cases;
- The command variable 'GEOM', which is more general;

#### 4.1.1 Operands E/NU, K/MU, CELE_P/CELE_S

One of the three pairs (E, NU), (K, MU) or (CELE_P, CELE_S) must be supplied.

E is Young's modulus. One checks that $E \ge 0$ .

NU is Poisson's ratio. One checks that $-1. \le \nu \le 0.5$ .

If one supplies (K, MU= $\mu$ ), one assigns instead:

If one supplies (CELE_P= $V_P$ , CELE_S= $V_S$) then RHO is necessary and one assigns instead:

#### 4.1.2 Operand RHO

Density. No check of the order of magnitude. Warning: for THM modellings in statics, only the homogenised density entered under THM_DIFFU (cf. section  10.7.2 ) will be taken into account. For THM modellings in dynamics, this value must on the contrary be entered here and be consistent with that indicated under THM_DIFFU.

**This keyword can be a function only of the geometry via the command variable 'GEOM'.**

#### 4.1.3 Operands ALPHA/TEMP_DEF_ALPHA/PRECISION

Isotropic thermal expansion coefficient.

The thermal expansion coefficient is a mean expansion coefficient which may depend on the temperature $T$ .

The values of the expansion coefficients are determined by dilatometry tests which take place at ambient temperature ($0°C$  or more generally $20°C$ ).

As a result, one generally has available the values of the expansion coefficient defined with respect to $20°C$ (the temperature at which the thermal strain is assumed to be zero).

Certain studies require a reference temperature different from the ambient temperature (thermal strain zero for a temperature other than the ambient temperature). One must then carry out a change of reference frame in the computation of the thermal strain [R4.08.01](doc:r4.08.01).

TEMP_DEF_ALPHA is the value of the temperature at which the values of the thermal expansion coefficient were determined, and were entered under the keyword ALPHA.

This keyword becomes mandatory as soon as one has entered ALPHA.

For non-THM constitutive behaviours, the thermal strain is computed by the formula [R4.08.01](doc:r4.08.01):

${e}^{\mathit{th}}\left(T\right)=\widehat{\alpha}\left(T\right)\left(T-{T}_{\mathit{ref}}\right)$ with $\widehat{\alpha}\left(T\right)=\frac{\alpha \left(T\right)\left(T-{T}_{\text{def}}\right)-\alpha \left({T}_{\mathit{ref}}\right)\left({T}_{\mathit{ref}}-{T}_{\text{def}}\right)}{T-{T}_{\mathit{ref}}}$

and

${e}^{\mathit{th}}\left({T}_{\mathit{ref}}\right)=0$

**Note**

It is not possible to use a formula or a nappe for ALPHA, because of the modifications to be taken into account described above. The parameter ALPHA can depend ONLY on the temperature and provided that it is a function. The user, if wishing to use a formula, must first tabulate it by means of the command CALC_FONC_INTERP.

This keyword is used when the keyword TEMP_DEF_ALPHA is specified.

It is a real which indicates with what precision a temperature ${T}_{i}$ (from the list of temperatures used to define $\alpha {\left({T}_{i}\right)}_{i=1,N}$ ) is close to the reference temperature ${T}_{\text{ref}}$ .

This real is used for the computation of the function $\widehat{\alpha}\left({T}_{i}\right)$ . The mathematical formula allowing the computation of $\widehat{\alpha}\left({T}_{i}\right)$ differs according to whether ${T}_{i}\ne {T}_{\text{ref}}$ or ${T}_{i}={T}_{\text{ref}}$ .

In THM, the computation of the thermal strain is different (see [R4.08.01](doc:r4.08.01)). The thermal strain is evaluated by the following formula:

${e}^{\mathit{th}}\left(T\right)=\alpha \left(T-{T}_{\mathit{ini}}\right)$

In particular, one does not use TEMP_DEF_ALPHA (but one must enter it) and ALPHA cannot be a function; it is necessarily a constant. Moreover, the reference temperature is given by THM_INIT/TEMP.

#### 4.1.4 Operands AMOR_ALPHA/AMOR_BETA/AMOR_HYST

```text
AMOR_ALPHA = a_alpha

AMOR_BETA = a_beta
```

Coefficients $\alpha$ and $\beta$ making it possible to build a viscous damping matrix proportional to the stiffness and/or to the mass $[C]=\alpha [K]+\beta [M]$ . One will refer to the documents on the modelling of mechanical damping [U2.06.03] and [R5.05.04](doc:r5.05.04).

```text
AMOR_HYST = eta
```

Hysteretic damping coefficient $\eta$ making it possible to define the complex Young's modulus (viscoelastic material) from which the complex stiffness matrix will be created, allowing the computation of the harmonic response [U2.06.03](doc:u2.06.03) and [R5.05.04](doc:r5.05.04).

- The presence of the keywords AMOR_ALPHA and AMOR_BETA associated with a zero value may lead, in certain algorithms, to assembling a damping matrix and thus generates additional computation costs.
- These keywords may depend on the geometry via the direct variables 'X', 'Y' and 'Z' but not on the command variable 'GEOM'.

**Notes:**

#### 4.1.5 Operands COEF_AMOR /LONG_CARA

For the justification of the use of these operands, one will refer to the documents on the modelling of absorbing boundary elements [R4.02.05](doc:r4.02.05).

```text
COEF_AMOR = coeam
```

Coefficient appearing in the expression of the stress vector integrated along the absorbing boundary of the finite-element domain and which corresponds to viscous dampers distributed along this boundary:

${\mathrm{t}}_{0}=\mathit{coeam}(\rho {c}_{P}\frac{\partial {u}_{3}}{\partial t}{\mathrm{e}}_{3}+\rho {c}_{S}\frac{\partial {\mathrm{u}}^{'}}{\partial t})$

This term is assigned to the constitutive material of the absorbing-boundary element.

```text
LONG_CARA = lcara
```

Coefficient corresponding to a characteristic dimension $L$ . Associated with the Lamé coefficients $\lambda$ and $\mu$ , this coefficient appears in the expression of the added term of the stress vector integrated along the absorbing boundary of the finite-element domain, which corresponds to stiffnesses distributed along this boundary of the finite-element domain:

${\mathrm{t}}_{1}=\frac{\lambda +2\mu }{L}{u}_{3}{\mathrm{e}}_{3}+\frac{\mu}{L}{\mathrm{u}}^{'}$

This term is assigned to the constitutive material of the absorbing-boundary element.

**Note**

The absence of the keyword LONG_CARA has the effect that no added stiffness is assigned on the absorbing boundary.

#### 4.1.6 Operands K_DESSIC/B_ENDOGE

```text
K_DESSIC = k
```

Desiccation shrinkage coefficient.

```text
K_ENDOGE = e
```

Autogenous shrinkage coefficient.

These characteristics are used with the constitutive behaviours of concrete (see ref. [R7.01.12]).

### 4.2 Keyword ELAS_VISCO

The keyword ELAS_VISCO makes it possible to define isotropic viscoelastic materials for a given frequency. It is simply the definition of an elastic material whose elastic moduli are complex.

#### 4.2.1 Operands G/NU

```text
G = g
```

Complex shear modulus.

```text
NU = nu
```

Complex Poisson's ratio.

**Note**

In the command file one must necessarily enter complex numbers even if the imaginary part is zero; for example if nu is real, one must enter complex(nu, 0).

#### 4.2.2 Operand RHO

```text
RHO = rho
```

Density. No check of the order of magnitude.

### 4.3 Factor keyword ELAS_FLUI

The keyword ELAS_FLUI makes it possible to define the equivalent density of a tubular structure with internal and external fluid, taking the confinement effect into account.

This operation falls within the study of the dynamic behaviour of a configuration of the type "tube bundle under cross-flow". The study of the behaviour of the bundle is reduced to the study of a single tube representative of the whole bundle. Cf. [U4.35.02](doc:u4.35.02).

The equivalent density of the structure ${\rho}_{\mathit{eq}}$ is defined by:

$\begin{array}{cc}{\rho}_{\mathrm{eq}}=\frac{1}{({d}_{e}^{2}-{d}_{i}^{2})}\left[{\rho}_{\mathrm{i.}}{d}_{i}^{2}+{\rho}_{\mathrm{t.}}({d}_{e}^{2}-{d}_{i}^{2})+{\rho}_{e}.{d}_{e}^{2}\right]& \\ {d}_{\mathrm{eq}}^{2}=\frac{2.{C}_{m}.{d}_{e}^{2}}{\pi}& \end{array}$

${\rho}_{i}$ , ${\rho}_{e}$ , ${\rho}_{t}$  are respectively the density of the fluid, of the external fluid and of the structure.

${d}_{e}$ , ${d}_{i}$ are respectively the external and internal diameter of the tube.

${C}_{m}$ is an added-mass coefficient (which defines the confinement).

#### 4.3.1 Operands RHO/E/NU

```text
RHO = rho
```

Density of the material.

```text
E = yg
```

Young's modulus.

```text
NU = nu
```

Poisson's ratio.

#### 4.3.2 Operands PROF_RHO_F_INT/PROF_RHO_F_EXIT/COEF_MASS_AJOU

```text
PROF_RHO_F_INT = rhoi
```

Concept of type [fonction] defining the density profile of the internal fluid along the tube. This function is parameterised by the curvilinear abscissa.

```text
PROF_RHO_F_EXT = rhoe
```

Concept of type [fonction] defining the density profile of the external fluid along the tube. This function is parameterised by the curvilinear abscissa, 'ABSC'.

```text
COEF_MASS_AJOU = fonc_cm
```

Concept of type [fonction] produced by the operator FONC_FLUI_STRU [U4.35.02].

This constant function, parameterised by the curvilinear abscissa, supplies the value of the added-mass coefficient ${C}_{m}$ .

### 4.4 Factor keyword CABLE

Definition of the constant non-linear elastic characteristic for cables: two different elastic constitutive behaviours in tension and in compression, defined by Young's moduli E and EC (modulus in compression).

The standard characteristics of the elastic material are to be entered under the factor keyword ELAS.

#### 4.4.1 Elasticity operands

```text
◊    EC_SUR_E = ecse
```

Ratio of the moduli in compression and in tension. If the compression modulus is zero, the global linear system in the displacements may become singular. This is the case when a node is connected only to cables and all of them go into compression.

### 4.5 Factor keywords ELAS_ORTH, ELAS_ORTH_FO, ELAS_VISCO_ORTH

Definition of the orthotropic elastic characteristics, constant or functions of the temperature, for the isoparametric continuum elements or the constituent layers of a composite [R4.01.02](doc:r4.01.02). It is possible to define constant orthotropic viscoelastic characteristics (ELAS_VISCO_ORTH) at a given frequency; the moduli to be entered are then complex.

#### 4.5.1 Elasticity operands

To define the orthotropy frame $(\text{L},\text{T},\text{N})$ attached to the elements, the reader will refer:

- for the isoparametric continuum elements, to the documentation [U4.42.01](doc:u4.42.01) AFFE_CARA_ELEM keyword MASSIF,
- for the composite shell elements, to the documentation [U4.42.01](doc:u4.42.01) AFFE_CARA_ELEM keyword COQUE as well as to the documentation [U4.42.03](doc:u4.42.03) DEFI_COMPOSITE keyword ORIENTATION.

![Figure](/figures/u4.43.01/10000000000002B400000120A136E5D1F51DEE50.png)

```text
E_L = ygl
```

Longitudinal Young's modulus.

```text
E_T = ygt
```

Transverse Young's modulus.

```text
E_N = ygn
```

Normal Young's modulus.

```text
GL_T = glt
```

Shear modulus in the plane $\text{LT}$.

```text
G_TN = gtn
```

Shear modulus in the plane $\text{TN}$.

```text
G_LN = gln
```

Shear modulus in the plane $\text{LN}$.

**Note:**

*For shells, the transverse shear moduli are not mandatory; in this case, one computes with a thin shell by assigning an infinite stiffness to the transverse shear (elements DST, DSQ and Q4G).*

```text
NU_LT = nult
```

Poisson's ratio in the plane $\text{LT}$.

**Important notes:**

$\mathit{nult}$ *is not equal to* $\mathit{nutl}$. In fact, one has the relation: $\mathit{nutl}=\frac{\mathit{ygt}}{\mathit{ygl}}.\mathit{nult}$

$\mathit{nult}$ *must be interpreted in the following way:*

if one applies a tension along the axis* $L$ giving rise to a strain along this axis equal to ${\epsilon}_{L}=\frac{{\sigma}_{L}}{\mathit{ygl}}$, one has a strain along the axis $T$ equal to: ${\epsilon}_{T}=-\mathit{nult}.\frac{{\sigma}_{L}}{\mathit{ygl}}$.

The various elasticity coefficients *E_L, G_LN* and *NU_LN* cannot be chosen arbitrarily: physically, a non-zero strain must always produce a strictly positive strain energy. This is expressed by the fact that the Hooke matrix must be positive definite. The operator **DEFI_MATERIAU** computes the eigenvalues of this matrix and emits an alarm if this property is not satisfied.

For 2D models, as the user has not yet chosen the *MODELISATION (D_PLAN, *C_PLAN, ...), one checks the positivity of the matrix in the various cases.*

```text
NU_TN = nutn
```

Poisson's ratio in the plane $\text{TN}$.

```text
NU_LN = nuln
```

Poisson's ratio in the plane $\text{LN}$.

The note made for NU_LT is to be applied to these last two coefficients. One thus has the relations:

$\begin{array}{}\mathrm{nunt}=\frac{\mathrm{ygn}}{\mathrm{ygt}}.\mathrm{nutn}\\ \mathrm{nunl}=\frac{\mathrm{ygn}}{\mathrm{ygt}}.\mathrm{nuln}\end{array}$

**Note:**

In the command file one must necessarily enter complex numbers even if the imaginary part is zero; for example if nult is real, one must enter ‘ *complex(nult,0)* ’

#### 4.5.2 Special case of cubic elasticity

Cubic elasticity corresponds to an elasticity matrix of the form:

$\begin{array}{cccccc}{y}_{1111}& {y}_{1122}& {y}_{1122}& & & \\ {y}_{1122}& {y}_{1111}& {y}_{1122}& & & \\ {y}_{1122}& {y}_{1122}& {y}_{1111}& & & \\ & & & {y}_{1212}& & \\ & & & & {y}_{1212}& \\ & & & & & {y}_{1212}\end{array}$

Given the cubic symmetry, 3 coefficients remain to be determined:

${E}_{L}={E}_{N}={E}_{T}=E,{G}_{\text{LT}}={G}_{\text{LN}}={G}_{\text{TN}}=G,{\nu}_{\text{LN}}={\nu}_{\text{LT}}={\nu}_{\text{LN}}=\nu$

To reproduce cubic elasticity with ELAS_ORTH, it is sufficient to compute the orthotropy coefficients such that the elasticity matrix obtained is of the form above:

$\begin{array}{}{y}_{1111}=\frac{E(1-{\nu}^{2})}{(1-3{\nu}^{2}-2{\nu}^{3})}\\ {y}_{1122}=\frac{E\nu (1+\nu )}{(1-3{\nu}^{2}-2{\nu}^{3})}\\ {y}_{1212}={G}_{\text{LT}}={G}_{\ln}={G}_{\mathrm{TN}}\end{array}$

therefore, as long as $(1-3{\nu}^{2}-2{\nu}^{3})\ne 0$ (that is to say $\nu$ different from $0.5$ ).

$\frac{{y}_{1122}}{{y}_{1111}}=\frac{\nu}{1-\nu }$ which supplies $\begin{array}{ccc}\text{}& \text{}& \text{}\\ \nu & =& \frac{1}{1+\frac{{y}_{1111}}{{y}_{1122}}}\end{array}$ then $E={y}_{1111}\frac{(1-3{\nu}^{2}-2{\nu}^{3})}{(1-{\nu}^{2})}$

#### 4.5.3 Operand RHO

```text
RHO = rho
```

Density.

#### 4.5.4 Operands ALPHA_L / ALPHA_T / ALPHA_N

```text
ALPHA_L = dil
```

Mean longitudinal thermal expansion coefficient.

```text
ALPHA_T = dit
```

Mean transverse thermal expansion coefficient.

```text
ALPHA_N = din
```

Mean normal thermal expansion coefficient.

#### 4.5.5 Operands TEMP_DEF_ALPHA / PRECISION

One will refer to paragraph [§3.1.4]. This keyword becomes mandatory as soon as one has entered ALPHA_L , or ALPHA_T or ALPHA_N.

#### 4.5.6 Failure criteria

```text
XT = trl
```

Failure criterion in tension in the longitudinal direction (first direction of orthotropy).

```text
XC = col
```

Failure criterion in compression in the longitudinal direction.

```text
YT = trt
```

Failure criterion in tension in the transverse direction (second direction of orthotropy).

```text
YC = cot
```

Failure criterion in compression in the transverse direction.

```text
S_LT = cis
```

Failure criterion in shear in the plane $\text{LT}$ .

#### 4.5.7 Operands AMOR_ALPHA/AMOR_BETA/AMOR_HYST

See RefHeading__26094_760432467.

**Note**

In the case of multi-layer shells (use of DEFI_COMPOSITE), these parameters are not taken into account.

### 4.6 Factor keywords ELAS_ISTR, ELAS_ISTR_FO, ELAS_VISCO_ISTR

Definition of the elastic characteristics, constant (ELAS_ISTR) or functions of the temperature, in the case of transverse isotropy for the isoparametric continuum elements (ELAS_ISTR_FO). It is possible to define constant transversely isotropic viscoelastic characteristics (ELAS_VISCO_ISTR) at a given frequency; the moduli to be entered are then complex.

Taking up the same notations as for orthotropy, transverse isotropy means here that the isotropy is in the plane $(\text{L},\text{T})$ and that the orthotropy is therefore carried by the direction $\text{N}$ [R4.01.02](doc:r4.01.02). The reader's attention may be drawn to the fact that this convention differs from a usual convention which designates as the "longitudinal direction" the direction of orthotropy of transversely isotropic materials.

#### 4.6.1 Elasticity operands

To define a frame $(\text{L},\text{T},\text{N})$ attached to the elements and defining the transverse isotropy of the material, the latter being isotropic in the plane $\text{LT}$ , the reader will refer to the documentation [U4.42.01](doc:u4.42.01) AFFE_CARA_ELEM keyword MASSIF.

**Note:**

*The directions* $\text{L}$ *and* $\text{T}$ *are arbitrary in the plane* $\text{LT}$ *.*

![Figure](/figures/u4.43.01/100000000000036A000001A3AA3EF62BCD6F718D.png)

```text
E_L = ygl
```

Young's modulus in the plane $\text{LT}$ .

```text
E_N = ygn
```

Normal Young's modulus.

```text
GL_N = gln
```

Shear modulus in the plane $\text{LN}$ .

**Note:**

*The shear modulus in the plane* $\text{LT}$ *is defined by the usual formula for isotropic materials:* $G=\frac{E}{2(1+\nu )}$ *that is, here,* $\mathrm{glt}=\frac{\mathrm{ygl}}{2(1+\mathrm{nult})}$ *.*

```text
NU_LT = nult
```

Poisson's ratio in the plane $\text{LT}$ .

```text
NU_LN = nuln
```

Poisson's ratio in the plane $\text{LN}$ .

**Important notes:**

$\mathrm{nult}=\mathrm{nutl}$ *since the material has an isotropic character in the plane* $\text{LT}$ *, but* $\mathrm{nuln}$ *is not equal to* $\mathrm{nunl}$ *.*

*One has the relation:* $\mathrm{nunl}=\frac{\mathrm{ygn}}{\mathrm{ygl}}.\mathrm{nuln}$

$\mathrm{nunl}$ *must be interpreted in the following way:*

*if one applies a tension along the axis* $N$ *giving rise to a tensile strain along this axis equal to* ${\varepsilon}_{N}=\frac{{\sigma}_{N}}{\mathrm{ygn}}$ *, one has a compression along the axis* $L$ *equal to:* $\mathrm{nunl}.\frac{{\sigma}_{N}}{\mathrm{ygn}}$ *.*

*The various elasticity coefficients* *E_L,* *G_LN and* *NU_LN* *cannot be chosen arbitrarily: physically, a non-zero strain must always produce a strictly positive strain energy. This is expressed by the fact that the Hooke matrix must be positive definite. The operator* *DEFI_MATERIAU computes the eigenvalues of this matrix and emits an alarm if this property is not satisfied.*

*For 2D models, as the user has not yet chosen the* *MODELISATION (D_PLAN,* *C_PLAN, ...), one checks the positivity of the matrix in the various cases.*

#### 4.6.2 Operand RHO

```text
RHO = rho
```

Density.

#### 4.6.3 Operands ALPHA_L / ALPHA_N

```text
ALPHA_L = dil
```

Mean thermal expansion coefficient in the plane $\text{LT}$ .

```text
ALPHA_N = din
```

Mean normal thermal expansion coefficient.

#### 4.6.4 Operands TEMP_DEF_ALPHA / PRECISION

One will refer to paragraph [§3.1.4]. This keyword becomes mandatory as soon as one has entered the keyword ALPHA_L or ALPHA_N.

### 4.7 Factor keywords ELAS_COQUE , ELAS_COQUE_FO

ELAS_COQUE allows the user to supply directly the coefficients of the elasticity matrix (split into membrane and bending) of orthotropic thin shells in linear elasticity [R3.07.03](doc:r3.07.03).

The user may also indicate an isotropic thermal expansion coefficient alpha and a mass per unit area rho.

On the other hand, one restricts oneself to the cases in which the thermal expansion coefficient is homogeneous and isotropic.

The elastic constitutive matrices appearing in the stiffness matrix in homogeneous isotropic elasticity are of the form:

| Membrane: | Bending: | Shear: |
| --- | --- | --- |
| $\mathrm{C}=\frac{\mathit{Eh}}{1-{\nu}^{2}}\left(\begin{array}{ccc}1& \nu & 0\\ \nu & 1& 0\\ 0& 0& \left(\frac{1-\nu }{2}\right)\end{array}\right)$ | $\mathrm{D}=\frac{E{h}^{3}}{12\left(1-{\nu}^{2}\right)}\left(\begin{array}{ccc}1& \nu & 0\\ \nu & 1& 0\\ 0& 0& \left(\frac{1-\nu }{2}\right)\end{array}\right)$ | $\mathrm{G}=\frac{5\mathit{Eh}}{12\left(1+\nu \right)}\left(\begin{array}{cc}1& 0\\ 0& 1\end{array}\right)$ |

For orthotropic shells whose elastic moduli are obtained by a homogenisation method, it is not possible in the general case to find an equivalent Young's modulus $\mathit{Eeq}$ , and an equivalent thickness $\mathit{heq}$ in order to recover the previous expressions.

The elastic stiffness matrices in the absence of coupling relating the generalised forces to the generalised strains are therefore given directly in the form:

| Membrane: | Bending: | Shear: |
| --- | --- | --- |
| $\mathrm{C}=\left(\begin{array}{ccc}C1111& C1122& 0\\ C1122& C2222& 0\\ 0& 0& C1212\end{array}\right)$ | $\mathrm{D}=\left(\begin{array}{ccc}D1111& D1122& 0\\ D1122& D2222& 0\\ 0& 0& D1212\end{array}\right)$ | $\mathrm{G}=\left(\begin{array}{cc}G11& 0\\ 0& G22\end{array}\right)$ |

These coefficients are to be supplied in the local frame of the element. The latter is defined under the keyword COQUE of AFFE_CARA_ELEM [U4.42.01](doc:u4.42.01).

**Note concerning the taking into account of transverse shear according to the shell models:**

*If one wishes to use* *ELAS_COQUE with transverse shear one must necessarily employ the modelling* *DST. If one uses the modelling* *DKT, the transverse shear will not be taken into account, whatever the values of* $\mathit{G11}$ *and* $\mathit{G22}$ *. The correspondence for an isotropic material is the following:*

- *The material* *ELAS_COQUE, modelling* *DST with* $\mathit{CISA}\text{\_*}=5/12\times (\mathit{Eh}/(1+nu))$ *is equivalent to the material* *ELAS, modelling* *DST.*
- *The material* *ELAS_COQUE, modelling* *DST with* $\mathit{CISA}\text{\_*}=5/12\times (\mathit{Eh}/(1+nu))\times N$ *, where* $N$ *is a large number (for example* ${10}^{5}$ *), is equivalent to the material* *ELAS, modelling* *DKT.*
- *The material* *ELAS_COQUE, modelling* *DKT is equivalent to the material* *ELAS, modelling* *DKT.*

In the case where one must take the coupling terms into account, the elastic stiffness matrices relating the generalised forces to the generalised strains for the plate elements are defined in the following way:

| Membrane: | Bending: | Membrane-bending: |
| --- | --- | --- |
| $\mathit{HM}=\left\|\begin{array}{ccc}\phantom{\rule{2em}{0ex}}H1111& H1122& H1112\\ \phantom{\rule{2em}{0ex}}\mathit{sym}& H2222& H2212\\ \phantom{\rule{2em}{0ex}}\mathit{sym}& \mathit{sym}& H1212\end{array}\right\|$ | $\mathit{HF}=\left\|\begin{array}{ccc}\phantom{\rule{2em}{0ex}}A1111& A1122& A1112\\ \phantom{\rule{2em}{0ex}}\mathit{sym}& A2222& A2212\\ \phantom{\rule{2em}{0ex}}\mathit{sym}& \mathit{sym}& A1212\end{array}\right\|$ | $\mathit{HMF}=\left\|\begin{array}{ccc}\phantom{\rule{2em}{0ex}}B1111& B1122& B1112\\ \phantom{\rule{2em}{0ex}}\mathit{sym}& B2222& B2212\\ \phantom{\rule{4em}{0ex}}\mathit{sym}& \mathit{sym}& B1212\end{array}\right\|$ |

| Membrane-shear: | Bending-shear: | Shear: |
| --- | --- | --- |
| $\mathit{HMC}=\left\|\begin{array}{cc}\phantom{\rule{2em}{0ex}}E1113& E1123\\ \phantom{\rule{2em}{0ex}}E2213& E2223\\ \phantom{\rule{2em}{0ex}}E1213& E1223\end{array}\right\|$ | $\mathit{HFC}=\left\|\begin{array}{cc}\phantom{\rule{2em}{0ex}}F1113& F1123\\ \phantom{\rule{2em}{0ex}}F2213& F2223\\ \phantom{\rule{2em}{0ex}}F1213& F1223\end{array}\right\|$ | $\mathit{HC}=\left\|\begin{array}{cc}\phantom{\rule{2em}{0ex}}G1313& G1323\\ \phantom{\rule{2em}{0ex}}G1323& G2323\end{array}\right\|$ |

### 4.8 Factor keyword ELAS_MEMBRANE

ELAS_MEMBRANE allows the user to supply directly the coefficients of the elasticity matrix of anisotropic membranes in linear elasticity.

The elastic stiffness matrix relating the membrane stresses to the membrane strains for the membrane elements is defined in the following way:

${\mathrm{H}}_{\mathrm{M}}=\left(\begin{array}{ccc}H1111& H1122& H1112\\ H1122& H2222& H2212\\ H1112& H2212& H1212\end{array}\right)$

In the case of an isotropic elastic membrane, one must recover the matrix:

$\frac{\mathit{Eh}}{1-{\nu}^{2}}\left(\begin{array}{ccc}1& \nu & 0\\ \nu & 1& 0\\ 0& 0& \left(\frac{1-\nu }{2}\right)\end{array}\right)$

These coefficients are to be supplied in the local frame of the element, defined under the factor keyword MEMBRANE of the command AFFE_CARA_ELEM [U4.42.01](doc:u4.42.01). These coefficients have the dimension of a force per metre. Recall that one uses the following notation conventions for the membrane strains and stresses, and that the coefficients of the previous matrix must be adapted accordingly:

$\epsilon =\left(\begin{array}{c}{\epsilon}_{11}\\ {\epsilon}_{22}\\ \sqrt{2}{\epsilon}_{12}\end{array}\right)$                 $\sigma =\left(\begin{array}{c}{\sigma}_{11}\\ {\sigma}_{22}\\ \sqrt{2}{\sigma}_{12}\end{array}\right)$

The user may also indicate an isotropic thermal expansion coefficient alpha and a mass per unit area rho.

### 4.9 Factor keyword ELAS_HYPER

Definition of the hyperelastic characteristics of Signorini type [R5.03.19](doc:r5.03.19). The Piola–Kirchhoff stresses $\mathrm{S}$ are related to the Green–Lagrange strains by:

$\mathrm{S}=\frac{\partial \Psi }{\partial \mathrm{E}}$ with: $\Psi ={C}_{10}\left({I}_{1}-3\right)+{C}_{01}\left({I}_{2}-3\right)+{C}_{20}{\left({I}_{1}-3\right)}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}K{\left(J-1\right)}^{2}$ and  ${I}_{1}={I}_{c}{J}^{-\frac{2}{3}},{I}_{2}={\mathit{II}}_{c}{J}^{-\frac{4}{3}},J={\mathit{III}}_{c}^{\frac{1}{2}}$

where ${I}_{c}$ , ${\mathit{II}}_{c}$ and ${\mathit{III}}_{c}$ are the 3 invariants of the right Cauchy–Green tensor.

#### 4.9.1 Operands C01, C10 and C20

C01 = c01, C10 = c10, C20 = c20

The three coefficients of the polynomial expression of the hyperelastic potential. The unit is the $N/{m}^{2}$ .

- If ${\mathrm{C}}_{01}$ and ${\mathrm{C}}_{20}$ are zero, one obtains a material of Neo-Hookean type.
- If only ${\mathrm{C}}_{20}$ is zero, one obtains a material of Mooney–Rivlin type.

The material is elastic and incompressible in small strains if one takes ${\mathrm{C}}_{10}$ and ${\mathrm{C}}_{01}$ such that $6\left({\mathrm{C}}_{01}+{\mathrm{C}}_{10}\right)=E$ , where $E$ is Young's modulus.

#### 4.9.2 Operands NU and K

NU= nu

Poisson's ratio. One checks that $-1<nu<0.5$ .

K = k

Bulk modulus.

These two parameters are mutually exclusive (when one of the two parameters is introduced, the other cannot be). They quantify the near-incompressibility of the material. One uses the bulk modulus $K$ supplied by the user, if it exists. Otherwise one computes $K$ by:

$K=\frac{6\left({C}_{01}+{C}_{10}\right)}{3\left(1-2\nu \right)}$ .

One may take $nu$ close to $0.5$ but never strictly equal (to within machine precision). If $nu$ is too close to $0.5$ , an error message invites the user to check the Poisson's ratio or the bulk modulus. The larger the bulk modulus, the more incompressible the material.

#### 4.9.3 Operand RHO

```text
RHO = rho
```

Constant real density (one does not accept a concept of type fonction). No check of the order of magnitude.

### 4.10 Factor keyword ELAS_HYPER_VISC

Definition of the visco-hyperelastic characteristics [R5.03.19](doc:r5.03.19). The viscous flow is taken into account by adding a Prony series of order $n$ to the long-term second Piola–Kirchhoff tensor PK2. The Piola–Kirchhoff stresses $\mathrm{S}$ are related to the Green–Lagrange strains by:

$\mathrm{S}=\frac{\partial \Psi }{\partial \mathrm{E}}+\sum_{i=1}^NH_{i}$
with: $\Psi ={C}_{10}\left({I}_{1}-3\right)+{C}_{01}\left({I}_{2}-3\right)+{C}_{20}{\left({I}_{1}-3\right)}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}K{\left(J-1\right)}^{2}$

and  $H_{i}|_{t+\triangle t}=\exp(-\frac{dt}{\tau_{i}})H_{i}|_{t}+g_i \tau_{i}(1-\exp(-\frac{dt}{\tau_{i}}))\frac{(S^{iso}|_{t+\triangle t}-S^{iso}|_{t})}{dt}$.

#### 4.10.1 Operands C01, C10 and C20

C01 = c01, C10 = c10, C20 = c20

The three coefficients of the polynomial expression of the long-term hyperelastic potential. The unit is the $N/{m}^{2}$ .

- If ${\mathrm{C}}_{01}$ and ${\mathrm{C}}_{20}$ are zero, one obtains a material of Neo-Hookean type.
- If only ${\mathrm{C}}_{20}$ is zero, one obtains a material of Mooney–Rivlin type.

The material is elastic and incompressible in small strains if one takes ${\mathrm{C}}_{10}$ and ${\mathrm{C}}_{01}$ such that $6\left({\mathrm{C}}_{01}+{\mathrm{C}}_{10}\right)=E$ , where $E$ is Young's modulus.

#### 4.10.2 Operand K

K = k

Bulk modulus. It quantifies the near-incompressibility of the material. The larger $K$ is, the more incompressible the material. The user may compute it from Poisson's ratio and from the coefficients of the hyperelastic potential of the instantaneous constitutive behaviour as in the case of ELAS_HYPER, cf. § 4.9.2.

#### 4.10.3 Operands G and TAU

G = g

List of length $n$ (order of the expansion of the Prony series) of long-term shear relaxation moduli.

TAU = tau

List of length $n$ containing the relaxation times.

### 4.11 Factor keyword HYPER_HILL

Definition of the characteristics of the compressible hyperelastic law of Hill  [R5.03.19](doc:r5.03.19). The potential of this law is expressed in the following way:

With, $\lambda_1, \lambda_2, \lambda_3$ principal stretches, $J$ volume change.

#### 4.11.1 Operands ALPHA, BETA and MU

ALPHA = alpha, BETA = beta , MU = mu

Lists of length $n$ (order of the expansion of the Hill law), presenting the coefficients of the law.

As regards the coefficients:

- The values of the list mu, ($\mu_i$) are related to the initial shear modulus $\mu$ by the relation $\sum_{i=1}^{n} \mu_i = \mu$,
- The values of the list beta, ($\beta_i$) are related to the Poisson's ratios $\nu_i$ by the relation $\beta_i = \frac{\nu_i}{1 - 2\nu_i}$.

### 4.12 Factor keyword SECOND_ELAS

Definition of the isotropic linear elastic characteristics of the second-gradient model proposed by Mindlin and detailed in the documentation [R5.04.03](doc:r5.04.03). This constitutive behaviour is used for the second-gradient dilatation regularisation modellings (* _DIL).

#### 4.12.1 Operand A1

This parameter defines the material characteristics of the law described in the document [R5.04.03](doc:r5.04.03).

### 4.13 Factor keyword ELAS_GLRC

Definition of the constant linear elastic characteristics of a homogenised plate for the laws GLRC_DM and GLRC_DAMAGE.

#### 4.13.1 Operands E_M/NU_M/E_F/NU_F/BT1/BT2

```text
E_M = em
```

Membrane Young's modulus. One checks that ${E}_{m}\ge 0$ .

```text
NU_M = num
```

Membrane Poisson's ratio. One checks that $-1.\le {\nu}_{m}\le 0.5$ .

```text
E_F = ef
```

Bending Young's modulus. One checks that ${E}_{f}\ge 0$ .

```text
NU_F = nuf
```

Bending Poisson's ratio. One checks that $-1.\le {\nu}_{f}\le 0.5$ .

```text
BT1 = bt1 et BT2 = bt2
```

In the case where the finite elements support the computation of the shear forces, these operands serve to define the elastic stiffness matrix of transverse shear. The shear forces $V$ are related to the shear strains $\gamma$ by:

$\mathrm{V}=\left(\begin{array}{cc}\mathit{BT}1& 0\\ 0& \mathit{BT}2\end{array}\right).\gamma$

The other operands are identical to those of linear elasticity, cf. § 4.1 .

### 4.14 Factor keyword ELAS_DHRC

Definition of the constant linear elastic characteristics of a homogenised plate for the law DHRC.

#### 4.14.1 Operands A0

```text
A0= a0
```

Components ($21$  supra-diagonal terms) of the symmetric membrane-bending elasticity tensor ${A}^{0}$ of the plate before damage, of order $4$ , in the reinforcement frame $(x,y)$ , in Voigt notation, identified by homogenisation: first in membrane, then in bending (unit of force per unit length for the membrane terms, unit of force for the coupled membrane-bending terms, and unit of force times unit of length for the bending terms):

$(\begin{array}{cccccc}{A}_{\mathrm{xxxx}}^{\mathrm{0mm}}& {A}_{\mathrm{xxyy}}^{\mathrm{0mm}}& {A}_{\mathrm{xxxy}}^{\mathrm{0mm}}& {A}_{\mathrm{xxxx}}^{\mathrm{0mf}}& {A}_{\mathrm{xxyy}}^{\mathrm{0mf}}& {A}_{\mathrm{xxxy}}^{\mathrm{0mf}}\\ \text{}& {A}_{\mathrm{yyyy}}^{\mathrm{0mm}}& {A}_{\mathrm{yyxy}}^{\mathrm{0mm}}& {A}_{\mathrm{yyxx}}^{\mathrm{0mf}}& {A}_{\mathrm{yyyy}}^{\mathrm{0mf}}& {A}_{\mathrm{yyxy}}^{\mathrm{0mf}}\\ \text{}& \text{}& {A}_{\mathrm{xyxy}}^{\mathrm{0mm}}& {A}_{\mathrm{xyxx}}^{\mathrm{0mf}}& {A}_{\mathrm{xyyy}}^{\mathrm{0mf}}& {A}_{\mathrm{xyxy}}^{\mathrm{0mf}}\\ \text{}& \text{}& \text{}& {A}_{\mathrm{xxxx}}^{\mathrm{0ff}}& {A}_{\mathrm{xxyy}}^{\mathrm{0ff}}& {A}_{\mathrm{xxxy}}^{\mathrm{0ff}}\\ \text{}& \text{}& \text{}& \text{}& {A}_{\mathrm{yyyy}}^{\mathrm{0ff}}& {A}_{\mathrm{yyxy}}^{\mathrm{0ff}}\\ \text{}& \text{}& \text{}& \text{}& \text{}& {A}_{\mathrm{xyxy}}^{\mathrm{0ff}}\end{array})$

The other operands are identical to those of linear elasticity, cf. § 4.1 .

### 4.15 Factor keywords VISC_ELAS

A regularisation mechanism by viscoelasticity is introduced in the command STAT_NON_LINE via the keyword REGU_VISC = ‘OUI’. The corresponding Maxwell model rests on two parameters, a stiffness $k$ (Pa) and a characteristic time $\tau$ (s). It is described in [R5.03.34](doc:r5.03.34).

### 4.16 Factor keyword CZM_ELAS

This factor keyword makes it possible to specify the parameters of the elastic interface law CZM_ELAS_MIX (see [R7.02.11](doc:r7.02.11)).

#### 4.16.1 Operands RIGI_TAN, RIGI_NOR, RIGI_NOR_TRAC and RIGI_NOR_COMP

The keyword RIGI_TAN makes it possible to define the tangential stiffness of the interface (transverse isotropy). The keyword RIGI_NOR makes it possible to define the normal stiffness of the interface, provided that it is identical in tension and in compression. One may also choose different normal stiffnesses in tension and in compression via the keywords RIGI_NOR_TRAC and RIGI_NOR_COMP respectively.

In the absence of these keywords, the corresponding stiffnesses are zero.

#### 4.16.2 Operand ADHE_NOR

This keyword makes it possible to characterise the constitutive behaviour normal to the interface. The default value, 'ELAS', corresponds to an elastic response (potentially distinct in tension and in compression); the stiffness is then defined via RIGI_NOR or, alternatively, RIGI_NOR_TRAC and RIGI_NOR_COMP. The value 'UNILATER' refers to a contact-type constitutive behaviour (zero interpenetration) in closure and an elastic one in opening (equivalent to an infinite stiffness in compression). Finally, the value 'PARFAITE' stipulates that the normal displacement jump is zero, that is to say that there is perfect adherence in the direction normal to the interface (equivalent to an infinite normal stiffness).

#### 4.16.3 Operand ADHE_TAN

This keyword makes it possible to characterise the constitutive behaviour tangent to the interface. The default value, 'ELAS', corresponds to an elastic response; the stiffness kt is then defined via RIGI_TAN. The value 'PARFAITE' stipulates that the tangential displacement jump (slip) is zero, that is to say that there is perfect adherence in the direction tangent to the interface (equivalent to an infinite tangential stiffness).

#### 4.16.4 Operand PENA_LAGR_ABSO

This keyword (mandatory) makes it possible to enter the value of the augmentation coefficient of the augmented Lagrangian, cf. [R7.02.11](doc:r7.02.11). Unlike other models, it is not supplied in a manner relative to other parameters (because they may all be zero) but indeed in an absolute manner. Its dimension is that of a pressure per unit length.

### 4.17 Factor keyword INTERF_POU_ELAS

This factor keyword makes it possible to specify the elastic parameters of the soil–pile interaction laws INTERF_POU_ELAS and INTERF_POU_CINE (see [R7.01.50](doc:r7.01.50)) applicable to the elements 3D_INTSOPLIEU (see [R7.01.49](doc:r7.01.49)).

#### 4.17.1 Operands K_T, K_N

The keyword K_T makes it possible to define the stiffness of the soil–pile interface along the axis of the pile. The keyword K_T defines the stiffness orthogonal to the axis. If Z is the direction of the axis of the pile, then K_T is equal for all the directions of the plane (X,Y).


## 5 General non-linear mechanical constitutive behaviours

In general, the definition of a non-linear mechanical constitutive behaviour requires on the one hand the definition of the elastic properties and on the other hand those relating to the non-linear aspect proper. In *Code_Aster*, these two types of data are defined separately, except for a few exceptions.

### 5.1 Factor keyword TRACTION

Definition of a traction curve (Von Mises elastoplasticity with non-linear isotropic hardening or non-linear elasticity).

#### 5.1.1 Operand SIGM


```text
SIGM = sigm_f
```


Curve $\sigma$ as a function of the total strain $\varepsilon$ (one checks that the concept fonction indeed depends only on the parameters EPSI and possibly TEMP).

The ordinate of the first point defines the elastic limit of the material; it is therefore imperative not to define a point with zero abscissa [R5.03.02](doc:r5.03.02).

In the case where sigm_f depends on the two parameters EPSI and TEMP (this concept was then defined by DEFI_NAPPE), one interpolates with respect to the temperature in order to find the traction curve at a given temperature $\theta$ . It is strongly recommended to refer to the document [R5.03.02](doc:r5.03.02) where the interpolation method is explained. One will note that, in order to avoid generating significant approximation errors or even obtaining poor traction curves by extrapolation, it is better not to use linear extension in DEFI_NAPPE.

**Note:**

*For multiphase materials, with metallurgical phases, the hardening characteristics are defined by* *META_ECRO_LINE or* *META_TRACTION* [R4.04.04](doc:r4.04.04).

### 5.2 Factor keywords ECRO_LINE, ECRO_LINE_FO

Definition of a linear hardening curve or of a set of curves depending on the temperature.

The functions may depend on the following command variables: 'TEMP', 'EPSI', 'HYDR', 'SECH'.

#### 5.2.1 Operands

Slope of the traction curve ${E}_{T}$ in the plastic regime.

Elastic limit ${s}_{y}$ .


| ![Figure](images/10000200000000CA0000008EE56805E4CF22A4A6.png) | The hardening curve used in the constitutive models is then:$R\left(p\right)={s}_{y}+H.p$ with $H=\frac{E.{E}_{T}}{E-{E}_{T}}$ One must therefore respect: ${E}_{T}<E$ (see for example [R5.03.02](doc:r5.03.02)). |
| --- | --- |


Young's modulus $E$ is to be specified by the keywords ELAS or ELAS_FO.

◊SIGM_LIM=sglim

Definition of the limit stress.

◊EPSI_LIM=eplim

Definition of the limit strain.

The operands sigm_LIM and espi_LIM make it possible to define the bounds in stress and in strain of the materials, which correspond to the serviceability and ultimate limit states, classically used in civil-engineering studies. These bounds are mandatory when one uses the constitutive behaviour VMIS_CINE_GC (see [U4.42.07](doc:u4.42.07) DEFI_MATER_GC). In the other cases they are not taken into account.

### 5.3 Factor keywords ECRO_NL

Definition of a non-linear hardening curve. This functionality is available for the constitutive laws 'VMIS_ISOT_NL', 'VISC_ISOT_NL', 'GTN' and 'VISC_GTN'.

#### 5.3.1 Operands

The coefficients introduced in the previous paragraph parameterise the hardening curve as follows:

$R\left(p\right)={R}_{0}+{R}_{H}p+{R}_{1}\left(1-{e}^{-{\gamma}_{1}p}\right)+{R}_{2}\left(1-{e}^{-{\gamma}_{2}p}\right)+{R}_{K}{\left(p+{p}_{0}\right)}^{{\gamma}_{m}}$

The terms parameterised by ${R}_{1}$ , ${R}_{2}$ and ${R}_{K}$ are optional. They are zero by default. This non-linear function is sufficiently general to make it possible to fit a good number of (increasing) experimental responses.

Moreover, it is possible to represent a Lüders plateau for the constitutive relations 'VMIS_ISOT_NL' and 'VISC_ISOT_NL' when $0\le p\le {\epsilon}_{L}^{p}$ . In this case, the hardening function actually taken into account is the following:

- $R({\epsilon}_{L}^{p})$     if    $p\le {\epsilon}_{L}^{p}$         (constant on the Lüders plateau)
- $R(p)$     if    $p\ge {\epsilon}_{L}^{p}$

One must note carefully that the initial elastic limit is then in this case $R({\epsilon}_{L}^{p})$ and no longer $R(0)$ .

### 5.4 Factor keywords PRAGER, PRAGER_FO

When the loading path is no longer monotonic, isotropic and kinematic hardenings are no longer equivalent. In particular, one may expect to have simultaneously a kinematic part and an isotropic part. If one seeks to describe precisely the effects of a cyclic loading, it is desirable to adopt sophisticated modellings (but simple to use) such as the Taheri model, for example, see [R5.03.05](doc:r5.03.05). On the other hand, for less complex loading paths, one may wish to include only a linear kinematic hardening, all the non-linearities of the hardening being carried by the isotropic term. This makes it possible to describe a traction curve precisely, while still representing phenomena such as the Bauschinger effect [R5.03.16](doc:r5.03.16).

The hardening characteristics are then given by a traction curve and a constant, called the Prager constant, for the linear kinematic hardening term. The keyword PRAGER makes it possible to define the PRAGER constant, used in the mixed-hardening models (linear kinematic combined with isotropic) VMIS_ECMI_LINE or VMIS_ECMI_TRAC.

The identification of $C$ is described in [R5.03.16](doc:r5.03.16).

### 5.5 Factor keywords ECRO_PUIS, ECRO_PUIS_FO

Parameters of plasticity with a Von Mises criterion and isotropic hardening following a power law.

#### 5.5.1 Operands

SY             =    sigy     Elastic limit

A_PUIS        =    a        Coefficient of the power law

N_PUIS        =    n         Exponent

The hardening curve is deduced from the uniaxial curve relating the strains to the stresses, whose expression is (cf. [R5.03.02](doc:r5.03.02)):

$\epsilon =\frac{\sigma}{E}+a\frac{{\sigma}_{y}}{E}{\left(\frac{(\sigma -{\sigma}_{y})}{{\sigma}_{y}}\right)}^{n}$

### 5.6 Factor keywords CIN1_CHAB, CIN1_CHAB_FO

Parameters of the Chaboche model (with a single kinematic variable) described in the document [R5.03.04](doc:r5.03.04).

Briefly, these relations are:


$$
F \left( \sigma, R, X \right) = \left( \tilde{\sigma} - X \right)_{eq} - R \left( p \right)

\dot{\varepsilon}^{p} = \dot{\lambda} \frac{\partial F}{\partial \sigma} = \dot{\lambda} \frac{3}{2} \frac{\tilde{\sigma} - X}{ \left( \tilde{\sigma} - X \right)_{eq} }

\dot{p} = \dot{\lambda} = \sqrt{\frac{2}{3} \dot{\varepsilon}^{p} : \dot{\varepsilon}^{p}}

\left\lbrace \begin{array}{cc}
    \dot{\lambda} = 0 & \text{si } F < 0 \text{ ou } \dot{F} < 0 \\
    \dot{\lambda} \ge 0 & \text{si } F = 0 \text{ et } \dot{F} = 0 \\
\end{array} \right.
$$

$$
X = \frac{2}{3} C \left( p \right) \alpha

\dot{\alpha} = \dot{\varepsilon}^{p} - \gamma \left( p \right) \alpha \dot{p}
$$



The functions $C(p)$, $\gamma (p)$ and $R(p)$ are defined by:


$\begin{array}{c}R\left(p\right)={R}_{\infty}+\left({R}_{0}-{R}_{\infty}\right){e}^{-\mathit{bp}}\hfill \\ C\left(p\right)={C}^{\infty}\left(1+\left(k-1\right){e}^{-\text{wp}}\right)\hfill \\ {\gamma}_{1}\left(p\right)={\gamma}^{0}\left({\alpha}_{\infty}+\left(1-{\alpha}_{\infty}\right){e}^{-\mathit{bp}}\right)\hfill \end{array}$


**Note**

$\tilde{\sigma}$ represents the stress deviator and ${()}_{\mathrm{eq}}$ the von Mises equivalent.

The definition of $X$ in the form :eq:`cin1-x-dotalpha` makes it possible to keep a formulation which takes into account the variations of the parameters with the temperature. These terms are necessary because failing to take them into account would lead to inaccurate results.

**Note**

A viscoplastic version of the Chaboche model is also available (see [R5.03.04](doc:r5.03.04)). It requires the viscous characteristics to be defined by means of the factor keyword LEMAITRE or LEMAITRE_FO, by setting the parameter UN_SUR_M to zero obligatorily.

### 5.7 Factor keywords CIN2_CHAB,CIN2_CHAB_FO

Parameters of the Chaboche model (with two kinematic variables) described in the document [R5.03.04](doc:r5.03.04).

Briefly these relations are:


$$
F \left( \sigma, R, X_{1}, X_{2} \right) = \left( \tilde{\sigma} - X_{1} - X_{2} \right)_{eq} - R \left( p \right)

\dot{\varepsilon}^{p} = \dot{\lambda} \frac{\partial F}{\partial \sigma} = \dot{\lambda} \frac{3}{2} \frac{\tilde{\sigma} - X_{1} - X_{2}}{ \left( \tilde{\sigma} - X_{1} - X_{2} \right)_{eq} }

\dot{p} = \dot{\lambda} = \sqrt{\frac{2}{3} \dot{\varepsilon}^{p} : \dot{\varepsilon}^{p}}

\left\lbrace \begin{array}{cc}
    \dot{\lambda} = 0 & \text{si } F < 0 \text{ ou } \dot{F} < 0 \\
    \dot{\lambda} \ge 0 & \text{si } F = 0 \text{ et } \dot{F} = 0 \\
\end{array} \right.
$$

$$
X_{1} = \frac{2}{3} C_{1} \left( p \right) \alpha_{1}

X_{2} = \frac{2}{3} C_{2} \left( p \right) \alpha_{2}

\dot{\alpha}_{1} = \dot{\varepsilon}^{p} - \gamma_{1} \left( p \right) \alpha_{1} \dot{p}

\dot{\alpha}_{2} = \dot{\varepsilon}^{p} - \gamma_{2} \left( p \right) \alpha_{2} \dot{p}
$$


The functions ${C}_{1}(p)$ , ${C}_{2}(p)$ , ${\gamma}_{1}(p)$ , ${\gamma}_{2}(p)$ and $R(p)$ are defined by:


$\begin{array}{c}R\left(p\right)={R}_{\infty}+\left({R}_{0}-{R}_{\infty}\right){e}^{-\mathit{bp}}\hfill \\ {C}_{1}\left(p\right)={C}_{1}^{\infty}\left(1+\left(k-1\right){e}^{-\text{wp}}\right)\hfill \\ {C}_{2}\left(p\right)={C}_{2}^{\infty}\left(1+\left(k-1\right){e}^{-\text{wp}}\right)\hfill \\ {\gamma}_{1}\left(p\right)={\gamma}_{1}^{0}\left({\alpha}_{\infty}+\left(1-{\alpha}_{\infty}\right){e}^{-\mathit{bp}}\right)\hfill \\ {\gamma}_{2}\left(p\right)={\gamma}_{2}^{0}\left({\alpha}_{\infty}+\left(1-{\alpha}_{\infty}\right){e}^{-\mathit{bp}}\right)\hfill \end{array}$


**Note:**

$\tilde{\sigma}$ *represents the stress deviator and* ${()}_{\mathrm{eq}}$ *the von Mises equivalent.*

*The definition of* ${X}_{1}$ *and* ${X}_{2}$ in the form :eq:`cin2-x-dotalpha` makes it possible to keep a formulation which takes into account the variations of the parameters with the temperature. These terms are necessary because failing to take them into account would lead to inaccurate results.

**Note**

A viscoplastic version of the Chaboche model with two kinematic variables is also available (cf. [R5.03.04](doc:r5.03.04)). It requires the viscous characteristics to be defined by means of the factor keyword LEMAITRE or LEMAITRE_FO, by setting the parameter UN_SUR_M to zero obligatorily.

### 5.8 Factor keywords VISCOCHAB, VISCOCHAB_FO

Definition of the coefficients of the elasto-viscoplastic model of Chaboche [R5.03.12](doc:r5.03.12).

Briefly, the constitutive equations of the model are:

Viscous stress ${\sigma}_{v}={J}_{2}(\tilde{\sigma}-X)-{\alpha}_{R}-k$

Viscoplastic strain rate


${\dot{\epsilon}}^{p}=\frac{3}{2}\dot{p}\frac{\stackrel{~}{\sigma}-X}{{J}_{2}(\stackrel{~}{\sigma}-X)}$

$\dot{p}=\langle \frac{{\sigma}_{v}}{{K}_{0}+{\alpha}_{k}R}\rangle \times exp\left[\alpha {\langle \frac{{\sigma}_{v}}{{K}_{0}+{\alpha}_{k}R}\rangle }^{n+1}\right]$

Isotropic hardening:

$\dot{R}=b(Q-R)\dot{p}+{\gamma}_{r}{({Q}_{\gamma}-R)}^{m}\mathrm{sgn}({Q}_{\gamma}-R)$

$Q={Q}_{0}+({Q}_{m}-{Q}_{0})\left(1-{e}^{-2\mu q}\right)$

$F({\epsilon}^{p},\xi ,q)=\frac{2}{3}{J}_{2}\left({\epsilon}^{p}-\xi \right)-q\le 0$

$\lbrace \begin{array}{c}\dot{q}=\eta \times H(F)\times ⟨n:n⟩\dot{p}\hfill \\ \dot{\xi}=\sqrt{3/2}(1-\eta )\times H(F)\times ⟨n:n⟩\dot{p}{n}^{\text{*}}\hfill \end{array}$ with: $n=\sqrt{\frac{3}{2}}\frac{\sigma -X}{{J}_{2}(\sigma -X)}$  ; ${n}^{\text{*}}=\sqrt{\frac{3}{2}}\frac{{\epsilon}^{p}-\xi }{{J}_{2}({\epsilon}^{p}-\xi )}$

${Q}_{r}=Q-{Q}_{r}^{\text{*}}\left(1-{\left(\frac{{Q}_{m}-Q}{{Q}_{m}}\right)}^{2}\right)$

Kinematic hardening $X={X}_{1}+{X}_{2}$

$\begin{array}{c}\dot{{X}_{i}}=\frac{2}{3}{C}_{i}{\dot{\epsilon}}^{p}-{\gamma}_{i}\left[{\delta}_{i}{X}_{i}+(1-{\delta}_{i})({X}_{i}:n)n\right]\dot{p}-{\gamma}_{{X}_{i}}{\left[{J}_{2}({X}_{i})\right]}^{{m}_{i}-1}{X}_{i}+\frac{1}{{C}_{i}}\frac{\partial {C}_{i}}{\partial T}{X}_{i}\dot{T}\\ {\gamma}_{i}={\gamma}_{i}^{0}[{a}_{\infty}+(1-{a}_{\infty}){e}^{-\mathit{bp}}]\hfill \end{array}$


**Notes:**

$\tilde{\sigma}$ *represents the stress deviator,* ${J}_{2}(Y)=\sqrt{3/2(Y:Y)}$ *the second invariant of the tensor* $Y$ *,*

$H(F)$ *the Heaviside function and* $\langle \mathrm{..}\rangle$ *the McCauley brackets (* $\langle x\rangle =x$ *if* $x\ge 0$ *,* $0$ *otherwise).*

*The variables* $q$ *and* $\xi$ *make it possible to take into account the memory effect of the hardening under cyclic loading. If* $\eta =1$ *, the memory effect is not modelled and the variables* $q$ *and* $\xi$ *are not considered in the solution of the system* $\text{(}q=0\text{)}$ *. Otherwise, one has the following condition on* $\eta$ *:* $0<\eta \le 1/2$ *.*

*From a thermodynamic point of view, the hardening variable* ${X}_{i}$ *is associated with its dual variable* ${\alpha}_{i}$ *for the relation* ${X}_{i}=\frac{2}{3}{C}_{i}{\alpha}_{i}$ *. The term in* $\dot{T}$ *appearing in the equation giving* $\dot{{X}_{i}}$ *makes it possible to treat the cases of anisothermal loadings for* ${C}_{i}$ *a function of the temperature.*

### 5.9 Factor keyword MEMO_ECRO

This keyword makes it possible to define the parameters associated with the maximum-hardening memory effect in the elastoplastic or elasto-viscoplastic constitutive behaviours of Chaboche (cf. [R5.03.04](doc:r5.03.04)). This keyword can be used, jointly with the keywords CIN1_CHAB or CIN2_CHAB, to define the parameters necessary for the constitutive behaviour VMIS_CIN2_MEMO. Moreover, by defining the viscosity parameters under LEMAITRE, it is possible to use a viscoplastic constitutive behaviour with maximum-hardening memory effect via VISC_CIN2_MEMO.

The equations of the model are written via a domain representing the maximum plastic strains reached:


$F\left({\epsilon}^{p},\xi ,q\right)=\frac{2}{3}{J}_{2}\left({\epsilon}^{p}-\xi \right)-q\le 0$ with the evolution law $\dot{\xi}=\frac{\left(1-\eta \right)}{\eta}\dot{q}{n}^{\ast }$

$q$ makes it possible to compute the evolution of the hardening law $R(p)$ by:

$\dot{R}=b\left(Q-R\right)\dot{p}$ , $Q={Q}_{0}+\left({Q}_{m}-{Q}_{0}\right)\left(1-{e}^{-2\mu q}\right)$

the plasticity criterion being written: $f\left(\sigma ,R,X\right)={\left(\stackrel{~}{\sigma}-{X}_{1}-{X}_{2}\right)}_{\mathit{eq}}-{R}_{0}-R\left(p\right)$


#### 5.9.1 Operands

Mu = mu

Coefficient of the exponential law

Q_M = Qm

Saturation value of the parameter $Q$ representing the isotropic hardening

Q_0 = Q0

Initial value of the parameter $Q$ representing the isotropic hardening

ETA = eta

Value making it possible to modify the taking into account of the memory of the maximum plastic strain: the value $eta=1/2$ corresponds to a total taking into account.

### 5.10 Factor keyword CIN2_NRAD

This keyword makes it possible to define the parameters associated with the non-proportionality effect of the Chaboche model (cf. [R5.03.04](doc:r5.03.04)).

#### 5.10.1 Operands

DELTA1, DELTA2: coefficients lying between 0 and 1 making it possible to take into account the possible non-proportionality of the loading. The default value of 1 cancels this effect.

### 5.11 Factor keywords TAHERI,TAHERI_FO

Definition of the coefficients of the cyclic elastoplasticity constitutive model of Saïd Taheri [R5.03.05](doc:r5.03.05). Briefly, we have to solve, for an elastoplastic increment:


$\lbrace \begin{array}{ccc}{\dot{\epsilon}}^{p}=\frac{3}{2}\dot{p}\frac{\stackrel{~}{\sigma}-X}{{\left(\stackrel{~}{\sigma}-X\right)}_{\mathit{eq}}}\hfill & \text{avec}& {\left(x\right)}_{\mathit{eq}}={\left(\frac{3}{2}{x}^{t}x\right)}^{1/2}\hfill \\ \sigma =\Lambda \left(\epsilon -{\epsilon}_{p}\right)\hfill & \phantom{\rule{2em}{0ex}}& R=D\left(A{\Vert \epsilon \Vert }^{\alpha}+{R}_{0}\right)\hfill \\ {\left(\sigma -X\right)}_{\mathit{eq}}-R=0\hfill & \phantom{\rule{2em}{0ex}}& X=C\left(S{\epsilon}_{p}-{\sigma}_{p}{\epsilon}_{p}^{n}\right)\hfill \\ \dot{{\sigma}_{p}}-\dot{R}-{\stackrel{.}{\stackrel{⏞}{\left(X\right)}}}_{\mathit{eq}}=0\hfill & \phantom{\rule{2em}{0ex}}& {\sigma}_{p}={\text{Max}}_{t}\left({X}_{\mathit{eq}}+R\right)\hfill \\ \dot{{\epsilon}_{p}^{n}}=0\hfill & \phantom{\rule{2em}{0ex}}& D=1-{\mathit{me}}^{-\mathit{bp}\left(1-\frac{{\sigma}_{p}}{S}\right)}\hfill \\ \phantom{\rule{2em}{0ex}}& \phantom{\rule{2em}{0ex}}& C={C}_{\infty}+{C}_{1}{e}^{-\mathit{bp}\left(1-\frac{{\sigma}_{p}}{S}\right)}\end{array}$


where the various material parameters are $S,{C}_{\infty},{C}_{1},b,m,A,\alpha ,{R}_{0}$ .

The various parameters may depend on the temperature; in this case one will use the keyword TAHERI_FO.

**Note:**

*A viscoplastic version of the model* *TAHERI* is also available (cf. [R5.03.05](doc:r5.03.05)). It requires the viscous characteristics to be defined by means of the factor keyword *LEMAITRE or* *LEMAITRE_FO.*

### 5.12 Factor keywords MONO_*

Definition of the coefficients of the single-crystal or polycrystal constitutive models [R5.03.11](doc:r5.03.11). In addition to these characteristics, the elastic constants must be defined under the keyword ELAS or ELAS_ORTH .

The constitutive behaviour associated with each slip system of a single crystal or of a phase of a polycrystal is (among the set of constitutive behaviours considered) of elasto-viscoplastic type.

The crystalline constitutive behaviours (other than those defined from dislocation dynamics) may be decomposed into 3 types of equations:

- flow relation: $\Delta {\gamma}_{s}=g({\tau}_{s},{\alpha}_{s},{\gamma}_{s},{p}_{s})$
- evolutions of the kinematic hardening: $\Delta {\alpha}_{s}=h\left({\tau}_{s},{\alpha}_{s},{\gamma}_{s},{p}_{s}\right)$
- evolution of the isotropic hardening: ${R}_{s}\left({p}_{s}\right)$ , with $\Delta {p}_{s}=\mid \Delta {\gamma}_{s}\mid$

The flow relation MONO_VISC1 is:


$\Delta {\gamma}_{s}=g\left({\tau}_{s},{\alpha}_{s},{\gamma}_{s},{p}_{s}\right)={\left(\frac{⟨|{\tau}_{s}-c{\alpha}_{s}|-R\left({p}_{s}\right)⟩}{K}\right)}^{n}.\text{}\frac{{\tau}_{s}-c{\alpha}_{s}}{|{\tau}_{s}-c{\alpha}_{s}|}$ , the parameters are: $c$ *,* $K$ *,* $n$

The flow relation MONO_VISC2 is:

$\Delta {\gamma}_{s}=g\left({\tau}_{s},{\alpha}_{s},{\gamma}_{s},{p}_{s}\right)={\left(\frac{⟨|{\tau}_{s}-c{\alpha}_{s}-a{\gamma}_{s}|-R\left({p}_{s}\right)+\frac{c}{2}d{\left(c{\alpha}_{s}\right)}^{2}⟩}{K}\right)}^{n}.\text{}\frac{{\tau}_{s}-c{\alpha}_{s}-a{\gamma}_{s}}{|{\tau}_{s}-c{\alpha}_{s}-a{\gamma}_{s}|}$

the parameters are then: $c$ *,* $K$ *,* $n$ *,* $a$ *,* $d$

The kinematic hardening may be of the form MONO_CINE1:

$\Delta {\alpha}_{s}=h({\tau}_{s},{\alpha}_{s},{\gamma}_{s},{p}_{s})=\Delta {\gamma}_{s}-d.{\alpha}_{s}.\Delta {p}_{s}$ with as parameter: $d$ *.*

or else MONO_CINE2:

$\Delta {\alpha}_{s}=h({\tau}_{s},{\alpha}_{s},{\gamma}_{s},{p}_{s})=\Delta {\gamma}_{s}-d.{\alpha}_{s}.\Delta {p}_{s}-{(\frac{\mid c{\alpha}_{s}\mid }{M})}^{m}\frac{{\alpha}_{s}}{\mid {\alpha}_{s}\mid }$ , the parameters then being: $d$ , $M$  and $m$ .

The isotropic hardening may for example be of the form MONO_ISOT1:

${R}_{s}\left({p}_{s}\right)={R}_{0}+Q\left(\sum_{r=1}^{N}{h}_{\mathit{sr}}\left(1-{e}^{-{\mathit{bp}}_{r}}\right)\right)$ with ${h}_{\mathit{sr}}$ interaction matrices, the parameters are $h$ , $Q$ , ${R}_{0}$ *,* $b$ *.*

Or again MONO_ISOT2:

${R}_{s}\left({p}_{s}\right)={R}_{0}+{Q}_{1}\left(\sum_{\mathit{sg}}^{\phantom{\rule{2em}{0ex}}}{h}_{\mathit{sr}}{q}^{\mathit{ls}}\right)+{Q}_{2}{q}^{2s}$ , with ${\mathrm{dq}}^{\mathrm{is}}={b}_{i}(1-{q}^{\mathrm{is}}).\mathrm{dp}$ the parameters are $h$ *,* ${Q}_{1}$ *,* ${Q}_{2}$ *,* ${b}_{1}$ *,* ${b}_{2}$ *,* ${R}_{0}$ .


The equations relating to the crystalline laws MONO_DD_KR, MONO_DD_CFC, MONO_DD_CFC_IRRA, MONO_DD_FAT, MONO_DD_CC, MONO_DD_CC_IRRA arising from dislocation dynamics are described in the document [R5.03.11](doc:r5.03.11).

#### 5.12.1 Syntax

These relations are accessible in 3D, plane strain (D_PLAN), plane stress (C_PLAN) (via the de Borst algorithm) and axisymmetric (AXIS) from the keyword COMPORTEMENT of the command STAT_NON_LINE. The choice of the relations making it possible to define the constitutive model of the single crystal is made via the operator DEFI_COMPOR [U4.43.06](doc:u4.43.06).


### 5.13 Factor keywords LEMAITRE,LEMAITRE_FO

Definition of the coefficients of the non-linear viscoplasticity relation of Lemaitre [R5.03.08](doc:r5.03.08).

The equations are the following:


$\lbrace \begin{array}{c}\dot{{\epsilon}_{ij}^{v}}=\frac{3}{2}\dot{p}\frac{\stackrel{~}{{\sigma}_{ij}}}{{\sigma}_{\mathit{eq}}}\hfill \\ \dot{p}={\left[\frac{1}{K}\frac{{\sigma}_{\mathit{eq}}}{{p}^{1/m}}\right]}^{n}\hfill \\ \sigma =\Lambda \left(\epsilon -{\epsilon}^{v}\right)\hfill \end{array}$


The coefficients to be introduced are: $n>0$ , $\frac{1}{K}$ and $\frac{1}{m}\ge 0.$ .

**Note:**

*By taking* $\frac{1}{m}=0$ *(that is* $m=+\infty$ *), that is to say by putting* $0$ *after the operand* *UN_SUR_M, one obtains a non-linear viscoelastic relation of Norton.*

### 5.14 Factor keywords NORTON,NORTON_FO

Definition of the coefficients of the non-linear viscoplasticity relation of Norton available with the constitutive model VISC_ECRO_NL [R5.03.33](doc:r5.03.33).

Denoting by $F(\sigma ,\kappa )$ the viscoplasticity threshold function, the evolution of the hardening variable $\kappa$ is governed by the following equation:


$\dot{\kappa}={\left(\frac{⟨F⟩}{K}\right)}^{n}$


The coefficients to be introduced are: $n>1$ , $K\ge 0$ .

### 5.15 Factor keyword VISC_SINH

Definition of the coefficients of the viscosity law defined by the following viscoplastic potential:


${\Phi}^{\mathrm{vp}}={\Phi}^{p}-{\sigma}_{0}{\sinh}^{-1}\left[{(\frac{\dot{p}}{\dot{{\varepsilon}_{0}}})}^{\frac{1}{m}}\right]$

The equation defining the cumulated plastic strain rate is therefore the following:

$\dot{p}=\dot{{\epsilon}_{0}}{\left[\sinh\left(\frac{⟨{\Phi}_{p}⟩}{{\sigma}_{0}}\right)\right]}^{m}$

an expression in which $⟨x⟩$ denotes the positive part of $x$ and ${\Phi}_{p}$ the plastic threshold.


This viscosity model may be associated:

- With the keyword ROUSSELIER in order to define the constitutive law ROUSS_VISC
- With the keywords VMIS_ISOT_TRAC and VMIS_ISOT_LINE, SIMO_MIEHE version: in order to define the constitutive laws VISC_ISOT_TRAC and VISC_ISOT_LINE.

The coefficients to be introduced are: $m,\dot{{\epsilon}_{0}}$ and ${\sigma}_{0}>0$ .

### 5.16 Keyword LEMA_SEUIL

Definition of the coefficients of the non-linear viscoplasticity relation of Lemaitre with threshold [R5.03.08](doc:r5.03.08). One places oneself in the small-perturbation assumption and one splits the strain tensor into an elastic part, a thermal part, an anelastic part (known) and a viscous part. The equations are then:


$\begin{array}{c}{\epsilon}_{\mathit{tot}}={\epsilon}_{e}+{\epsilon}_{\mathit{th}}+{\epsilon}_{a}+{\epsilon}_{v}\\ \sigma =\mathrm{A}\left(T\right){\epsilon}_{e}\\ \dot{{\epsilon}_{v}}=g\left({\sigma}_{\mathit{eq}},\lambda ,T\right)\frac{3}{2}\frac{\stackrel{~}{\sigma}}{{\sigma}_{\mathit{eq}}}\end{array}$

with:

$\lambda$ : cumulated viscous strain $\dot{\lambda}=\sqrt{\frac{2}{3}\dot{{\varepsilon}_{v}}:\dot{{\varepsilon}_{v}}}$

$\tilde{\sigma}$ : stress deviator $\stackrel{~}{\sigma}=\sigma -\frac{1}{3}\mathit{Tr}\left(\sigma \right)\mathrm{Id}$

${\sigma}_{\mathrm{eq}}$ : equivalent stress ${\sigma}_{\mathrm{eq}}=\sqrt{\frac{3}{2}\tilde{\sigma}:\tilde{\sigma}}$

$\mathrm{A}\left(T\right)$ : elasticity tensor

and:

if $D\le 1$ then $g(\sigma ,\lambda ,T)=0$ (purely elastic constitutive behaviour)

if $D>1$ then $g\left(\sigma ,\lambda ,T\right)=A\left(\frac{2}{\sqrt{3}}\sigma \right)\Phi$ with $A\ge 0,\Phi \ge 0$

With: $D=\frac{1}{S}\underset{0}{\overset{t}{\int}}{\sigma}_{\mathit{eq}}\left(u\right)\mathit{du}$

The material data to be entered by the user are $A$ and $S$.

As for the parameter $\Phi$, it is the neutron flux which bombards the material (quotient of the fluence increment, defined by the keyword AFFE_VARC of the operator AFFE_MATERIAU, by the time increment).


The Young's modulus $E$ and Poisson's ratio $\nu$ are those supplied under the factor keywords ELAS or ELAS_FO.

### 5.17 Factor keyword VISC_IRRA_LOG

Definition of the parameters of a creep law under irradiation of the guide tubes. This law is made up of a primary-type law and of a secondary law in the logarithm of the fluence (cf. [R5.03.08](doc:r5.03.08)).

The formulation is the following (in uniaxial conditions):


${\epsilon}_{f}=A.\exp\left(-\frac{Q}{T}\right).\sigma .\ln\left(1+\omega .\Phi .t\right)+B.\exp\left(-\frac{Q}{T}\right).\sigma .\Phi .t$

| ${\epsilon}_{f}$ | axial creep strain |
| --- | --- |
| $Q$ | activation energy |
| $T$ | temperature |
| $\sigma$ | axial stress applied to the guide tube in $\mathrm{MPa}$ |
| $\Phi .t={\Phi}_{t}$ | Fluence (${10}^{24}\mathit{neutrons}/{m}^{2}$ ) = product of the flux $\Phi$ by the time $t$ , given by the command variable IRRA in AFFE_MATERIAU |
| $t$ | time, expressed in hours |
| $\omega$ | Time constant, expressed such that $\omega .\Phi .t$ is dimensionless |
| $A$ | Constant, expressed so that $A.\sigma$ is homogeneous to a strain |
| $B$ $C$ | C Constant, expressed so that $B.\sigma .\Phi .t$ is homogeneous to a strain Constant, homogeneous to a strain |



Note: in the programming, the ratio $Q/T$ is in fact taken as $Q/(T+273,15)$ , which means that the temperature field (command variable) must be in $°C$ and $Q$ in $°K$ .

### 5.18 Factor keyword GRAN_IRRA_LOG

Definition of the parameters of a creep law under irradiation with growth of the guide tubes. With respect to VISC_IRRA_LOG, a growth term is added (cf. [R5.03.08](doc:r5.03.08)):


${\epsilon}_{f}=A.\exp\left(-\frac{Q}{T}\right).\sigma .\ln\left(1+\omega .\Phi .t\right)+B.\exp\left(-\frac{Q}{T}\right).\sigma .\Phi .t+C.\exp\left(-\omega .\Phi .t\right)$

${\epsilon}_{g}=f(T,{\Phi}_{t})$ where $f$ is a function of the temperature $T$ expressed in $°C$ and of the fluence ${\Phi}_{t}$ expressed in ${10}^{24}\mathit{neutrons}/{m}^{2}$ (given by the command variable IRRA in AFFE_MATERIAU).


### 5.19 Factor keywords IRRAD3M

Constitutive law of steels under irradiation (cf. [R5.03.23](doc:r5.03.23)).

The plastic law having to be described in the form ${K}^{\text{*}}{(p+{p}_{0})}^{n}$ , it is necessary to compute these parameters from $\mathrm{R02}$ , $\mathrm{RM}$ , ${\mathrm{EPSILON}}_{U}$ and $\mathrm{KAPPA}$ via a dichotomy method.

#### 5.19.1 Operands R02/RM/EPSI_U/KAPPA


```text
R02        = R02

EPSI_U    = eps i _u

RM        = RM

KAPPA    = KAPPA
```


Parameters appearing in the plastic part of the law. R02 is the elastic limit at $0.2\text{\%}$ of plastic strain, Rm is the ultimate stress, and epsi_u is the uniform elongation.


```text
TOLER_ET    = inc
```



This keyword corresponds to the error that one allows on the exceeding of the irradiation-creep threshold during the numerical integration. If during the analysis the criterion is not satisfied, Code_Aster subdivides the time steps, provided that the subdivision of the time steps is authorised; otherwise the code stops.

#### 5.19.2 Operands AI02/ZETA_F/ETAI_S


```text
AI0        = AIO,

ZETA_F    = y0

ETAI_S    = etai,

Paramètres liés à l’irradiation. y0 est une fonction de la température.
```


#### 5.19.3 Operands RG0/ALPHA/PHI0/ZETA_G


```text
ALPHA        = ALPHA,

PHI0        = PHI0,

RG0            = R,
```

ZETA_G        = z0

```text
Paramètres liés au gonflement.
```


### 5.20 Factor keywords ECRO_COOK, ECRO_COOK_FO

Parameters of a plasticity law with a Von Mises criterion and isotropic hardening following a Johnson–Cook relation.

### 5.21 Factor keywords KICHENIN_NL

One defines here the parameters of the elastoplastic / viscoelastic constitutive model 'KICHENIN_NL', in addition to those defined under the factor keyword ELAS.

#### 5.21.1 Operands

The coefficients introduced in the previous paragraph correspond to those described in [R5.03.36](doc:r5.03.36).

The first two, SIGC and PRAGER, refer to the elastoplastic branch of the model. They characterise respectively the elastic limit and the Prager constant which relates the back-stress to the plastic strain according to $X=C{\epsilon}^{p}$ . Young's modulus ${E}^{p}$ and Poisson's ratio ${\nu}^{p}$ specific to the elastoplastic branch are for their part defined under the factor keyword ELAS, via the keywords E and NU. The same applies to the thermal expansion coefficient ALPHA where appropriate.

The five other parameters of the factor keyword KICHENIN_NL characterise the viscoelastic branch, of Maxwell type and in which the damper is non-linear according to a Norton law. Thus E_VISC and NU_VISC characterise respectively Young's modulus and Poisson's ratio of the viscoelastic branch. Finally, NU_AMOR is the Poisson's ratio specific to the damper, ETA_AMOR its viscosity and N_AMOR the exponent of its non-linearity such that ${\sigma}^{v}=\eta {(\dot{{\epsilon}^{v}})}^{n}$ .

Note carefully that the elastic characteristics of the elastoplastic branch are defined under ELAS whereas the elastic characteristics of the viscoelastic branch are defined under KICHENIN_NL. And this even if the global stiffness of the constitutive model takes all of these values into account.

### 5.22 Factor keywords VISC_ISOT_PLAS, VISC_ISOT_PLAS_FO


```text
| VISC_ISOT_PLAS = _F(
      ◆ YoungModulus = float,
      ◆ PoissonRatio = float,
      ◆ TAILLE_GRAIN = float,
      ◆ D_DISLOC = float,
      ◆ C_AMAS = float,
      ◆ TAILLE_AMAS = float,
      ◇ UNITE_LONGUEUR = / "M" (par défaut),
                         / "MM",
      ◇ UNITE_CONTRAINTE = / "Pa" (par défaut),
                           / "MPa",
        ),
| VISC_ISOT_PLAS_FO = _F(
      ◆ YoungModulus = fonction / formule / nappe,
      ◆ PoissonRatio = fonction / formule / nappe,
      ◆ TAILLE_GRAIN = fonction / formule / nappe,
      ◆ D_DISLOC = fonction / formule / nappe,
      ◆ C_AMAS = fonction / formule / nappe,
      ◆ TAILLE_AMAS = fonction / formule / nappe,
      ◇ UNITE_LONGUEUR = / "M" (par défaut),
                         / "MM",
      ◇ UNITE_CONTRAINTE = / "Pa" (par défaut),
                           / "MPa",
        ),

◆ : obligatoire
◇ : optionnel
/ : un seul parmi
| : plusieurs choix possibles
```


The constitutive law VISC_ISOT_PLAS is an isotropic elasto-viscoplastic flow model described in [R5.03.38](doc:r5.03.38), which models the multi-scale effects of plasticity as well as those of irradiation. This modelling is mainly adopted to describe the mechanical constitutive behaviour of the steels of the vessels (bainitic) in the non-irradiated and irradiated states.

The parameters of the model comprise the elastic mechanical characteristics of the material, *i.e.* Young's modulus (YoungModulus) and Poisson's ratio (PoissonRatio), as well as the mechanical characteristics of the microstructure of the material, generally obtained experimentally: grain size (TAILLE_GRAIN) for the non-irradiated state, initial dislocation density (D_DISLOC), concentration (C_AMAS) and size (TAILLE_AMAS) of the solute clusters for the irradiated state.

These material parameters may be constant (VISC_ISOT_PLAS) or functions of the temperature (VISC_ISOT_PLAS_FO) or of any other command variable such as creep.

The unit of length considered is the metre by default. It is possible to choose the millimetre by means of the simple keyword UNITE_LONGUEUR. Likewise, the unit of stress considered is the pascal by default, possibly modifiable to the megapascal via the operand UNITE_CONTRAINTE.

#### 5.22.1 Operand YoungModulus

```text
| VISC_ISOT_PLAS = _F(
      ◆ YoungModulus = float,

| VISC_ISOT_PLAS_FO = _F(
      ◆ YoungModulus = fonction / formule / nappe,
```


This is the Young's modulus considered. The value entered must be strictly positive.

Moreover, it must also be entered via the operand E under the factor keyword ELAS, ELAS_FO. In the scalar case only, a check is carried out and a fatal error will be emitted if the values entered are different.

The default unit considered for Young's modulus is that prescribed via the operand UNITE_CONTRAINTE. By default, if nothing is specified, it is the pascal. It is also possible to choose the megapascal by means of the operand UNITE_CONTRAINTE. In all cases, a check is carried out and a fatal error will be emitted if the value entered is negative or zero, whatever the unit chosen.

#### 5.22.2 Operand PoissonRatio

```text
| VISC_ISOT_PLAS = _F(
      ◆ PoissonRatio = float,

| VISC_ISOT_PLAS_FO = _F(
      ◆ PoissonRatio = fonction / formule / nappe,
```


This is the Poisson's ratio considered. The value entered must be strictly greater than $-1,0$ and strictly less than $0,5$. In all cases, a check is carried out and a fatal error will be emitted if the value entered does not lie in the interval $\left] -1,0; \, 0,5 \right[$.

Moreover, it must also be entered via the operand NU under the factor keyword ELAS, ELAS_FO. In the scalar case only, a check is carried out and a fatal error will be emitted if the values entered are different.

One recalls that Poisson's ratio has no unit.

#### 5.22.3 Operand TAILLE_GRAIN

```text
| VISC_ISOT_PLAS = _F(
      ◆ TAILLE_GRAIN = float,

| VISC_ISOT_PLAS_FO = _F(
      ◆ TAILLE_GRAIN = fonction / formule / nappe,
```


This is the mean size of the grains (lath packets) of the microstructure considered for the non-irradiated state. The value entered must be strictly positive.

The default unit considered for the grain size is that prescribed via the operand UNITE_LONGUEUR. By default, if nothing is specified, it is the metre (denoted $m$ hereafter). It is also possible to choose the millimetre (denoted $mm$ hereafter) by means of the operand UNITE_LONGUEUR.

For the vessel steels, the value entered is generally between $10^{-7} \, m$ and $10^{-3} \, m$ (or equivalently between $10^{-4} \, mm$ and $1 \, mm$). In all cases, a check is carried out and a fatal error will be emitted if the value entered is negative or zero, whatever the unit chosen.

#### 5.22.4 Operand D_DISLOC

```text
| VISC_ISOT_PLAS = _F(
      ◆ D_DISLOC = float,

| VISC_ISOT_PLAS_FO = _F(
      ◆ D_DISLOC = fonction / formule / nappe,
```


This is the initial dislocation density of the microstructure considered. The value entered must be positive or zero.

It corresponds to a length of dislocation line per unit volume. It is expressed by default in $m^{-2}$ but possibly in $mm^{-2}$ if the unit of length set by UNITE_LONGUEUR is the millimetre.

For the vessel steels, the value entered is generally between $10^{12} \, m^{-2}$ and $10^{20} \, m^{-2}$ (or equivalently between $10^{6} \, mm^{-2}$ and $10^{14} \, mm^{-2}$). In all cases, a check is carried out and a fatal error will be emitted if the value entered is strictly negative, whatever the unit chosen.

#### 5.22.5 Operand C_AMAS

```text
| VISC_ISOT_PLAS = _F(
      ◆ C_AMAS = float,

| VISC_ISOT_PLAS_FO = _F(
      ◆ C_AMAS = fonction / formule / nappe,
```


This is the concentration of the solute clusters for the irradiated state of the microstructure considered. The value entered must be positive or zero.

It corresponds to a number of clusters per unit volume. It is expressed by default in $m^{-3}$ but possibly in $mm^{-3}$ if the unit of length set by UNITE_LONGUEUR is the millimetre.

For the vessel steels, the value entered is generally less than $10^{25} \, m^{-3}$ (or equivalently less than $10^{16} \, mm^{-3}$). In all cases, a check is carried out and a fatal error will be emitted if the value entered is strictly negative, whatever the unit chosen.

#### 5.22.6 Operand TAILLE_AMAS


```text
| VISC_ISOT_PLAS = _F(
      ◆ TAILLE_AMAS = float,

| VISC_ISOT_PLAS_FO = _F(
      ◆ TAILLE_AMAS = fonction / formule / nappe,
```


This is the size of the solute clusters for the irradiated state of the microstructure considered. The value entered must be positive or zero.

The default unit considered for the size of the solute clusters is that prescribed via the operand UNITE_LONGUEUR. By default, if nothing is specified, it is the metre. It is also possible to select the millimetre via the operand UNITE_LONGUEUR.

For the vessel steels, the value entered is generally between $10^{-9} \, m$ and $10^{-8} \, m$ (or equivalently between $10^{-6} \, mm$ and $10^{-5} \, mm$). In all cases, a check is carried out and a fatal error will be emitted if the value entered is strictly negative, whatever the unit chosen.

#### 5.22.7 Operand UNITE_LONGUEUR (optional)

```text
◇ UNITE_LONGUEUR = / "M" (par défaut),
                   / "MM",
```


This is to specify the unit of length chosen to express the mechanical characteristics of the microstructure of the material considered. The expected value is a character string which may be 'M' to designate the metre or 'MM' to designate the millimetre (*i.e.* one thousandth of a metre). Any other textual value entered will lead to a fatal error.

This operand is optional and its default value is 'M', meaning that the metre is the unit of length used by default.

#### 5.22.8 Operand UNITE_CONTRAINTE (optional)

```text
◇ UNITE_CONTRAINTE = / "Pa" (par défaut),
                     / "MPa",
```


This is to specify the unit of stress chosen to express the elastic mechanical characteristics of the material considered. The expected value is a character string which may be 'Pa' to designate the pascal or 'MPa' to designate the megapascal (*i.e.* one million pascals). Any other textual value entered will lead to a fatal error.

This operand is optional and its default value is 'Pa', meaning that the pascal is the unit of stress used by default.

## 6 Constitutive behaviours related to damage and fracture

### 6.1 Factor keywords ROUSSELIER,ROUSSELIER_FO

Definition of the coefficients of the ductile-fracture constitutive model of Rousselier (cf. [R5.03.06] and [R5.03.07](doc:r5.03.07)). This model may be used in small strains, in large strains and in viscoplasticity (keyword VISC_SINH)

Briefly, one solves for an elastoplastic increment:


$\lbrace \begin{array}{cc}\frac{{\sigma}_{\mathit{eq}}}{\rho}-R\left(p\right)+D{\sigma}_{1}f\exp\left(\frac{{\sigma}_{H}}{{\sigma}_{1}}\rho \right)=0& \text{éq 5.1-1}\\ \sigma =\rho \Lambda \left(\epsilon -{\epsilon}^{p}\right)\hfill & \phantom{\rule{2em}{0ex}}\\ \dot{{\epsilon}_{p}}=\dot{p}\rho \frac{\partial f}{\partial \sigma }\hfill & \phantom{\rule{2em}{0ex}}\\ \dot{f}=3\left(1-f\right){\epsilon}_{H}^{p}\hfill & \phantom{\rule{2em}{0ex}}\end{array}$


with $\lbrace \begin{array}{cc}\frac{\partial f}{\partial \sigma }=\frac{1}{\rho}\left(\frac{3}{2}\frac{\stackrel{~}{\sigma}}{{\sigma}_{\mathit{eq}}}+\frac{\mathit{Df}}{3}\exp\left(\frac{{\sigma}_{H}}{{\sigma}_{1}\rho }\right)\right)& \phantom{\rule{2em}{0ex}}\\ \rho =\frac{1-f}{1-{f}_{0}}\hfill & \text{éq 5.1-2}\end{array}$


$R(p)$ entered by means of the traction curve (keyword TRACTION).

With the material coefficients $D,{\sigma}_{1,}{f}_{0}$ specific to the ROUSSELIER model.

These various parameters may depend on the temperature; in this case one will use the keyword ROUSSELIER_FO.

It is possible to complete the model by bringing in the following quantities:

- the critical porosity ${f}_{c}$ beyond which the growth of the cavities is accelerated:


$\dot{f}=3A(1-f){\epsilon}_{H}^{p}\text{}\mathit{si}f>{f}_{c}$


two additional characteristics are then necessary: ${f}_{c}$ and $A$ .

- the limit porosity ${f}_{l}$ beyond which the material is considered broken. The constitutive behaviour is then replaced by an imposed drop of the stresses:


$\dot{\sigma}=-\lambda E\frac{\sigma}{\mid \sigma \mid }\mid \dot{\varepsilon}\mid \text{}\mathrm{si}f={f}_{l}$ (with $E$ defined under ELAS).


two additional characteristics are then necessary: ${f}_{1}$ and $\lambda$ .

- the volumetric nucleation rate of cleavage cracks ${A}_{n}$ , modifying as follows equations [éq 5.1-1] and [éq 5.1-2].


$\lbrace  \begin{array}{c}\frac{{\sigma}_{\mathit{eq}}}{\rho}-R(p)+D{\sigma}_{1}(f+{A}_{n}p)\exp(\frac{{\sigma}_{H}}{{\sigma}_{1}\rho })=0\\ \rho =\frac{1-f-{A}_{n}p}{1-{f}_{0}}\end{array}$
