These last five parameters are independent of the temperature.

The following correspondence table must be used:

| **Modelling** | **Keywords** |
| --- | --- |
| $D$ | D |
| ${\sigma}_{1}$ | SIGM_1 |
| ${f}_{0}$ | PORO_INIT |
| ${f}_{c}$ | PORO_CRIT dp |
| $A$ | PORO_ACCE |
| ${A}_{n}$ | AN |
| ${f}_{l}$ | PORO_LIMI |
| $\lambda$ | D_SIGM_EPSI_NORM |

In the SIMO_MIEHE version the constitutive law requests a sub-stepping when the plastic-strain increment is greater than the value $\mathit{dp}$ supplied after the keyword DP_MAXI.

The keyword BETA is to be entered with the constitutive behaviours ROUSS_PR or ROUSS_VISC in order to take adiabatic heating into account: it sets the proportion of plastic energy which is actually transformed into heat.

The choice is given to the user, by means of the keyword PORO_TYPE, of changing the formulation of the porosity as a function of the plastic strain or of the total strain. It has been observed that for a low initial porosity ${f}_{0}$ , the behaviour at the beginning of the evolution changes strongly as a function of this parameter. Thus PORO_TYPE is assigned 1 (porosity in plastic strain), 2 (porosity in total strain).

#### 6.1.1 Help with use

The Rousselier model has been the subject of numerous developments and has several variants named ROUSSELIER, ROUSS_PR and ROUSS_VISC (these models are available in the command STAT_NON_LINE by means of the keyword RELATION). Thus each of these models requires a particular knowledge from the "user" point of view. In order to clarify this, a summary table is set out below, accompanied by various notes.

|  |  | ROUSSELIER [R5.03.06](doc:r5.03.06) | ROUSS_PR [R5.03.07](doc:r5.03.07) | ROUSS_VISC [R5.03.07](doc:r5.03.07) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| COMPORTEMENT | SIMO_MIEHE | x |  |  |
| GDEF_LOG | x |  |  |  |
| PETIT_REAC |  | x | x |  |
| MODELISATION | 3D | x | x | x |
| Axisymmetric | x | x | x |  |
| CP |  | x | x |  |
| DP | x | x | x |  |
| INCOMPRESSIBLE (xxx_inco_ugp) | x |  |  |  |

**Notes:**

When one uses the incompressible elements (refer to the doc [R3.06.08](doc:r3.06.08)), the keyword C_GONF must be entered in DEFI_MATERIAU under the operand NON_LOCAL (refer to §5.6 and to the test case ssnp122a).

For a three-field UPG formulation, it is preferable to use the MUMPS solver to solve the linear systems. Moreover, the user must be warned that it is advisable to consider, as convergence criterion, a convergence criterion by reference stress under the keyword RESI_REFE_RELA.

### 6.2 Factor keyword GTN

This keyword makes it possible to define the coefficients of the GTN model (constitutive relations GTN and VISC_GTN) for the part related to the evolution of the porosity, cf. [R5.03.29](doc:r5.03.29). It covers the three aspects of ductile fracture, namely the phases of nucleation, growth and coalescence of cavities.

#### 6.2.1 Operands

The cavity-nucleation phase (*nucleation* in English, hence the prefix NUCL in the keywords) is modelled by a contribution to the porosity, denoted ${f}^{\mathit{nuc}}$ , the other contribution being related to the growth of the cavities ${f}^{\mathit{growth}}$ :


> $\dot{f}={\dot{f}}^{\mathit{growth}}+{\dot{f}}^{\mathit{nuc}};{\dot{f}}^{\mathit{growth}}=\left(1-f\right)\mathrm{tr}{\dot{\mathrm{E}}}^{\mathrm{p}}\text{avec}f(0)={f}_{0}$


The evolution of the nucleation porosity is a function of the hardening variable $\kappa$ and of the cumulated plastic strain according to the following incremental model:


${\dot{f}}^{\mathit{nuc}}={B}_{n}\left(\kappa \right)\dot{\kappa}+{b}_{0}{\dot{E}}_{\mathit{eq}}^{p}H({E}_{\mathit{eq}}^{p}-{E}_{c}^{p});{B}_{n}(\kappa )=\frac{{f}_{N}}{{s}_{N}\sqrt{2\pi }}\exp\left[-\frac{1}{2}{\left(\frac{\kappa -{\kappa}_{N}}{{s}_{N}}\right)}^{2}\right]+\frac{{c}_{0}}{{\kappa}_{f}-{\kappa}_{i}}{\mathrm{\chi }}_{[{\kappa}_{i};{\kappa}_{f}]}$


where $H$ denotes the Heaviside function (zero for a strictly negative argument, equal to 1 for a positive argument) and ${\chi }_{I}$ the characteristic function of a set $I$ (equal to 1 in $I$ , zero elsewhere).

In the absence of a declaration of the nucleation parameters, the function $B$ and ${b}_{0}$ are zero, so that there is then no nucleation.

Cavity growth is described by the evolution of the plasticity, governed by a threshold function which relies on an equivalent stress ${T}_{\text{*}}$ . The latter is defined implicitly by the following equation, in which $\mathrm{T}$ denotes the stress tensor specific to the GDEF_LOG kinematics:


${\left(\frac{{T}_{\mathit{eq}}}{{T}_{\text{*}}}\right)}^{2}+2{q}_{1}{f}_{\text{*}}\cosh\left({q}_{2}\frac{3}{2}\frac{{T}_{H}}{{T}_{\text{*}}}\right)-1-{q}_{1}{f}_{\text{*}}=0$


where ${T}_{\mathit{eq}}$ denotes the von Mises equivalent stress and ${T}_{H}$ one third of the trace of $T$ .

Coalescence is expressed (in loading) by an acceleration of the evolution of the effective porosity ${f}_{\text{*}}$ according to the following relation:


$\lbrace \begin{array}{cc}\mathrm{si}\phantom{\rule{2em}{0ex}}f\le {f}_{c}& {f}_{\text{*}}=f\hfill \\ \mathrm{si}\phantom{\rule{2em}{0ex}}f>{f}_{c}& {f}_{\text{*}}={f}_{c}+{\delta}_{c}(f-{f}_{c})\end{array}$


Here again, there will be no coalescence mechanism (${f}_{\text{*}}=f$ ) if the corresponding parameters are not entered. The slope ${\delta}_{c}$ may be supplied directly or recomputed if one prefers to enter the fracture porosity ${f}_{r}$ such that ${q}_{1}{f}_{\text{*}}({f}_{r})=1$ .

Finally, two additional parameters activate regularisation techniques of the model in order to reinforce its robustness: ENDO_CRIT_VISC and ENDO_CRIT_RUPT. Denoting by "damage" the value ${q}_{1}{f}_{\text{*}}$ , one introduces for the model VISC_GTN the viscosity-threshold damage ${d}_{v}$ beyond which one limits the loss of viscosity which results from the damage, which makes it possible to maintain a certain degree of viscous regularisation in order to absorb stress shocks. Moreover, in order to avoid seeking to solve the equations of the model for a damage too close to 1 (they degenerate for $d=1$ ), one may force prematurely the fracture of the point considered beyond a damage ${d}_{r}$ . By default, it is computed as a function of the numerical precision requested by the user (RESI_INTE_RELA) but this may prove too constraining and lead to an excessive cutting of the time steps.

### 6.3 Keywords VENDOCHAB/ VENDOCHAB_FO

Definition of the coefficients of the viscoplastic model with scalar damage of Chaboche, see [R5.03.15](doc:r5.03.15)). It is a constitutive behaviour with multiplicative hardening-viscosity coupled to isotropic damage. Briefly, the relations are:


$\lbrace \begin{array}{c}\sigma =\left(1-D\right)A{\epsilon}^{e}\text{et}{\epsilon}^{e}=\epsilon -{\epsilon}^{\mathit{th}}-{\epsilon}^{p}\\ \dot{{\epsilon}^{p}}=\frac{3}{2}\dot{p}\frac{\stackrel{~}{\sigma}}{{\sigma}_{\mathit{eq}}}\text{avec}\dot{p}=\frac{\dot{r}}{\left(1-D\right)}\hfill \\ \dot{r}={\left(\frac{{\sigma}_{\mathit{eq}}-S\left(1-D\right)}{\left(1-D\right){\mathit{Kr}}^{1/M}}\right)}^{N}\hfill \\ \dot{D}={\left(\frac{\chi \left(\sigma \right)}{A}\right)}^{R}{\left(1-D\right)}^{-k\left(\chi \left(\sigma \right)\right)}\hfill \end{array}$


with $D$ , the scalar variable of isotropic damage and:


$\chi \left(\sigma \right)=\alpha {J}_{0}\left(\sigma \right)+\beta {J}_{1}\left(\sigma \right)+\left(1-\alpha -\beta \right){J}_{2}\left(\sigma \right)$


where:

| ${J}_{0}\left(\sigma \right)$ is the maximum principal stress |
| --- |
| ${J}_{1}\left(\sigma \right)=\mathit{Tr}\left(\sigma \right)$ |
| ${J}_{2}\left(\sigma \right)={\sigma}_{\mathit{eq}}$ |

$\langle x\rangle$ : positive part of $x$ , $\stackrel{~}{\sigma}$ stress deviator and ${\sigma}_{\mathrm{eq}}$ the Von Mises stress.

The table below summarises the correspondences between the symbols of the equations and the keywords of *Aster*.

| **Material parameter** | **Symbol in the equations** | **Keyword in** **Aster** |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Viscoplasticity threshold | $S$ | 'SY' |
| Coefficient 1 of the equivalent creep stress | $\alpha$ | 'ALPHA_D' |
| Coefficient 2 of the equivalent creep stress | $\beta$ | 'BETA_D' |
| Coefficient of the damage law | $A$ | 'A_D' |
| First exponent of the damage law | $R$ | 'R_D' |
| Second exponent of the damage law | $k\left[\chi \left[\sigma \right]\right]$ | 'K_D' |

**Note**

The parameter K_D may be defined as a constant, a function of a parameter 'TEMP' or a nappe (temperature variable and stress $\chi \left(\sigma \right)$). In this case, use DEFI_NAPPE with as first parameter 'TEMP' for the temperature in $°C$ and as second parameter 'X' (**mandatory**) for the stresses in $\mathit{MPa}$. If K_D depends only on $\chi \left(\sigma \right)$, one must use DEFI_NAPPE anyway by introducing for example twice the same set of stress data for two different values of the temperature.

### 6.4 Keywords VISC_ENDO/ VISC_ENDO_FO

Definition of the coefficients of the viscoplastic model of Lemaître with scalar damage VISC_ENDO_LEMA cf. [R5.03.15](doc:r5.03.15)), which corresponds to a simplified and optimised version of the model VENDOCHAB (cf. [U4.51.11](doc:u4.51.11)).


$\lbrace \begin{array}{c}\sigma =\left(1-D\right)A{\epsilon}^{e}\text{et}{\epsilon}^{e}=\epsilon -{\epsilon}^{\mathit{th}}-{\epsilon}^{p}\\ \dot{{\epsilon}^{p}}=\frac{3}{2}\dot{p}\frac{\stackrel{~}{\sigma}}{{\sigma}_{\mathit{eq}}}\text{avec}\dot{p}=\frac{\dot{r}}{\left(1-D\right)}\hfill \\ \dot{r}={\left(\frac{\frac{{\sigma}_{\mathit{eq}}}{(1-D)}-{\sigma}_{y}}{{\mathit{Kr}}^{1/M}}\right)}^{N}\text{}\dot{D}={\left(\frac{{\sigma}_{\mathit{eq}}}{A\left(1-D\right)}\right)}^{R}\hfill \end{array}$


The table below summarises the correspondences between the symbols of the equations and the keywords.

| Material parameter | Symbol in the equations | Keyword |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Viscoplasticity threshold | ${\sigma}_{y}$ | 'SY' |
| Coefficient of the damage law | $A$ | 'A_D' |
| First exponent of the damage law | $R$ | 'R_D' |

### 6.5 Keywords HAYHURST and HAYHURST_FO

Definition of the coefficients of the viscoplastic model of Hayhurst, in order to describe the elasto-viscoplastic constitutive behaviour of austenitic steels, with a scalar damage in hyperbolic sine, a function of the maximum principal stress, an isotropic hardening and a viscous law in hyperbolic sine:


$\lbrace \begin{array}{c}\sigma =\left(1-D\right)C{\epsilon}^{e}\text{et}{\epsilon}^{e}=\epsilon -{\epsilon}^{\mathit{th}}-{\epsilon}^{p}\hfill \\ \dot{{\epsilon}^{p}}=\frac{3}{2}\dot{p}\frac{\stackrel{~}{\sigma}}{{\sigma}_{\mathit{eq}}}\text{avec}\dot{p}=\dot{{\epsilon}_{0}}\sinh\left(\frac{{\sigma}_{\mathit{eq}}(1-H)}{K\left(1-D\right)(1-\varphi )}\right)\phantom{\rule{4em}{0ex}}\text{avec}\phantom{\rule{4em}{0ex}}\dot{\varphi}=\frac{{k}_{c}}{3}{(1-\varphi )}^{4}\hfill \\ \text{si S\_EQUI\_D}=0\phantom{\rule{4em}{0ex}}\dot{D}=\dot{{A}_{0}}\sinh\left(\frac{\alpha \text{<}{\sigma}_{I}{\text{>}}_{+}+{\sigma}_{\mathit{eq}}(1-\alpha )}{{\sigma}_{0}}\right)\hfill \\ \text{si S\_EQUI\_D}=1\phantom{\rule{4em}{0ex}}\dot{D}=\dot{{A}_{0}}\sinh\left(\frac{\alpha \text{<}\mathit{tr}(\sigma ){\text{>}}_{+}+{\sigma}_{\mathit{eq}}(1-\alpha )}{{\sigma}_{0}}\right)\hfill \\ H={H}_{1}+{H}_{2}\hfill \\ \dot{{H}_{i}}=\frac{{h}_{i}}{{\sigma}_{\mathit{eq}}}\left({H}_{i}^{\text{*}}-{\delta}_{i}{H}_{i}\right)\dot{p}\phantom{\rule{4em}{0ex}}i=1,2\hfill \end{array}$


### 6.6 Factor keyword RUPT_FRAG, RUPT_FRAG_FO

The fracture theory of Francfort and Marigo makes it possible to model the appearance and the propagation of cracks in brittle fracture. It relies on the Griffith criterion which compares the release of elastic energy and the energy dissipated during the creation of a cracked surface, supplied by the keyword GC. These are the notions which are used to describe fracture in the cohesive-zone models, subject to the definition of other notions specific to certain laws. RUPT_FRAG is the keyword used to define the material parameters of the cohesive constitutive laws, CZM_xxx (with the exception of CZM_TRA_MIX and CZM_LAB_MIX) (see [R7.02.11](doc:r7.02.11)).

#### 6.6.1 Operand G_C

The dissipated energy is proportional to the crack surface created, the coefficient of proportionality being the critical energy density of the material ${G}_{c}$ .

#### 6.6.2 Operand SIGM_C

Critical stress at the origin from which the crack will open and the stress between the lips will decrease.

#### 6.6.3 Operand PENA_ADHERENCE

Small regularisation parameter of the stress at zero (for further details see [R7.02.11](doc:r7.02.11)).

**Note**

The parameters SIGM_C and PENA_ADHERENCE are mandatory only in the case of the modellings xxx_JOINT. They are not used for the Griffith criterion, which is why they appear as optional at catalogue level.

#### 6.6.4 Operand PENA_CONTACT

Small regularisation parameter of the contact.

#### 6.6.5 Operands PENA_LAGR and RIGI_GLIS

Penalisation parameter of the Lagrangian ($\mathit{pla}\ge 1.01$ ) and stiffness in slip mode.

Operand CINEMATIQUE

Determines the opening modes authorised by the interface law for the law CZM_TAC_MIX. 'UNILATER' means that the two volumes on either side of the interface cannot interpenetrate, 'GLIS_2D' that the two volumes can only slide in the plane tangent to the interface, and 'GLIS_1D' that they can slide only in a single direction.

The tangent frame considered is defined via the factor keyword MASSIF of AFFE_CARA_ELEM [U4.42.01](doc:u4.42.01). In the case of a one-dimensional slip, the only possible slip direction is defined by the second vector of the rotated frame ($\mathit{Oy}$ ).

### 6.7 Factor keyword NON_LOCAL

This factor keyword makes it possible to enter the characteristics necessary for the use of non-local constitutive models for which the response of the material is no longer defined at the scale of the material point but at that of the structure; see also AFFE_MODELE [U4.41.01](doc:u4.41.01) and [R5.04](doc:r5.04).

#### 6.7.1 Operands LONG_CARA/C_GRAD_VARI/COEF_RIGI_MINI/C_GONF/PENA_LAGR/TAU_EPSI


```text
LONG_CARA = long
```


Determines the characteristic length or internal length scale of the material. Not to be used with the non-local damage laws with damage gradient GRAD_VARI.


```text
C_GRAD_VARI = long
```


Non-locality parameter for the internal-variable gradient formulation, present in the free energy in the form $c/2{\left(\nabla a\right)}^{2}$ . It determines the characteristic length of the damage zone. To be used exclusively with the non-local damage laws with damage gradient GRAD_VARI.


```text
COEF_RIGI_MINI = coef
```


For its part it has an algorithmic role since it sets, for the damage models which degrade the stiffness of the material, the proportion of the initial stiffness (Young's modulus) defined under ELAS (0.1% for example) below which one stops the damage mechanism: this residual stiffness makes it possible to preserve the well-posed character of the elastic problem.

C_GONF = gonf

In the Rousselier model, the softening character is carried by the porosity which has a purely hydrostatic effect. In order to control the localisation, the idea is to regularise the problem only on this part and therefore to regularise the swelling variable if one uses the INCO_UPG modelling.


```text
PENA_LAGR = pena
```


Penalisation parameter used for the modellings with internal-variable gradients ( _GRAD_VARI ) and second gradient ( _DIL ), which makes it possible to control the coincidence between a nodal field (degrees of freedom specific to the non-local formulation) and a field at the Gauss points (internal variable or strain).

A default value of 1000 is implemented. For the modelling _DIL it is inadvisable to decrease this value (loss of precision for the solution). For the modelling GRAD_VARI this parameter corresponds to the multiplier $r$ of the quadratic penalisation term in the free energy: $r/2{\left(\alpha -a\right)}^{2}$ . It is up to the user to adjust its value as a function of the law used.


```text
PENA_LAGR_INCO = pena_inco
```


Penalisation parameter used for the modellings with internal-variable gradients in large strains ( _GRAD_INCO), which makes it possible to reinforce the kinematic relation between the swelling degree of freedom and the volume change seen from the strain. Numerical tests in the case of a ductile-fracture problem lead to recommending a value of the order of ten times the initial elastic limit. Too low a value (or zero) may lead to the appearance of plastic localisation bands. Too high a value leads to numerical locking in incompressibility which this enriched kinematics precisely seeks to limit.


```text
TAU_EPSI = tau_epsi
```


Stabilisation parameter of the stabilised mixed modellings (MIX_STA). Its value lies between 0 and 1.
For a value of 1, one recovers a standard linear finite element. For a value of 0, one recovers a non-stabilised mixed formulation.
In practice, it is recommended to use $\tau_{\varepsilon} = \frac{h}{L}$ where L represents the characteristic length of the problem, and h the size of the mesh cells.

### 6.8 Factor keyword CZM_LAB_MIX

This factor keyword makes it possible to specify the parameters of the steel–concrete interface law CZM_LAB_MIX (see [R7.02.11](doc:r7.02.11)).

#### 6.8.1 Operand SIGM_C

Maximum stress that can be supported by the steel–concrete interface.

#### 6.8.2 Operand GLIS_C

Slip for which the stress at the interface is maximum.

#### 6.8.3 Operands ALPHA and BETA

Shape parameters of the steel–concrete bond law. alpha typically varies between 0 and 1, whereas beta is positive.

#### 6.8.4 Operands PENA_LAGR

Penalisation parameter of the Lagrangian ($\mathit{pla}\ge 1.01$ ).

#### 6.8.5 Operand CINEMATIQUE

Determines the slip modes authorised by the interface law. 'UNILATER' means that the two volumes on either side of the interface cannot interpenetrate, 'GLIS_2D' that the two volumes can only slide in the plane tangent to the interface, and 'GLIS_1D' that they can slide only in a single direction.

The tangent frame considered is defined via the factor keyword MASSIF of AFFE_CARA_ELEM [U4.42.01](doc:u4.42.01). In the case of a one-dimensional slip, the only possible slip direction is defined by the second vector of the rotated frame ($\mathit{Oy}$ ).

### 6.9 Factor keyword RUPT_DUCT

These material characteristics are intended to define the constitutive behaviour of a ductile cohesive crack with the constitutive law CZM_TRA_MIX, see [R7.02.11](doc:r7.02.11).

#### 6.9.1 Operand G_C

The dissipated energy is proportional to the crack surface created, the coefficient of proportionality being the critical energy density of the material ${G}_{c}$ .

#### 6.9.2 Operand SIGM_C

Critical stress at the origin from which the crack will open.

#### 6.9.3 Operands COEF_EXTR and COEF_PLAS

Shape parameters of the cohesive law CZM_TRA_MIX, see [R7.02.11](doc:r7.02.11).

#### 6.9.4 Operands PENA_LAGR and RIGI_GLIS

Penalisation parameter of the Lagrangian ($\mathit{pla}\ge .01$ ) and stiffness in slip mode.

### 6.10 Factor keyword RANKINE

The simplified modelling of the joints of concrete dams relies on this material [R7.01.39](doc:r7.01.39). It is a perfect-plasticity criterion in tension bearing on the components of the principal stresses: ${\sigma}_{i=\mathrm{1,2,3}}\le {\sigma}_{t}$ . When a principal stress reaches the threshold value ${\sigma}_{t}$ , the joint opens in this direction. It should be noted that the plastic strain thus created is not reversible; the model therefore does not make it possible to represent the reclosing of the joint and is valid only on a monotonic loading path.

#### 6.10.1 Operand SIGMA_T

Threshold in tension (positive value).

### 6.11 Factor keyword JOINT_MECA_RUPT

The modelling of the joints of dams relies on these material characteristics, cf. [R7.01.25](doc:r7.01.25). The hydrostatic pressure due to the possible presence of fluid in the joint is taken into account. Two industrial procedures are also implemented: keying — the injection of concrete under pressure between the blocks of the structure — and sawing — the sawing of the dam in order to release the compressive stresses. This material keyword is used by the constitutive law of the same name: JOINT_MECA_RUPT.

#### 6.11.1 Operand K_N

Normal stiffness in tension.

#### 6.11.2 Operand K_T

Tangential stiffness.

#### 6.11.3 Operand SIGM_MAX

Maximum critical stress from which the crack opens and the stress between the lips decreases. This stress is often called the tensile strength.

#### 6.11.4 Operand ALPHA

Regularisation parameter of the tangential damage. The critical opening length from which the tangential stiffness falls towards zero is defined thus:


${L}_{\mathit{CT}}={L}_{C}\tan(\mathit{ALPHA}\pi /4)$


Operand PENA_RUPTURE

Brittle-fracture smoothing parameter. The maximum opening before complete fracture is given by ${L}_{C}=\mathrm{SIGM}\text{\_}\mathrm{MAX}(1+\mathrm{PENA}\text{\_}\mathrm{RUPTURE})/K\text{\_}N$

#### 6.11.5 Operand PENA_CONTACT

Ratio between the normal stiffness in compression and in tension.

#### 6.11.6 Operand PRES_FLUIDE

Pressure on the lips of the crack due to the presence of fluid (function which may depend on geometric coordinates or on the time instant). Valid only with the mechanical-joint modellings: xxx_JOINT, and incompatible with RHO_FLUIDE, VISC_FLUIDE and OUV_MIN.

#### 6.11.7 Operand PRES_CLAVAGE

Pressure of concrete injected into the joint during the keying phase (function which may depend on geometric coordinates or on the time instant). Valid only with the mechanical-joint modellings: xxx_JOINT, and incompatible with RHO_FLUIDE, VISC_FLUIDE and OUV_MIN.

#### 6.11.8 Operand SCIAGE

The saw size used during the sawing phase. Valid only with the mechanical-joint modellings: xxx_JOINT, and incompatible with RHO_FLUIDE, VISC_FLUIDE and OUV_MIN.

#### 6.11.9 Operand RHO_FLUIDE

Density of the fluid (positive real [mass]/[volume]), valid only for the coupled hydro-mechanical modellings: xxx_JOINT_HYME and incompatible with PRES_FLUIDE and PRES_CLAVAGE

#### 6.11.10 Operand VISC_FLUIDE

Dynamic viscosity of the fluid (strictly positive real [pressure].[time]), valid only for the coupled hydro-mechanical modellings: xxx_JOINT_HYME and incompatible with PRES_FLUIDE and PRES_CLAVAGE.

#### 6.11.11 Operand OUV_MIN

Regularisation opening at the crack tip (strictly positive real [length]), valid only for the coupled hydro-mechanical modellings: xxx_JOINT_HYME and incompatible with PRES_FLUIDE and PRES_CLAVAGE.

### 6.12 Factor keyword JOINT_MECA_FROT

The modelling of friction between the joints of dams relies on this material [R7.01.25](doc:r7.01.25). The hydrostatic pressure due to the possible presence of fluid in the joint is taken into account. It is an elastoplastic version of the Mohr–Coulomb law, which depends on five parameters. Two elastic parameters: the tangential stiffness and the normal stiffness. Two parameters characterising the threshold function: adhesion and the friction coefficient. Plus a regularisation parameter of the tangent matrix in slip. An industrial procedure is also implemented: the sawing of the dam in order to release the compressive stresses. This material keyword is used by the constitutive law of the same name: JOINT_MECA_FROT.

#### 6.12.1 Operand K_N

Normal stiffness.

#### 6.12.2 Operand K_T

Tangential stiffness in the elastic domain.

#### 6.12.3 Operand MU

Friction coefficient.

#### 6.12.4 Operand ADHESION

Friction stress at zero normal stress. The tensile strength is then given by:


${R}_{T}=C/\mu$



![Figure](/figures/u4.43.01/100000000000024F000001683BE78F41A3C61079.jpg)


#### 6.12.5 Operand AMOR_NOR

Surface density of normal damping integrated over the surface of a face of a 3D_JOINT element then distributed as a discrete characteristic on each segment joining each pair of vertex nodes facing each other from one face of the element to the other. These characteristics are assigned with their full value only if the joint element is in compression: that is, if the seventh component of the internal variable of the constitutive behaviour JOINT_MECA_FROT is negative.

The unit is therefore (in SI): Pa.s.m-1

#### 6.12.6 Operand AMOR_TAN

Surface density of tangential damping integrated over the surface of a face of a 3D_JOINT element then distributed as a discrete characteristic on each segment joining each pair of vertex nodes facing each other from one face of the element to the other. These characteristics are assigned with their full value only if the joint element is in compression: that is, if the seventh component of the internal variable of the constitutive behaviour JOINT_MECA_FROT is negative.

The unit is therefore (in SI): Pa.s.m-1

#### 6.12.7 Operand COEF_AMOR

If the joint element is not in compression when the seventh component of the internal variable of the constitutive behaviour JOINT_MECA_FROT is not negative, the previous characteristics of normal or tangential damping are not assigned with their full value but with a coefficient entered by the keyword COEF_NOR.

#### 6.12.8 Operand PENA_TANG

Regularisation parameter of the tangent matrix in slip; it is introduced in order to make the elementary tangent matrix invertible. One sets it by default to a value small with respect to the tangent stiffness. If the structure is subjected to very large slips, one must check that the analysis is not sensitive to the value of this parameter.

#### 6.12.9 Operand SCIAGE

The saw size used during the sawing phase. Valid only with the mechanical-joint modellings: xxx_JOINT, and incompatible with RHO_FLUIDE, VISC_FLUIDE and OUV_MIN.

#### 6.12.10 Operand PRES_FLUIDE

Pressure on the lips of the crack due to the presence of fluid (function which may depend on geometric coordinates or on the time instant). Valid only with the mechanical-joint modellings: xxx_JOINT, and incompatible with RHO_FLUIDE, VISC_FLUIDE and OUV_MIN.

#### 6.12.11 Operand RHO_FLUIDE

Density of the fluid (positive real [mass]/[volume]), valid only for the coupled hydro-mechanical modellings: xxx_JOINT_HYME and incompatible with PRES_FLUIDE.

#### 6.12.12 Operand VISC_FLUIDE

Dynamic viscosity of the fluid (strictly positive real in units of [pressure].[time]), valid only for the coupled hydro-mechanical modellings: xxx_JOINT_HYME and incompatible with PRES_FLUIDE.

#### 6.12.13 Operand OUV_MIN

Regularisation opening at the crack tip (strictly positive real [length]), valid only for the coupled hydro-mechanical modellings: xxx_JOINT_HYME and incompatible with PRES_FLUIDE.

### 6.13 Factor keyword CZM_FROT_MIX

This factor keyword makes it possible to specify the parameters of the interface law CZM_FROT_MIX (see [R7.02.23](doc:r7.02.23)).

#### 6.13.1 Operand RIGI_NOR

This operand (optional) makes it possible to enter the normal stiffness when the interface is closed. The operand is however mandatory when the operand ADHE is specified equal to 'ELAS'.

#### 6.13.2 Operand RIGI_TAN

This operand (optional) makes it possible to enter the tangential stiffness in the reversible domain of the interface. The operand is however mandatory when the operand ADHE is specified equal to 'ELAS'.

#### 6.13.3 Operand COHESION

This operand (mandatory) makes it possible to enter the cohesion of the interface.

#### 6.13.4 Operand COEF_FROT

This operand (mandatory) makes it possible to enter the friction coefficient of the interface.

#### 6.13.5 Operands PENA_LAGR_ABSO

This operand (mandatory) makes it possible to enter the augmentation coefficient of the augmented Lagrangian of the interface, cf. [R7.02.11](doc:r7.02.11). Its dimension is that of a pressure per unit length.

#### 6.13.6 Operand ADHE

This operand (mandatory) makes it possible to characterise the reversible tangential constitutive behaviour of the interface and the normal constitutive behaviour of the interface when the latter is closed. The value 'ELAS' corresponds to a linear elastic response; the normal stiffness is then defined via RIGI_NOR, and the tangential stiffness via RIGI_TAN. The value 'PARFAITE' corresponds to the situation in which the two stiffnesses, normal and tangential, are infinitely large (rigid response).

### 6.14 Factor keyword JOINT_MECA_ENDO

The law JOINT_MECA_ENDO is a unified constitutive model for joints (main application for dams) [R7.01.25](doc:r7.01.25). The latter makes it possible to model both the fracture and the friction between the lips of the joints. The surrounding material has a purely mechanical constitutive behaviour. Friction is managed by the internal plasticity variable and fracture by the damage variable. The coupling is done through the kinematic-hardening term. The law is written in the generalised standard formalism; it admits a hydro-mechanical coupling similar to that of the previous laws JOINT_MECA_FROT and JOINT_MECA_RUPT. The keying and sawing procedures are not activated.

#### 6.14.1 Operand K_N

Normal stiffness in tension.

#### 6.14.2 Operand K_T

Tangential stiffness.

#### 6.14.3 Operand MU

Friction coefficient.

#### 6.14.4 Operand Bn

Strength in pure tension.

#### 6.14.5 Operand Bt

Strength in pure shear.

#### 6.14.6 Operand ALPHA

Initial-damage parameter. This parameter makes it possible to extend the elastic zone into the damage zone in order to favour the transition between the elastic zone and the damaging zone if convergence difficulties are encountered. By default, a value of 1e-7 is recommended.

#### 6.14.7 Operand PENA_RUPTURE

Regularisation parameter in fracture.

#### 6.14.8 Operand PRES_FLUIDE

Pressure on the lips of the crack due to the presence of fluid (function which may depend on geometric coordinates or on the time instant). Valid only with the mechanical-joint modellings: xxx_JOINT, and incompatible with RHO_FLUIDE, VISC_FLUIDE and OUV_MIN.

#### 6.14.9 Operand RHO_FLUIDE

Density of the fluid (positive real [mass]/[volume]), valid only for the coupled hydro-mechanical modellings: xxx_JOINT_HYME and incompatible with PRES_FLUIDE.

#### 6.14.10 Operand VISC_FLUIDE

Dynamic viscosity of the fluid (strictly positive real in units of [pressure].[time]), valid only for the coupled hydro-mechanical modellings: xxx_JOINT_HYME and incompatible with PRES_FLUIDE.

#### 6.14.11 Operand OUV_MIN

Regularisation opening at the crack tip (strictly positive real [length]), valid only for the coupled hydro-mechanical modellings: xxx_JOINT_HYME and incompatible with PRES_FLUIDE.

### 6.15 Factor keyword CORR_ACIER

The law CORR_ACIER is a constitutive model of steel, subjected to corrosion in reinforced-concrete structures. This model is developed in 1D and 3D elastoplastic damageable with isotropic hardening and relies on the model of Lemaître, cf. [R7.01.20](doc:r7.01.20).


$\lbrace \begin{array}{c}\frac{{\sigma}_{\mathit{eq}}}{1-D}-R\left(p\right)-{\sigma}_{y}>0\hfill \\ \dot{{\epsilon}^{p}}=\frac{3}{2}\frac{\dot{\lambda}}{1-D}\frac{\stackrel{~}{\sigma}}{{\sigma}_{\mathit{eq}}}\\ \dot{r}=\dot{\lambda}=\dot{p}\left(1-D\right)\\ R={\mathit{kp}}^{1/m}\end{array}$ . In the plastic domain $D=0$ , otherwise $D=\frac{\mathit{Dc}}{{p}_{R}-{p}_{D}}\left(p-{p}_{D}\right)$


#### 6.15.1 Operand D_CORR

Critical damage coefficient.

#### 6.15.2 Operands ECRO_K, ECRO_M

Coefficients of the hardening law $R={\mathrm{kp}}^{1/m}$ .

#### 6.15.3 Operand SY

Initial elastic limit, denoted ${\sigma}_{y}$ in the equations.

### 6.16 Factor keyword ENDO_HETEROGENE

The characteristics of the law ENDO_HETEROGENE, which is an isotropic damage model representing the formation and the propagation of cracks, are described in [R5.03.24](doc:r5.03.24). The presence of a crack in the structure is modelled by lines of broken elements ($d=1$ ). The failure of the elements may be caused either by the initiation of a new crack, or by propagation. This law is suited to heterogeneous materials (for example clay).

#### 6.16.1 Operand WEIBULL

Parameter associated with the Weibull model.

#### 6.16.2 Operand SY

Initial elastic limit, denoted ${\sigma}_{y}$ in the equations.

#### 6.16.3 Operand KI

Toughness ${K}_{\mathrm{IC}}$ .

#### 6.16.4 Operand EPAI

Thickness of the specimen represented. Warning: even if this value is purely geometric, it is necessary for this constitutive law.

#### 6.16.5 Operand GR

Seed of the random draw defining the initial defects. Makes it possible to obtain a unique result for each command file. If the seed is zero, the draw will be truly random and will differ at each launching. By default, the value is equal to 1.

### 6.17 Factor keyword RUPT_FM

The law RUPT_FM makes it possible to define the critical toughness of a material as a criterion depending on the temperature

#### 6.17.1 Operand KIC

Function of critical toughness depending on the temperature, generally expressed in $\mathit{MPa}\sqrt{(m)}$ . The user will have to pay attention to the consistency of the units used by this function. For example in SI units ($\mathit{Pa}$ , $m$ ) one will have to enter a function in $\mathit{Pa}\sqrt{(m)}$ , or for engineering units ($\mathit{MPa}$ , $\mathit{mm}$ ), one will have to enter a function in $\mathit{MPa}\sqrt{(\mathit{mm})}$ .

### 6.18 Factor keyword RUPT_TURON

Gives the characteristics of the cohesive constitutive law CZM_TURON making it possible to model the damage of a cracking interface with a coupling between the responses according to the various loading modes (opening mode N and tangent modes T1 and T2). The law indeed makes it possible to consider different properties in pure normal and tangent modes (anisotropy of the joint). It is described in detail in the documentation [R7.02.21](doc:r7.02.21). This law is typically used to model joints in structures made of composite material such as wind-turbine blades.

#### 6.18.1 Operand GC_N

Critical surface-energy density in pure normal mode expressing the energy cost of cracking in normal mode.

#### 6.18.2 Operand GC_T

Critical surface-energy density in pure tangential mode expressing the energy cost of cracking in tangential mode.

#### 6.18.3 Operand SIGM_C_N

Critical stress at fracture in pure normal mode.

#### 6.18.4 Operand SIGM_C_T

Critical stress at fracture in pure tangential mode.

#### 6.18.5 Operand K

Initial stiffness of the interface, serving as penalisation parameter.

#### 6.18.6 Operand ETA_BK

Parameter $\eta$ of the Benzeggagh–Kenane criterion.

#### 6.18.7 Operand C_RUPT

Dimensionless parameter making it possible to define a residual "post-fracture" stiffness (when the element is completely damaged and the cohesive stress is zero) as a fraction of the initial stiffness. It is worth 0.001 by default and cannot exceed 0.1.

#### 6.18.8 Operand CRIT_INIT

Parameter making it possible to choose the type of coupling between modes giving the damage-initiation criterion, among 'YE' (elliptic criterion) or 'TURON' (so-called Benzeggagh–Kenane criterion depending on the mode-mixity ratio of the loading).

## 7 Thermal characteristics

The various thermal constitutive behaviours are mutually exclusive.

### 7.1 Factor keywords THER,THER_FO

Definition of the linear thermal characteristics, constant or function defined by a concept of type fonction of the parameter 'INST'.

#### 7.1.1 Operands LAMBDA/RHO_CP


```text
LAMBDA = lambda
```


Isotropic thermal conductivity.


```text
RHO_CP = cp
```


Volumetric heat at constant pressure (product of the density and of the specific heat). It is the coefficient appearing in the equation:

$\mathit{cp}\dot{T}-div(\lambda .\mathit{grad}T)=f$

### 7.2 Factor keyword THER_ORTH

Definition of the thermal characteristics for an orthotropic material.

The reader may refer to the following documentations:

[U4.42.03](doc:u4.42.03) DEFI_COMPOSITE

[U4.42.01](doc:u4.42.01) AFFE_CARA_ELEM

in order to define the longitudinal direction associated with the shells or with the non-isotropic 3D.


![Figure](/figures/u4.43.01/10000000000002EE0000019A718721A540F524E6.png)


#### 7.2.1 Operands LAMBDA / RHO_CP


```text
LAMBDA_L = lal
```


Thermal conductivity in the longitudinal direction.


```text
LAMBDA_T = lat
```


Thermal conductivity in the transverse direction.


```text
LAMBDA_N = lan
```


Thermal conductivity in the normal direction.


```text
RHO_CP = cp
```


Volumetric heat.

### 7.3 Factor keyword THER_NL

Makes it possible to describe the thermal characteristics depending on the temperature. The formulation brings in the volumetric enthalpy (cf. [R5.02.02](doc:r5.02.02)).

$\dot{\beta}-div(\lambda (T).\mathrm{grad}T)=f$

#### 7.3.1 Operands BETA / LAMBDA / RHO_CP


```text
BETA = beta
```


Volumetric enthalpy as a function of the temperature. For the enthalpy, the extensions of the function are necessarily linear.


```text
RHO_CP = cp
```


Volumetric heat.

If the enthalpy is not supplied by the user, it will be computed by integration of RHO_CP and will not be extended to the left. RHO_CP must therefore be defined over the whole analysis range, which means that the left-hand extension of RHO_CP is ignored for the estimation of the enthalpy.


```text
LAMBDA = lambda
```


Isotropic thermal conductivity as a function of the temperature.

**Note:**

*It is not possible to use a formula for these three parameters of the material because the algorithm needs to compute their derivative many times, which is more easily accessible for a piecewise linear function. Thus the user, if wishing to use a formula rather than a function, must first tabulate it by means of the command* *CALC_FONC_INTERP.*

### 7.4 Factor keywords THER_COQUE, THER_COQUE_FO

Makes it possible to define the membrane and transverse conductivities and the thermal capacity for homogenised heterogeneous thermal shells.

Directions 1 and 2 designate those of the plane of the plate; direction 3 is perpendicular. One assumes that the conductivity tensor at each point is diagonal and that its eigenvalues are l1, l2 and l3. The coefficients are therefore defined by the user in the orthotropy frame of the plate.

The code then carries out the change of frame in order to recover the correct values in the frame of the element.

#### 7.4.1 Operands COND_LMM / COND_LMP / COND_LPP / COND_LSI / COND_TMM / COND_TMP / COND_TPP / COND_TSI

P1, P2, P3 designate the interpolation functions of the temperature through the thickness.

If a is the matrix of mean surface conductivity defined in the note [R3.11.01](doc:r3.11.01), one then has for the membrane conductivity tensor.


```text
COND_LMM = a1111
```


term related to the integral of l1*P1*P1


```text
COND_LMP = a1112
```


term related to the integral of l1*P1*P2


```text
COND_LPP = a1122
```


term related to the integral of l1*P2*P2


```text
COND_LSI = a1123
```


term related to the integral of l1*P2*P3


```text
COND_TMM = a2211
```


term related to the integral of l2*P1*P1


```text
COND_TMP = a2212
```


term related to the integral of l2*P1*P2


```text
COND_TPP = a2222
```


term related to the integral of l2*P2*P2


```text
COND_TSI = a2223
```


term related to the integral of l2*P2*P3

Operands COND_NMM / COND_NMP / COND_NPP / COND_NSI

If b is the tensor which describes the transverse conduction and the exchanges on the surfaces ${\omega}_{+}$ and ${\omega}_{-}$ , defined in the note [R3.11.01](doc:r3.11.01), one has for the transverse conductivity tensor:


```text
COND_NMM = b11
```


term related to the integral of l3*P1*P1


```text
COND_NMP = b12
```


term related to the integral of l3*P1*P2


```text
COND_NPP = b22
```


term related to the integral of l3*P2*P2


```text
COND_NSI = b23
```


term related to the integral of l3*P2*P3

#### 7.4.2 Operands CMAS_MM / CMAS_MP / CMAS_PP / CMAS_SI

One finally has for the thermal-capacity tensor.


```text
CMAS_MM = c11
```


term related to the integral of RHOCP*P1*P1


```text
CMAS_MP = c12
```


term related to the integral of RHOCP*P1*P2


```text
CMAS_PP = c22
```


term related to the integral of RHOCP*P2*P2


```text
CMAS_SI = c23
```


term related to the integral of RHOCP*P2*P3

### 7.5 Factor keyword THER_NL_ORTH

Identical to THER_NL, making it possible to describe the thermal characteristics depending on the temperature; this material moreover authorises the definition of thermal characteristics for an orthotropic material, which may themselves depend on the temperature.

The reader may refer to the following documentations:

[U4.42.03](doc:u4.42.03) DEFI_COMPOSITE

[U4.42.01](doc:u4.42.01) AFFE_CARA_ELEM

in order to define the longitudinal direction associated with the shells or with the non-isotropic 3D.


![Figure](/figures/u4.43.01/10000000000002EE0000019A718721A540F524E6.png)


#### 7.5.1 Operands BETA / LAMBDA / RHO_CP


```text
BETA = beta
```


Volumetric enthalpy as a function of the temperature. For the enthalpy, the extensions of the function are necessarily linear.


```text
RHO_CP = cp
```


Volumetric heat.

If the enthalpy is not supplied by the user, it will be computed by integration of RHO_CP and will not be extended to the left. RHO_CP must therefore be defined over the whole analysis range, which means that the left-hand extension of RHO_CP is ignored for the estimation of the enthalpy.


```text
LAMBDA_L = lal
```


Thermal conductivity in the longitudinal direction.


```text
LAMBDA_T = lat
```


Thermal conductivity in the transverse direction.


```text
LAMBDA_N = lan
```


Thermal conductivity in the normal direction.

**Note:**

*It is not possible to use a formula for these parameters of the material because the algorithm needs to compute their derivative many times, which is more easily accessible for a piecewise linear function. Thus the user, if wishing to use a formula rather than a function, must first tabulate it by means of the command* *CALC_FONC_INTERP.*

## 8 Characteristics specific to concretes

### 8.1 Factor keyword THER_HYDR

Makes it possible to define the constitutive behaviour associated with the hydration of concrete.

The hydration of concrete is a phenomenon which is accompanied by a release of heat depending on the temperature [R7.01.12](doc:r7.01.12).


$\begin{array}{cc}\begin{array}{}\frac{d\beta }{\mathrm{dt}}+divq=Q\frac{d\xi (T)}{\mathrm{dt}}+s\\ q=-\lambda \mathrm{grad}T\end{array}\rbrace & \text{éq 7.1-1}\\ \frac{d\xi }{\mathrm{dt}}=\mathrm{AFF}(\xi ,T)& \text{éq 7.1-2}\end{array}$


#### 8.1.1 Operands LAMBDA/ BETA


```text
LAMBDA = lambda
```


Isotropic thermal conductivity as a function of the temperature.


```text
BETA = beta
```


Volumetric enthalpy as a function of the temperature. The extensions are at least linear, the volumetric enthalpy being definable as the integral of the volumetric heat.

#### 8.1.2 Operand AFFINITE


```text
AFFINITE = AFF
```


Function of the degree of hydration and of the temperature. In general, one uses:

$\text{AFF}(\xi ,T)=A(\xi )\exp(-\frac{{E}_{a}}{\mathrm{RT}})$ with $\text{QSR\_K}=\frac{{E}_{a}}{R}$ the Arrhenius constant expressed in degrees Kelvin, and A determined by a calorimetric test of the concrete (function of the quantity HYDR).

#### 8.1.3 Operand CHAL_HYDR


```text
CHAL_HYDR = Q
```


Heat released per unit of hydration (assumed constant); this function depends on the type of concrete.

### 8.2 Factor keyword BETON_DESORP

Definition of the sorption–desorption curve (or sorption isotherm) by a function, formula or nappe (keyword LEVERETT='NON') or else by means of the Leverett model (keyword LEVERETT='OUI').

#### 8.2.1 Case LEVERETT='NON': Operand FONC_DESORP

Sorption–desorption curve or nappe [R7.01.12](doc:r7.01.12) giving the hygrometry $h$ as a function of the water concentration $C$ (command variable SECH in mechanics and state variable TEMP in thermal analysis applied to drying).
This sorption–desorption curve may also depend on the temperature (command variable TEMP in mechanics, variable TSEC for a drying analysis).

#### 8.2.2 Case LEVERETT='OUI': Operands VG_PR, VG_N, ATH, TEMP_0_C, PORO and UNITE_PRESSION

The first 5 operands make it possible to define a sorption–desorption curve from the Leverett model which gives the capillary pressure as a function of the water concentration and of the temperature.
In this case, the command variables SECH and TEMP must imperatively be declared in the material for the mechanical analyses.


```text
♦ VG_PR
```


Parameter $\mathit{Pr}$ in the Van Genuchten model [Pa]. Denoted $\alpha$ in the formulae below.


```text
♦ VG_N
```


Parameter $N$ , in the Van Genuchten model. Denoted $\beta$ in the formulae below.


```text
♦ ATH
```


Constant making it possible to take into account the effects of the temperature. Denoted $A_d$ in the formulae below.


```text
♦ TEMP_0_C
```


Reference temperature, in degrees Celsius [°C], at which the Van Genuchten parameters were fitted. The corresponding temperature in degrees Kelvin is denoted $T_0$ in the formulae below.


```text
♦ PORO
```


Porosity of the material, denoted $\Phi$.


```text
◇ UNITE_PRESSION = / "MPa",
                    / "Pa" (par défaut)
```


This sixth operand has an effect only during the computation of the option HYGR_ELGA by CALC_CHAMP (available only on the concepts EVOL_SECH).
It makes it possible to define the unit of the component PCAP (capillary pressure) of this field.

The value of the hygrometry is then given by the following formula, considering $T$ and $T_0$ in degrees Kelvin [°K]


$$
h(C, T)= \exp \left[Pc(C, T) \frac{M}{\rho_l(T) R T} \right]
$$


where $M$ is the molar mass of water, $R$ the perfect-gas constant and where $Pc$ corresponds to the following expression:


$$
Pc(C, T) = -\frac{\rho_l(T_0) R T_0}{\alpha M} \left((\dfrac{C}{\Phi*1000})^{-1/\beta}-1\right)^{1-\beta} \frac{\gamma_{lv}(T_0)}{\gamma_{lv}(T)}\sqrt{\frac{m(T_0)}{m(T)}}
$$


with


$$
\frac{m(T)}{m(T_0)} = 10^{A_d\left[2\cdot10^{-3} (T-T_0)-10^{-6} (T-T_0)^2\right]}
$$

$$
\rho_l(T) = 314.4+685.6\left[1-\left(\frac{T-273.15}{374.14}\right)^{1/0.55}\right]^{0.55}
$$

$$
\gamma_{lv}(T) = 0.1558 \left(1-\frac{T}{647.1}\right)^{1.26}
$$


### 8.3 Factor keyword SECH_GRANGER

Definition of the parameters characterising the diffusion coefficient $D\left(C,T\right)$ appearing in the non-linear drying equation proposed by Granger (cf. [R7.01.12](doc:r7.01.12)). These characteristics are constants, whereas the diffusion coefficient depends on the analysis variable, that is the current water concentration $C$ , (as the thermal conductivity depended on the temperature).

#### 8.3.1 Operands A/B/QSR_K/TEMP_0_C

These coefficients make it possible to express the diffusion coefficient in its form most commonly used in the literature and proposed by Granger:


$D\left(C,T\right)=a.{e}^{\left(b.C\right)}\frac{T}{{T}_{0}}{e}^{\left[-\frac{Q}{R}\left(\frac{1}{T}-\frac{1}{{T}_{0}}\right)\right]}$



```text
A= a
```


Diffusion coefficient varying from $0.5{10}^{-13}$ to ${2.10}^{-13}{m}^{2}/s$ for concrete.


```text
B= b
```


Coefficient of the order of $0.05$ for concrete.


```text
QSR_K= QsR
```


QsR is in general worth $4700.K$ . ($R$  is the perfect-gas constant).


```text
TEMP_0_C= T0
```


Reference temperature in the Arrhenius law. The reference temperature $\mathit{T0}$ is **in degrees Celsius** , and converted into kelvin during the solution.

### 8.4 Factor keyword SECH_MENSI

Definition of the parameters characterising the diffusion coefficient appearing in the non-linear drying equation proposed by Mensi (cf. [R7.01.12](doc:r7.01.12)). These characteristics are constants, whereas the diffusion coefficient depends on the analysis variable, that is the current water concentration $C$ , (as the thermal conductivity depended on the temperature). It is a simplified formulation of the general case, constituting the law of Mensi.

Operands A/B

These coefficients make it possible to express the diffusion coefficient according to the law of Mensi:


$\xi =\frac{{\epsilon}_{r}^{n-1}-{\epsilon}_{y}^{n-1}}{{\epsilon}_{y}^{n}-{\epsilon}_{r}^{n-1}}$



$D\left(C\right)=a.{e}^{\left(b.C\right)}$


A= a

Diffusion coefficient varying from $0.5{.10}^{-13}$ to ${2.10}^{-13}{m}^{2}/s$ for concrete.


```text
B= b
```


Coefficient of the order of $0.05$ for concrete.

### 8.5 Factor keyword SECH_BAZANT

Definition of the parameters characterising the diffusion coefficient appearing in the non-linear drying equation proposed by Bazant (see [R7.01.12](doc:r7.01.12)). These characteristics are constants, whereas the diffusion coefficient depends on the analysis variable, that is the current water concentration $C$ , (as the thermal conductivity depended on the temperature). This formulation constitutes the law of Bazant.

#### 8.5.1 Operands D1/ALPHA_BAZANT/N/ FONC_DESORP

These coefficients make it possible to express the diffusion coefficient according to the law of Bazant:


$D(h)={d}_{1}(\alpha +\frac{1-\alpha }{1+{(\frac{1-h}{1-0.75})}^{n}})$


where $h$ is the degree of hydration, related to the water concentration by the desorption curve.


```text
D1 = d1
```


Diffusion coefficient which is of the order of ${3.10}^{-13}{m}^{2}/s$ for concrete.


```text
ALPHA_BAZANT = alpha
```


Coefficient varying from $0.025$ to $0.1$ for concrete.


```text
N = n
```


Exponent of the order of 6 for concrete.


```text
FONC_DESORP = desorp
```


Desorption curve, making it possible to pass from the water concentration to the degree of hydration $h$ .

**Important note:**

desorp *is a function of the analysis variable,* $C$ *, the water concentration, which is assimilated for the solution to a temperature, of type* *'TEMP'.*

### 8.6 Factor keyword SECH_NAPPE

The diffusion coefficient, characterising the non-linear drying equation, is expressed by means of a nappe, a tabulated function of the water concentration, analysis variable, and of the temperature, auxiliary analysis variable, given in the form of a data structure of type evol_ther. For the solution of the drying by the operator THER_NON_LINE, the water concentration is assimilated to a temperature, of type 'TEMP'.

For the consistency of the data, the parameters of the nappe, that is the analysis variable and the auxiliary variable, cannot be of the same type. A new type of variable has been added in DEFI_NAPPE, the "type of the temperature computed prior to the drying", 'TSEC', which does indeed correspond to a temperature.

#### 8.6.1 Operand FONCTION

The diffusion coefficient is expressed by means of a function tabulated on the parameters $C$ and $T.$


```text
FONCTION = nom_fonc
```


Name of the nappe.

### 8.7 Factor keyword SECH_RFT

Definition of the parameters characterising the diffusion coefficient $D\left(C,T\right)$ appearing in the non-linear drying equation according to the model RICHARD_FICK_TEMPERATURE (RFT) (cf. [R7.01.12](doc:r7.01.12)). These characteristics are constants, whereas the diffusion coefficient depends on the analysis variable, that is the current water concentration $C$ and on the temperature $T$.
The expression of $D\left(C,T\right)$ is fairly complex; it is presented in detail in R7.01.12.

#### 8.7.1 Operands PERM_IN, A_MIL, B_MIL, QSR_K, VG_M_P


```text
♦ PERM_IN
```


Intrinsic permeability of the liquid (homogeneous to an area)


```text
♦ A_MIL
```


First Millington coefficient


```text
♦ B_MIL
```


Second Millington coefficient


```text
♦ QSR_K
```


Activation energy [K]


```text
♦ VG_M_P
```


Pore interaction factor

#### 8.7.2 Operands UNITE_LONGUEUR and UNITE_TEMPS

These two operands make it possible to adapt the computation of the diffusion coefficient of the law to the system of units.


```text
◇ UNITE_LONGUEUR = / "M" (par défaut),
                    / "MM",
```


Unit of length used in the analysis


```text
◇ UNITE_TEMPS = / "H",
                 / "J",
                 / "MIN",
                 / "S" (par défaut),
```


Unit of time used in the analysis

### 8.8 Factor keywords BPEL_BETON,BPEL_ACIER

Definition of the characteristics appearing in the constitutive model of the prestressing cables within the regulatory framework of the BPEL [R7.01.02](doc:r7.01.02).

The linear elastic characteristics of the concrete material and of the steel material must be defined simultaneously under the keyword ELAS.

#### 8.8.1 Operands

Constitutive behaviour: BPEL_BETON

Factor keyword for the definition of the characteristic parameters of the concrete material which appear in the estimation of the tension losses along the prestressing cables. This factor keyword can be used only jointly with the factor keyword ELAS.


```text
PERT_FLUA = xflu
```


Flat-rate ratio of tension loss by creep of the concrete, with respect to the initial tension.


$\Delta {F}_{\mathit{flu}}={x}_{\mathit{flu}}.{F}_{0}$ where ${F}_{0}$ denotes the initial tension defined by DEFI_CABLE_BP. [U4.42.04]

The default value is 0: in this case, one does not take into account the tension losses by creep of the concrete.

Warning: this value will not be affected by the entering of the relaxation coefficient R_J in DEFI_CABLE_BP. The value xflu must therefore take this effect into account (multiplication by $r(t)=\frac{t}{t+9{r}_{m}}$ , $t$ corresponding to the date at which one wants to estimate the state of the structure and ${r}_{m}$ the mean radius).


```text
PERT_RETR = xret
```


Flat-rate ratio of tension loss by shrinkage of the concrete, with respect to the initial tension.


$\Delta {F}_{\mathit{ret}}={x}_{\mathit{ret}}.{F}_{0}$ where ${F}_{0}$ denotes the initial tension.

The default value is 0: in this case, one does not take into account the tension losses by shrinkage of the concrete.

Warning: this value will not be affected by the entering of the relaxation coefficient ${R}_{J}$ in DEFI_CABLE_BP. The value xret must therefore take this effect into account (multiplication by $r(t)=\frac{t}{t+9{r}_{m}}$ , $t$ corresponding to the date at which one wants to estimate the state of the structure and ${r}_{m}$ the mean radius).

Constitutive behaviour: BPEL_ACIER

Factor keyword for the definition of the characteristic parameters of the steel material which appear in the estimation of the tension losses along the prestressing cables. This factor keyword can be used only jointly with the factor keyword ELAS.


```text
RELAX_1000 = rh1000
```


Relaxation of the steel at 1000 hours, expressed in %.

The default value is 0: in this case, one does not take into account the tension losses by relaxation of the steel.


```text
MU0_RELAX = mu0
```


Dimensionless relaxation coefficient of the prestressed steel. The default value is 0.


```text
F_PRG = fprg
```


Guaranteed stress of the maximum load at fracture (according to the BPEL)

If one takes into account the tension losses by relaxation of the steel (RELAX_1000 entered with a non-zero value), one must obligatorily enter the operand F_PRG, with a non-zero value.


```text
FROT_COURB = f
```


Friction coefficient between the cable and the duct, in the curved part, in ${\mathit{rad}}^{–1}$ . The default value is 0.


```text
FROT_LINE = phi
```


Friction coefficient per unit length ${m}^{-1}$ , in the straight part. The default value is 0. **Note:**

*FROT_LINE=FROT_COURB*PERT_LIGNE.*

### 8.9 Factor keywords ETCC_BETON,ETCC_ACIER

Definition of the characteristics appearing in the constitutive model of the prestressing cables, within the regulatory framework of the ETCC [R7.01.02](doc:r7.01.02).

The linear elastic characteristics of the concrete material and of the steel material must be defined simultaneously under the keyword ELAS.

#### 8.9.1 Operands

Constitutive behaviour: ETCC_BETON

Factor keyword to be indicated in order to be able to compute the tension in the cables according to the formulae of the ETCC. No information is required. This factor keyword can be used only jointly with the factor keyword ELAS.

Constitutive behaviour: ETCC_ACIER

Factor keyword for the definition of the characteristic parameters of the steel material which appear in the estimation of the tension losses along the prestressing cables. This factor keyword can be used only jointly with the factor keyword ELAS.


```text
RELAX_1000 = rh1000
```


Relaxation of the steel at 1000 hours, expressed in %.

The default value is 0: in this case, one does not take into account the tension losses by relaxation of the steel.


```text
F_PRG = fprg
```


Guaranteed stress of the maximum load at fracture (according to the ETCC).

If one takes into account the tension losses by relaxation of the steel (RELAX_1000 entered with a non-zero value), one must obligatorily enter the operand F_PRG, with a non-zero value.


```text
COEF_FROT = f
```


Friction coefficient between the cable and its duct in the curved part, in ${\mathit{rad}}^{–1}$ . The default value is 0.


```text
PERT_LIGNE = phi
```


In-line loss coefficient in ${\mathit{rad.m}}^{-1}$ . The default value is 0.

**Note:**

*PERT_LIGNE=FROT_LINE/FROT_COURB.*

### 8.10 Factor keyword BETON_DOUBLE_DP

The 3D constitutive model developed in *Code_Aster* is formulated within the framework of thermoplasticity, for the description of the non-linear constitutive behaviour of concrete, in tension and in compression, with the taking into account of the irreversible variations of the thermal and mechanical characteristics of the concrete, particularly sensitive at high temperature [R7.01.03](doc:r7.01.03).

The functions may depend on the following command variables:

'TEMP','INST','HYDR','SECH'.

BETON_DOUBLE_DP makes it possible to define all the characteristics associated with the constitutive law with double Drücker–Prager criterion. In addition to these characteristics, the elastic modulus, Poisson's ratio, and the thermal expansion coefficient $\alpha$ , as well as the coefficients of autogenous shrinkage and of desiccation shrinkage, must be defined under the keyword ELAS for the real coefficients, or ELAS_FO, for the coefficients defined by functions, or by nappes. All the characteristics of the model, $\text{(E, nu,}\alpha ,f'c,f't,\beta ,\text{Gc,Gt)}$ of type [fonction] may depend on one or two variables among the temperature, the hydration and the drying. When they depend on the temperature, they are functions of the maximum of the temperature reached during the loading history $\theta$ , which is kept in memory for each Gauss point, in the form of an internal variable. This makes it possible to take into account the irreversible variations of these characteristics at high temperature.

#### 8.10.1 Operands F_C /F_T /COEF_BIAX


```text
F_C= f’c
```


Strength in uniaxial compression $f{'}_{c}$ .


```text
F_T= f’t
```


Strength in uniaxial tension $f{'}_{t}$ .


```text
COEF_BIAX= beta
```


The ratio of the biaxial compressive strength to the uniaxial compressive strength $\beta$ .

#### 8.10.2 Operands ENER_COMP_RUPT /ENER_TRAC_RUPT / COEF_ELAS_COMP


```text
ENER_COMP_RUPT= Gc
```


The fracture energy in compression ${G}_{c}$ *,*


```text
ENER_TRAC_RUPT= Gt
```


The fracture energy in tension ${G}_{t}$ .


```text
COEF_ELAS_COMP= phi
```


The elastic limit in compression, given by a coefficient of proportionality as a percentage of the peak strength ${f}_{c}^{'}(\theta )$ is in general of the order of 30% for standard concretes. It is important to emphasise that this parameter is a real and not a function.

#### 8.10.3 Operands LONG_CARA

This operand makes it possible to overload the characteristic length computed automatically, for each mesh cell, as a function of its dimensions (from its area in 2D, from its volume in 3D).

The characteristic length computed automatically makes it possible, when the fineness of the mesh changes from one analysis to another, to keep stable results by avoiding localisation phenomena. This length, computed automatically or given by the user, leads to the value of the ultimate hardening in tension according to the formula (for a linear post-peak hardening):


${\kappa}_{u}\left(\theta \right)=\frac{2.{G}_{t}\left(\theta \right)}{{l}_{c}.{f}_{t}^{\prime }\left(\theta \right)}$


In the particular case of a mesh containing adjacent mesh cells whose dimensions are very different, the ultimate hardenings of the model BETON_DOUBLE_DP computed from the characteristic length of the mesh cells are consequently very different, which may generate convergence problems or lead to a rather unphysical stress state. (This characteristic length is computed from the volume of the current mesh cell). For this reason, one proposes to give the user the possibility of defining a mean length which overloads the characteristic length computed for each mesh cell. The default value of *Code_Aster* is the characteristic length computed for each mesh cell.

Choosing an arbitrary length identical for all the mesh cells may also generate convergence difficulties. The best solution consists in creating a mesh whose variations of the dimensions of the mesh cells respect the sense of variation of the stress field, and in using the characteristic length computed automatically as a function of the size of the mesh cells. The overloading by LONG_CARA must be reserved for particular cases, when the user cannot freely intervene on the mesh.

In the case where the user defines the characteristic length in the material, he or she will choose a pair $({G}_{t},\text{LONG\_CARA})$ such that $\frac{2.{G}_{t}\left(\theta \right)}{{l}_{c}.{f}_{t}^{\prime }\left(\theta \right)}$ is worth the value that he or she wishes for the ultimate hardening in tension ${\kappa}_{u}$ . (The usual value of the strain associated with the ultimate hardening in tension of an average concrete is $5.E-4$ ).

#### 8.10.4 Operands ECRO_COMP_P_PIC / ECRO_TRAC_P_PIC

The parameters making it possible to define the softening curve in compression and in tension are optional, and have default values.


```text
ECRO_COMP_P_PIC=     /    ’LINEAIRE’
                     /    ’PARABOLE’
```


Shape of the post-peak curve in compression of text type, which may take the values 'LINEAIRE' and 'PARABOLE'. The non-linear curve is then of parabolic type.


```text
ECRO_TRAC_P_PIC=     /    ’LINEAIRE’
                     /    ’EXPONENT’
```


Shape of the post-peak curve in tension of text type, which may take the values 'LINEAIRE' and 'EXPONENT'. The non-linear curve is then of exponential type.

### 8.11 Factor keyword BETON_GRANGER,V_BETON_GRANGER

Definition of the material parameters for the viscoelastic model of Granger, modelling the basic creep of concrete. There exist 2 constitutive relations: the first (BETON_GRANGER) does not take into account the ageing phenomenon but models the effect of hygrometry. The second (BETON_GRANGER_V) takes into account the effects of ageing and of hygrometry (Cf. [R7.01.01](doc:r7.01.01)).

In 1D and under the action of a constant stress ${\sigma}_{0}$ , the creep strain as a function of time and of the loading instant ${t}_{c}$ is written: ${\epsilon}_{\mathit{fl}}\left(t\right)=J\left(t,{t}_{c},\right)\cdot {\sigma}_{0}$

$(0.5{10}^{-4}<{\epsilon}_{d0}<1.5{10}^{-4})$

The creep function $J\left(t,{t}_{c}\right)$ is worth:


$J(t,{t}_{c})=k\left(a\left({t}_{c}\right)\right)\cdot \sum_{s=0}^{n}{J}_{s}\left(1-exp\left(\frac{t-{t}_{c}}{{\tau}_{s}}\right)\right)$


where $a$ is the age of the material, that is the time elapsed since the placing of the concrete. It is an internal variable of the model.

The function $k\left(a\left({t}_{c}\right)\right)$ which appears in $J\left(t,{t}_{c}\right)$ serves to model the ageing because it introduces the direct dependence on the loading instant. One may use for example the CEB curve which models the ageing due to hydration:


$k\left(a\right)=\frac{{28}^{0.2}+0.1}{{a}^{0.2}+0.1}$ with $a$ in days

Without ageing this function is constant and is worth $1$ , the creep function in this case depending only on the time elapsed since the loading $t-{t}_{c}$ .

Hygrometry is taken into account by means of an equivalent stress $S=h\cdot \sigma$ , $h$ being the relative humidity of the material. It is therefore necessary to enter the desorption isotherm $c$ which makes it possible to pass from the water content $C$ to $h$ *:* $h={c}^{-1}\left(C\right)$ . This curve is supplied under the keyword BETON_DESORP.

**Note:**

*This constitutive behaviour may be associated with the drying and thermo-hydration shrinkages defined by the operands* *K_DESSIC,* *B_ENDOGE*  and *ALPHA under the keyword* *ELAS_FO.*

For the law BETON_GRANGER, the parameters of the law are to be entered under the keyword: BETON_GRANGER. For the law BETON_GRANGER_V one must also enter the keyword BETON_GRANGER but one must add to it the keyword V_BETON_GRANGER for the parameters specific to the ageing law.

The internal variables of the constitutive law are described in [R7.01.01](doc:r7.01.01).

#### 8.11.1 Operands for the basic creep


```text
8 coefficients matériaux de la fonction de fluage, homogènes à un temps.

J1            =    J1

...

...

J8            =    J8
```

```text
8 temps de retard de la fonction de fluage.

TAUX_1    =tau1

...

...

TAUX_8    =tau8
```


#### 8.11.2 Operands for the ageing


```text
FONC_V = k(a)
```


Ageing function.

### 8.12 Factor keyword MAZARS, MAZARS_FO

The constitutive model of Mazars is an elastic damageable constitutive model making it possible to describe the softening behaviour of concrete. It distinguishes the behaviour in tension and in compression, but uses only a single scalar damage variable (see [R7.01.08](doc:r7.01.08)). The Mazars model implemented corresponds to the 2012 version, that is to the reformulation improving the behaviour in bi-compression and in pure shear.

The parameters may be a function of the temperature, of the hydration and of the drying. In this case one must use MAZARS_FO. In the case of this constitutive law the parameters depend on the maximum temperature reached during the whole loading history.

The functions may depend on the following command variables: 'TEMP', 'HYDR', 'SECH'.

MAZARS (or MAZARS_FO) makes it possible to define all the characteristics associated with the constitutive model of Mazars. In addition to these characteristics, the elastic constants must be defined under the keyword ELAS for the real coefficients or ELAS_FO for the coefficients depending on the temperature.

#### 8.12.1 Operands: EPSD0 / EPSC0/ EPST0 /AC/ AT/ BC/ BT/ k

♦ / epsd0 = epsd0

Damage threshold in strain.

♦ / epsC0 = epsc0

epsT0 = epst0

Damage threshold in strain, in compression and in tension.

If ${\epsilon}_{d0}$ is given ${\epsilon}_{t0}$ and ${\epsilon}_{c0}$ are computed by:



$\begin{array}{c}{\epsilon}_{t0}={\epsilon}_{d0}\\ {\epsilon}_{c0}=\frac{{\epsilon}_{d0}}{\nu \ast \sqrt{2}}\end{array}$



♦ AC = ac

Coefficient making it possible to set the shape of the post-peak curve in compression. Introduces a horizontal asymptote which is the $\varepsilon$ axis for $\mathrm{Ac}=1$ and the horizontal passing through the peak for $\mathrm{Ac}=0$ (generally ).

♦ AT = at

Coefficient making it possible to set the shape of the post-peak curve in tension. Introduces a horizontal asymptote which is the $\varepsilon$ axis for $\mathrm{Ac}=1$ and the horizontal passing through the peak for $\mathrm{Ac}=0$ (generally $0.7<\mathrm{At}<1$ ).

♦ BC=bc

Coefficient making it possible to set the shape of the post-peak curve in compression. Depending on its value it may correspond to an abrupt drop of the stress ($\mathrm{BC}<{10}^{4}$ ) or a preliminary phase of increase of the stress followed by a more or less rapid decrease (generally ${10}^{3}<\mathrm{Bc}<2.{10}^{3}$ ).

♦BT=bt

Coefficient making it possible to set the shape of the post-peak curve in tension. Depending on its value it may correspond to an abrupt drop of the stress ($\mathrm{BC}<{10}^{4}$ ) or a preliminary phase of increase of the stress followed by a more or less rapid decrease (generally ${10}^{\mathrm{4 }}<\mathrm{Bt}<{10}^{5}$ ).

♦K=k

Parameter introducing a horizontal asymptote in pure shear. It lies between 0 and 1. Recommended value $0,7$ .

#### 8.12.2 Operand CHI

♦CHI=chi

Within the framework of the coupling of BETON_UMLV with the law of MAZARS. The parameter chi makes it possible to define the importance of the coupling:

> $\mathrm{CHI}=0$  : no coupling,
> $\mathrm{CHI}=1$  : total coupling.

The total coupling generates a premature appearance of the damage of the concrete. The recommended value lies in the interval: $[0.4;0.7]$ .

#### 8.12.3 Operands SIGM_LIM, EPSI_LIM

◊SIGM_LIM=sglim

Definition of the limit stress.

◊EPSI_LIM=eplim

Definition of the limit strain.

The operands sigm_LIM and espi_LIM make it possible to define the bounds in stress and in strain which correspond to the serviceability and ultimate limit states, classically used in civil-engineering studies.

The operands sigm_LIM and espi_LIM make it possible to define the bounds in stress and in strain which correspond to the serviceability and ultimate limit states, classically used in civil-engineering studies.

**Note:**  These bounds are mandatory when one uses the constitutive behaviour mazars_UNIL (see [R7.01.08](doc:r7.01.08) Damage model of MAZARS, [U4.42.07](doc:u4.42.07)DEFI_MATER_GC). *In the other cases they are not taken into account* .

### 8.13 Keyword BETON_UMLV

The UMLV creep law assumes a total decoupling between the spherical and deviatoric components: the strains induced by the spherical stresses are purely spherical and the strains induced by the deviatoric stresses are purely deviatoric [R7.01.06](doc:r7.01.06). Moreover, the basic-creep strain is assumed proportional to the internal relative humidity:


Spherical part: ${\epsilon}^{s}=h\cdot f\left({\sigma}^{s}\right)$  and, deviatoric part: $\underline{\underline{{\epsilon}^{d}}}=h\cdot f\left(\stackrel{~}{\underline{\underline{\sigma}}}\right)$


where $h$ denotes the internal relative humidity.

The constitutive model BETON_UMLV is a non-ageing viscoelastic model developed in partnership with the Université de Marne-la-Vallée in order to describe the basic creep of concretes. It is particularly suited to multiaxial configurations by not presupposing the value of the creep Poisson's ratio.

The spherical stresses are at the origin of the migration of the water absorbed at the interfaces between the hydrates at the scale of the macro-porosity and absorbed within the micro-porosity into the capillary porosity. The diffusion of the inter-lamellar water from the hydrate pores towards the capillary porosity takes place in an irreversible manner. The total spherical creep strain is therefore written as the sum of a reversible part and of an irreversible part:


${\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{fs}}=\underset{\begin{array}{}\mathrm{partie}\\ \mathrm{réversible}\end{array}}{\underset{\underbrace{}}{{\varepsilon}_{r}^{\mathrm{fs}}}}+\underset{\begin{array}{}\mathrm{partie}\\ \mathrm{irréversible}\end{array}}{\underset{\underbrace{}}{{\varepsilon}_{i}^{\mathrm{fs}}}}$


The process of spherical creep strain is governed by the following system of coupled equations:


$\lbrace \begin{array}{c}{\dot{\varepsilon}}^{\mathrm{fs}}=\frac{1}{{\eta}_{r}^{s}}\cdot \left[h\cdot {\sigma}^{s}-{k}_{r}^{s}\cdot {\varepsilon}_{r}^{\mathrm{fs}}\right]-{\dot{\varepsilon}}_{i}^{\mathrm{fs}}\\ {\dot{\varepsilon}}_{i}^{\mathrm{fs}}=\frac{1}{{\eta}_{i}^{s}}{\langle \left[{k}_{r}^{s}\cdot {\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{fs}}-({k}_{r}^{s}+{k}_{i}^{s})\cdot {\varepsilon}_{i}^{\mathrm{fs}}\right]-\left[h{\sigma}^{s}-{k}_{r}^{s}\cdot {\varepsilon}_{r}^{\mathrm{fs}}\right]\rangle }^{+}\end{array}$


where ${k}_{r}^{s}$ denotes the apparent stiffness associated with the skeleton formed by blocks of hydrates at the mesoscopic scale; ${\eta}_{r}^{s}$  the apparent viscosity associated with the diffusion mechanism within the capillary porosity; ${k}_{i}^{s}$ denotes the apparent stiffness associated intrinsically with the hydrates at the microscopic scale and ${\eta}_{i}^{s}$ the apparent viscosity associated with the interfoliar diffusion mechanism.

(The brackets ${⟨⟩}^{+}$ denote the MacCauley operator: ${⟨x⟩}^{+}=\frac{1}{2}\left(x+|x|\right)$ )

The deviatoric stresses are at the origin of a sliding mechanism (or quasi-dislocation mechanism) of the CSH sheets in the nano-porosity. Under deviatoric stress, the creep takes place at constant volume. Moreover, the UMLV creep law assumes the isotropy of the deviatoric creep. Phenomenologically, the sliding mechanism comprises a reversible viscoelastic contribution of the water strongly adsorbed on the CSH sheets and an irreversible viscous contribution of the free water:


$\underset{\begin{array}{c}\mathit{déformation}\\ \mathit{déviatorique}\\ \mathit{totale}\end{array}}{\underset{\underbrace{}}{{\underline{\underline{\varepsilon}}}^{\mathit{fd}}}}=\underset{\begin{array}{c}\mathit{contribution}\\ \mathit{eau}\\ \mathit{absorbée}\end{array}}{\underset{\underbrace{}}{{\underline{\underline{\varepsilon}}}_{\text{}r}^{\mathit{fd}}}}+\underset{\begin{array}{c}\mathit{contribution}\\ \mathit{eau}\\ \mathit{libre}\end{array}}{\underset{\underbrace{}}{{\underline{\underline{\varepsilon}}}_{\text{}i}^{\mathit{fd}}}}$


The *jth* principal component of the total deviatoric strain is governed by the following system of equations:


${\tilde{\dot{\sigma}}}^{j}(1+\frac{{\eta}_{r}^{d}}{{\eta}_{i}^{d}})+\frac{{k}_{r}^{d}}{{\eta}_{i}^{d}}{\tilde{\sigma}}^{j}={\eta}_{r}^{d}{\ddot{\varepsilon}}^{d,j}+{k}_{r}^{d}{\dot{\varepsilon}}^{d,j}$


where ${k}_{r}^{d}$ denotes the stiffness associated with the capacity of the absorbed water to transmit loads (*load bearing water*); ${\eta}_{r}^{d}$ the viscosity associated with the water adsorbed by the hydrate sheets and ${\eta}_{i}^{d}$ denotes the viscosity associated with the free water.

#### 8.13.1 Operand


```text
K_RS    =    K_RS
```


${k}_{r}^{s}$ apparent stiffness associated with the skeleton formed by blocks of hydrates at the mesoscopic scale.


```text
K_IS    =    K_IS
```



:math:`{k}_{i}^{s}` apparent stiffness associated intrinsically with the hydrates at the microscopic scale.



```text
K_RD    =    K_RD
```



:math:`{k}_{r}^{d}` stiffness associated with the capacity of the adsorbed water to transmit loads (load bearing water).



```text
ETA_RS    =    ETA_RS
```



:math:`{eta}_{r}^{s}` apparent viscosity associated with the diffusion mechanism within the capillary porosity.



```text
ETA_IS    =    ETA_IS
```



:math:`{eta}_{i}^{s}` apparent viscosity associated with the interlamellar diffusion mechanism.



```text
ETA_RD    =    ETA_RD
```



:math:`{eta}_{r}^{d}` viscosity associated with the water absorbed by the hydrate sheets.



> ETA_FD        =    ETA_FD


makes it possible to take into account the desiccation creep according to the law of Bazant.

Note:

*The desorption curve giving the hygrometry* $h$ *as a function of the water concentration* $C$ *must be entered under the keyword* *BETON_DESORP.*

### 8.14 Factor keyword BETON_ECRO_LINE

Definition of a linear hardening curve with taking into account of the confinement in the case specific to concrete. In order to improve the behaviour in compression one defines a reversibility threshold ( [R7.01.04](doc:r7.01.04) model ENDO_ISOT_BETON).

#### 8.14.1 Operands


```text
D_SIGM_EPSI = dsde (ET)
```


Slope of the traction curve.


```text
SYT = sigt
```


Maximum stress in simple tension.


```text
SYC = sigc
```


Maximum stress in simple compression (it does not exist for a Poisson's ratio $\nu =0$ ; in this case one does not specify $\mathrm{SYC}$ )

Young's modulus $E$ is to be specified by the keywords ELAS or ELAS_FO.

### 8.15 Factor keyword ENDO_ORTH_BETON

Definition of the parameters of the constitutive law ENDO_ORTH_BETON, making it possible to describe the anisotropy induced by the damage of the concrete, as well as the unilateral effects [R7.01.09](doc:r7.01.09). One will refer to the documents [R7.01.09](doc:r7.01.09) and [V6.04.176](doc:v6.04.176) for the precise meaning of the parameters and the identification procedure.

#### 8.15.1 Operand ALPHA

Coupling constant between the evolution of the tensile damage and that of the compressive damage. It must be taken between $0$ and $1$ , rather close to $1$ . The default value is $0.9$ .

#### 8.15.2 Operands K0 / K1 / K2

K0 = k0

Constant part of the threshold function. Makes it possible to calibrate the height of the peak in tension.

K1 = k1

Parameter of the threshold function making it possible to increase the threshold in compression.

K2 = k2

Parameter controlling the shape of the failure envelope for biaxial tests. The default value is ${7.10}^{-4}$ .

#### 8.15.3 Operands ECROB / ECROD


```text
ECROB = ecrob
```


Term of the blocked energy (equivalent to a hardening energy) relating to the evolution of the tensile damage. It makes it possible to control the shape of the peak in tension.


```text
ECROD = ecrod
```


Term of the blocked energy (equivalent to a hardening energy) relating to the evolution of the compressive damage. It makes it possible to control the shape of the peak in compression.

Young's modulus $E$ and Poisson's ratio $\nu$ are to be specified by the keywords ELAS or ELAS_FO.

### 8.16 Factor keywords ENDO_SCALAIRE/ENDO_SCALAIRE_FO

Definition of the parameters of the constitutive law ENDO_SCALAIRE [R5.03.25](doc:r5.03.25), which describes the brittle elastic fracture of a homogeneous isotropic material. This law is available only for the damage-gradient modelling GRAD_VARI.

#### 8.16.1 Operands K, P, M

These are the internal parameters of the model which define the hardening, see [R5.03.25](doc:r5.03.25): $k$ denotes an energy density $\mathrm{Pa}$ , $k$ and $m$ are dimensionless parameters. $k$ and $m$ may be fitted from the non-local scale $D$ (approximately the half-width of the localisation band) and from the following macroscopic parameters: $E$ Young's modulus, ${G}_{f}$ the cracking energy and ${f}_{t}$ the value of the stress at the peak in simple tension. The fitting relations are then written:


$k=\frac{3{G}_{f}}{4D};\phantom{\rule{4em}{0ex}}m=\frac{3E{G}_{f}}{2{f}_{t}^{2}D};\phantom{\rule{4em}{0ex}}c=\frac{3}{8}D{G}_{f}$


where $c$ is the parameter entered by NON_LOCAL = _F(C_GRAD_VARI = c), which also depends on the macroscopic response. As for the parameter $p$ , greater than 1, it controls the curvature of the post-peak response.

#### 8.16.2 Operands C_COMP, C_VOLU

These are the internal parameters of the model, dimensionless, which define the shape of the loading surface (to within a homothety), see [R5.03.25](doc:r5.03.25). The default values make it possible to recover the (symmetric) energy model for which the loading surface corresponds to a level line of the elastic energy density (ellipsoid of revolution about the axis $(1,1,1)$ which is centred at the origin of coordinates).

In the more general case the ellipsoidal loading surface (still the axis $1,1,1$ ), non-centred, may be defined by three parameters more accessible to measurement: ${f}_{t}$ the value of the stress at the peak in simple tension, ${f}_{c}$ the value of the stress at the peak in simple compression and $\tau$ the value of the stress at the peak in pure shear. The fitting relations are the following:


${c}_{\mathit{comp}}=\frac{1+\nu }{1-2\nu }\frac{({f}_{c}-{f}_{t})\tau \sqrt{3}}{2{f}_{t}{f}_{c}};\phantom{\rule{6em}{0ex}}{c}_{\mathit{volu}}=\frac{2(1+\nu )}{1-2\nu }\left[{\left(\frac{({f}_{c}+{f}_{t})\tau \sqrt{3}}{2{f}_{t}{f}_{c}}\right)}^{2}-1\right]$


#### 8.16.3 Operands COEF_RIGI_MINI


```text
COEF_RIGI_MINI
```


It is the regularisation parameter of the tangent matrix at fracture, in order to avoid zero pivots if the cracking were to cut the part into several pieces not held by the boundary conditions. It does not depend on the command variables.
