Young's modulus $E$ and Poisson's ratio $\nu$ are to be specified by the keywords ELAS or ELAS_FO.

The non-locality parameter is entered under the keyword C_GRAD_VARI behind the factor keyword NON_LOCAL. It is related to the macroscopic parameters by:

### 8.17 Factor keyword ENDO_FISS_EXP/ENDO_FISS_EXP_FO

Definition of the parameters of the constitutive law ENDO_FISS_EXP [R5.03.27](doc:r5.03.27), which describes the brittle elastic fracture of a homogeneous isotropic material. This law is available only for the damage-gradient modelling GRAD_VARI.

#### 8.17.1 Operands K, M, P, Q

These are the internal parameters of the model which define the hardening, see [R5.03.27](doc:r5.03.27). Their identification is taken care of by the command DEFI_MATER_GC [U4.42.07](doc:u4.42.07), from quantities accessible experimentally.

#### 8.17.2 Operands TAU, SIG0, BETA

These are the internal parameters of the model which define the shape of the loading surface (to within a homothety), see [R5.03.27](doc:r5.03.27). It relies on the Von Mises stress and the exponential of the stress tensor and compares well with experimental results on concrete in biaxial loading. The parameter BETA is of a more numerical nature and its only interest is to make the elasticity domain bounded, including for hydrostatic compressions; the default value fulfils this role well, with no effect on the shape of the domain in the zones of interest.

Here too, the command DEFI_MATER_GC [U4.42.07](doc:u4.42.07) makes it possible to identify these parameters from quantities accessible experimentally (limits in tension and in compression).

#### 8.17.3 Operand REST_RIGIDITE

Stiffness restoration is active for the directions of strain in compression. In order to avoid an abrupt change of regime on passing from tension to compression, a function S' regularises the jump in stiffness, see [R5.03.27](doc:r5.03.27). The parameter REST_RIGIDITE, positive, controls this regularisation; it corresponds to the coefficient γ of the function S'. A value of 0 leads to not restoring the stiffness (i.e. the model is without stiffness restoration) whereas a very large value amounts to practically dispensing with the regularisation. The command DEFI_MATER_GC [U4.42.07](doc:u4.42.07) facilitates the quantification of this parameter by stipulating what proportion of the stiffness is restored for a strain level corresponding to the initial threshold in compression.

#### 8.17.4 Operands COEF_RIGI_MINI

It is the regularisation parameter of the tangent matrix at fracture, in order to avoid zero pivots if the cracking were to cut the part into several pieces not held by the boundary conditions. It does not depend on the command variables.

Young's modulus $E$ and Poisson's ratio $\nu$ are to be specified by the keywords ELAS or ELAS_FO.

The non-locality parameter is entered under the keyword C_GRAD_VARI behind the factor keyword NON_LOCAL; the command DEFI_MATER_GC [U4.42.07](doc:u4.42.07) makes it possible to identify it from quantities accessible experimentally.

### 8.18 Factor keyword GLRC_DM

This factor keyword makes it possible to define the parameters of the constitutive law GLRC_DM. It is a model of global damage of a reinforced-concrete slab formulated in terms of generalised strain/stress relations (membrane extension, bending and membrane force, bending moment), cf. [R7.01,32].

#### 8.18.1 Operands


```text
NYT = Nt
```


Membrane force of the damage threshold in simple tension of a reinforced-concrete slab (unit of force per length).


```text
NYC = Nc
```


Membrane force of the "damage" threshold (end of linearity of the compression curve) in simple compression of a reinforced-concrete slab (unit of force per length).


```text
MYF = Mf
```


Bending moment of the damage threshold in simple bending of a reinforced-concrete slab (unit of force).


```text
GAMMA_T = Gmt
```


Relative damaging slope with respect to the elastic slope in simple tension ($0<{\gamma}_{\mathrm{MT}}<1$ ).


```text
GAMMA_C = Gmc
```


Relative damaging slope with respect to the elastic slope in simple compression ($0<{\gamma}_{\mathit{MC}}<1$ ).


GAMMA_F = Gmf

Relative damaging slope with respect to the elastic slope in simple bending ($0<{\gamma}_{F}<1$ ).


```text
ALPHA_C     =     Alfc
```


Parameter modulating the damage function in compression in order to introduce a decoupling of the thresholds in tension and in compression and inducing a curvature of the compression curve. The damage function in membrane is written:

${\xi}_{m}(x,{d}_{1,}{d}_{2})=\frac{1}{2}((\frac{1+{\gamma}_{\mathrm{mt}}{d}_{1}}{1+{d}_{1}}+\frac{1+{\gamma}_{\mathrm{mt}}{d}_{2}}{1+{d}_{2}})H(x)+(\frac{{\alpha}_{c}+{\gamma}_{\mathrm{mc}}{d}_{1}}{{\alpha}_{c}+{d}_{1}}+\frac{{\alpha}_{c}+{\gamma}_{\mathrm{mc}}{d}_{2}}{{\alpha}_{c}+{d}_{2}})H(-x))$







One may refer to the reference documentation [R7.01.32](doc:r7.01.32) section §3.2.4 where a summary of the identification of the parameters of the model is set out.

### 8.19 Factor keyword DHRC

This factor keyword makes it possible to define the parameters of the constitutive law DHRC. It is a model of global damage of a reinforced-concrete slab formulated by means of a homogenisation method, in terms of generalised strain/stress relations (membrane extension, bending and membrane force, bending moment) and comprising internal state variables of damage and of slip at the steel–concrete interface, see [R7.01.36](doc:r7.01.36).

The $258$ parameters of the law to be identified, by homogenisation and by the least-squares method on various damage values, correspond:

- to the parameters controlling the components of the damageable elastic stiffness tensors $A$ , of coupling between generalised strains and slips $B$ and of energy stored in slip $C$ , for which one does not have an analytical expression available;
- to the macroscopic threshold parameters which are related to the parameters of the microscopic thresholds.

#### 8.19.1 Operands


```text
NYD = nyd
```


List of the two damage thresholds ${G}^{\zeta ,\mathit{crit}}$ in simple tension of the reinforced concrete


```text
SCRIT= scrit
```


List of the four equivalent steel–concrete slip thresholds ${\Sigma}_{\alpha}^{\zeta ,\mathit{crit}}$


```text
AA_C = alpha_Ac
```


Parameters ($42$ ) ${\alpha}^{\mathrm{Ac}}$ of the dependencies on damage variables of the components ($21$  supra-diagonal terms) of the symmetric tensor of order $4$ $A$ of membrane-bending of the plate, in the compression domain, in the reinforcement frame $(x,y)$ , in Voigt notation, identified by homogenisation and by the least-squares method on various values of ${D}_{\rho}$ , in the upper zone ($1$ ) then the lower zone ($2$ ):


${A}_{\beta \delta \tau \upsilon }^{\rho}({D}_{\rho})={A}_{\beta \delta \tau \upsilon }^{0}\frac{{\alpha}_{\beta \delta \tau \upsilon }^{\mathrm{Ac}\rho }+{\gamma}_{\beta \delta \tau \upsilon }^{\mathrm{Ac}\rho }{D}_{\rho}}{{\alpha}_{\beta \delta \tau \upsilon }^{\mathrm{Ac}\rho }+{D}_{\rho}}$


**Note:**

*In current practice, because of the isotropy of the concrete and of the orientation of the steels along the axes* $(x,y)$ *, one will have:*

AAC131= 1., AAC161= 1., AAC231= 1., AAC261= 1., AAC341= 1., AAC351= 1.,

AAC461= 1., AAC561= 1.; AAC132= 1., AAC162= 1., AAC232= 1., AAC262= 1.,

AAC342= 1., AAC352= 1., AAC462= 1., AAC562= 1..


```text
AA_T = alpha_At
```


Parameters ($42$ ) ${\alpha}^{\mathrm{At}}$ of the dependencies on damage variables of the components ($21$  supra-diagonal terms) of the symmetric tensor of order $4$ $A$ of membrane-bending of the plate, in the tension domain, in the reinforcement frame $(x,y)$ , in Voigt notation, identified by homogenisation and by the least-squares method on various values of ${D}_{\rho}$ , in the upper zone ($1$ ) then the lower zone ($2$ ):


${A}_{\beta \delta \tau \upsilon }^{\rho}({D}_{\rho})={A}_{\beta \delta \tau \upsilon }^{0}\frac{{\alpha}_{\beta \delta \tau \upsilon }^{\mathrm{At}\rho }+{\gamma}_{\beta \delta \tau \upsilon }^{\mathrm{At}\rho }{D}_{\rho}}{{\alpha}_{\beta \delta \tau \upsilon }^{\mathrm{At}\rho }+{D}_{\rho}}$


**Note:**

*In current practice, because of the isotropy of the concrete and of the orientation of the steels along the axes* $(x,y)$ *, one will take:*

AAT131= 1., AAT161= 1., AAT231= 1., AAT261= 1., AAT341= 1., AAT351= 1.,

AAT461= 1., AAT561= 1.; AAT132= 1., AAT162= 1., AAT232= 1., AAT262= 1.,

AAT342= 1., AAT352= 1., AAT462= 1., AAT562= 1..


```text
GA_C = gamma_Ac
```


Parameters ($42$ ) ${\gamma}^{\mathrm{Ac}}$ of the dependencies on damage variables of the components ($21$  supra-diagonal terms) of the symmetric tensor of order $4$ $A$ of membrane-bending of the plate, in the compression domain, in the reinforcement frame $(x,y)$ , in Voigt notation, identified by homogenisation and by the least-squares method on various values of ${D}_{\rho}$ , in the upper zone ($1$ ) then the lower zone ($2$ ).

**Note:**

*In current practice, because of the isotropy of the concrete and of the orientation of the steels along the axes* $(x,y)$ *, one will take:*

GAC131= 1., GAC161= 1., GAC231= 1., GAC261= 1., GAC341= 1., GAC351= 1.,

GAC461= 1., GAC561= 1.; GAC132= 1., GAC162= 1., GAC232= 1., GAC262= 1.,

GAC342= 1., GAC352= 1., GAC462= 1., GAC562= 1..


```text
GA_T = gamma_At
```


Parameters ($42$ ) ${\gamma}^{\mathrm{At}}$ of the dependencies on damage variables of the components ($21$  supra-diagonal terms) of the symmetric tensor of order $4$ $A$ of membrane-bending of the plate, in the tension domain, in the reinforcement frame $(x,y)$ , for the slips in the upper grid ($1$ ) or lower grid ($2$ ), in Voigt notation, identified by homogenisation and by the least-squares method on various values of ${D}_{\rho}$ , in the upper zone ($1$ ) then the lower zone ($2$ ).

**Note:**

*In current practice, because of the isotropy of the concrete and of the orientation of the steels along the axes* $(x,y)$ *, one will take:*

GAT131= 1., GAT161= 1., GAT231= 1., GAT261= 1., GAT341= 1., GAT351= 1.,

GAT461= 1., GAT561= 1.; GAT132= 1., GAT162= 1., GAT232= 1., GAT262= 1.,

GAT342= 1., GAT352= 1., GAT462= 1., GAT562= 1..


```text
AB = alpha_B
```


Parameters ($24$ ) ${\alpha}^{B}$ of the dependencies on damage variables of the components ($24$  supra-diagonal terms) of the symmetric tensor of order 3 $B$ of membrane-bending-slip coupling of the plate, in the reinforcement frame $(x,y)$ , for the slips in the upper grid ($1$ ) then the lower grid ($2$ ), in Voigt notation, identified by homogenisation and by the least-squares method on various values of ${D}_{\rho}$ :

with: ${B}_{\beta \delta \zeta }^{m\rho \pi }({D}_{\rho})=\frac{{\gamma}_{\beta \delta \zeta }^{Bm\rho \pi }{D}_{\rho}}{{\alpha}_{\beta \delta \zeta }^{Bm\rho \pi }+{D}_{\rho}}$ ; ${B}_{\beta \delta \zeta }^{f\rho \pi }({D}_{\rho})=\frac{{\gamma}_{\beta \delta \zeta }^{Bf\rho \pi }{D}_{\rho}}{{\alpha}_{\beta \delta \zeta }^{Bf\rho \pi }+{D}_{\rho}}$


**Note:**

*In current practice, because of the isotropy of the concrete and of the orientation of the steels along the axes* $(x,y)$ *, one will take:*

AB311= 1., AB321= 1., AB611= 1., AB621= 1.,

AB312= 1., AB322= 1., AB612= 1., AB622= 1.


```text
GB = gamma_B
```


Parameters (24) ${\gamma}^{B}$ of the dependencies on damage variables of the components ($24$  supra-diagonal terms) of the symmetric tensor of order $3$ $B$ of membrane-bending-slip coupling of the plate, in the reinforcement frame $(x,y)$ , for the slips in the upper grid ($1$ ) then the lower grid ($2$ ), in Voigt notation, identified by homogenisation and by the least-squares method on various values of ${D}_{\rho}$ .

**Note:**

*In current practice, because of the isotropy of the concrete and of the orientation of the steels along the axes* $(x,y)$ *, one will take:*

GB311= 0, GB321= 0, GB611= 0, GB621= 0,

GB312= 0, GB322= 0, GB612= 0, GB622= 0.


```text
C0 = C0
```


Components ($6$  non-zero supra-diagonal terms) of the symmetric tensor of order $2$ of free energy of steel–concrete slip ${C}^{0}$ of the plate before damage, along the directions of the slips considered, in the reinforcement frame $(x,y)$ , in the upper grid ($1$ ) or lower grid ($2$ ), in Voigt notation, identified by homogenisation:


$(\begin{array}{cccc}{C}_{xx}^{01}& {C}_{yx}^{01}& 0& 0\\ \text{}& {C}_{yy}^{01}& 0& 0\\ \text{}& \text{}& {C}_{xx}^{02}& {C}_{yx}^{02}\\ \text{}& \text{}& \text{}& {C}_{yy}^{02}\end{array})$


**Note:**

*In current practice, because of the isotropy of the concrete and of the orientation of the steels along the axes* $(x,y)$ *, one will have:* C0211= 0, C0212= 0.


```text
AC = alpha_C
```


Parameters ($6$ ) ${\alpha}^{C}$ of the dependencies on damage variables of the components ($6$  supra-diagonal terms) of the symmetric tensor $C$ identified by homogenisation and by the least-squares method on various values of ${D}_{\rho}$ :


${C}_{\beta \delta }^{\rho}({D}_{\rho})={C}_{\beta \delta }^{0\rho }\frac{{\alpha}_{\beta \delta }^{C\rho }+{\gamma}_{\beta \delta }^{C\rho }{D}_{\rho}}{{\alpha}_{\beta \delta }^{C\rho }+{D}_{\rho}}$


**Note:**

In current practice, because of the isotropy of the concrete and of the orientation of the steels along the axes $(x,y)$ , as: C0211= 0, C0212= 0, one will take: AC211= 1., AC212= 1..


```text
GC = gamma_C
```


Parameters ($6$ ) ${\gamma}^{C}$ of the dependencies on damage variables of the components ($6$  supra-diagonal terms) of the symmetric tensor $C$ identified by homogenisation and by the least-squares method on various values of ${D}_{\rho}$ .

**Note:**

In current practice, because of the isotropy of the concrete and of the orientation of the steels along the axes $(x,y)$ , as: C0211= 0, C0212= 0, one will take: GC211= 1., GC212= 1..

One may refer to the reference documentation [R7.01.36](doc:r7.01.36) where a summary of the identification of the parameters of the model is set out.

### 8.20 Factor keyword BETON_REGLE_PR

This keyword serves to define the material parameters used by the constitutive behaviour BETON_REGLE_PR ("Parabola–Rectangle" rule). This constitutive behaviour can be used only in 2D (plane stress or plane strain) or in shells (modellings DKT, COQUE_3D) (see for example the test ssnp129a). It reduces to a one-dimensional constitutive behaviour, which is written, in each of the principal directions of the 2D strain tensor:

- In tension:         $\lbrace \begin{array}{ccc}\sigma =E\varepsilon & \text{si}& 0<\epsilon <\frac{{\sigma}_{y}^{t}}{E}\\ \sigma ={\sigma}_{y}^{t}+{E}_{T}(\varepsilon -\frac{{\sigma}_{y}^{t}}{E})& \text{si}& \frac{{\sigma}_{y}^{t}}{E}<\varepsilon <\frac{{\sigma}_{y}^{t}}{E}(1-\frac{E}{{E}_{T}})\\ \sigma =0& \text{sinon}& \end{array}$

- In compression: $\lbrace \begin{array}{ccc}\sigma ={\sigma}_{y}^{c}\left[1-{(1-\frac{\varepsilon}{{\varepsilon}_{c}})}^{n}\right]& \text{si}& \varepsilon >{\varepsilon}_{c}\\ \sigma ={\sigma}_{y}^{c}& \text{sinon}& \end{array}$


#### 8.20.1 Operands


```text
DSIGM_EPSI     =     Et
```


Post-peak tangent modulus in tension ${E}_{t}$ (negative).


```text
SYT =    Syt
```


Ultimate stress in tension ${\sigma}_{y}^{t}$ .


```text
SYC =    Syc
```


Ultimate stress in compression ${\sigma}_{y}^{c}$ . It must be given positive.


```text
EPSC = Epsc
```


Ultimate strain in compression ${\varepsilon}_{c}$ . It must be given positive.


```text
N =    n

Exposant de la loi d’écrouissage en compression.
```



### 8.21 Keyword JOINT_BA

These parameters define the non-linear constitutive model of the steel–concrete bond; it is used for the fine analysis of reinforced-concrete structures where the prediction of the cracks and the redistribution of the stresses in the concrete are very important. Available for analyses under the effect of monotonic and cyclic loadings, the model is written within the framework of the thermodynamic formulation of irreversible processes. It makes it possible to take into account the damage of the interface in shear, in combination with the effects of the friction of the cracks, as well as the irreversible strains. The document [R7.01.21](doc:r7.01.21) describes the corresponding details.

This model must be used with the "joint" elements in 2D [R3.06.09](doc:r3.06.09). The steel reinforcements may be modelled with plane elements (QUAD4) or one-dimensional elements (BARRE).

**Note:**

*The taking into account of the effect of a thermal loading is not possible for the moment.*

#### 8.21.1 Operands


```text
HPEN    =    HPEN
```


Penetration parameter between surfaces by crushing of the concrete.

One checks that:math:mathrm{HPEN}>0..


```text
GTT     =    GTT
```


Stiffness modulus of the bond.

One checks that:math:{text{G}}_{mathit{beton}}le text{GTT}le {text{G}}_{mathit{acier}}.


```text
GAMD0    =     Gam0
```


Threshold of perfect bond or elastic-strain limit.

One checks that:math:1.E-4<mathrm{Gam0}<1.E-2.


```text
AD1        =    ad1
```


Parameter of the evolution of the damage in region 1 (passage from the small strains to the large slips).

One checks that:math:1.E-1<mathrm{AD1}<1.E+1.


```text
BD1        =    bd1
```


Power parameter describing the evolution of the damage variable in region 1 (passage from the small strains to the large slips).

One checks that:math:mathrm{BD1}<1.E-1.


```text
GAMD2    =    Gam2
```


Threshold of the large slips.

One checks that:math:1.E-4<mathrm{Gam2}<1.E+0.


```text
AD2        =    ad2
```


Parameter of the evolution of the damage in region 2 (maximum strength of the bond and degradation in friction).


```text
On vérifie que:math:`\mathrm{AD2}<1.E-6`.
```



```text
BD2        =    bd2
```


Power parameter describing the evolution of the damage variable in region 2 (maximum strength of the bond and degradation in friction).

One checks that:math:mathrm{BD2}<1.E-1.


```text
VIFROT    =    vifrot
```


Material parameter describing the influence of the friction of the cracks.

One checks that:math:mathrm{VIFROT}<0.0E+0.


```text
FA        =    alpha
```


Material parameter related to the kinematic hardening by friction of the cracks.

One checks that:math:mathrm{FA}<0.0E+0.


```text
FC     =    c
```


Parameter describing the influence of the confinement on the strength of the bond.

One checks that:math:mathrm{FC}<0.0E+0.


```text
EPSTR0    =     EPSN
```


Threshold of elastic strain in the normal direction before fracture. One checks that $1.E-4<\mathrm{EPSN}<1.E+0$ .


```text
ADN        =    adn
```


Parameter of the damage in the normal direction by opening of the crack.

One checks that:math:mathrm{ADN}<1.E-10.


```text
BDN        =    bdn
```


Power parameter describing the evolution of the damage variable in the normal direction.

One checks that:math:mathrm{BDN}<1.E-1.

### 8.22 Keyword BETON_RAG

This model is used to estimate the long-term constitutive behaviour of structures affected by the alkali–aggregate reaction [R7.01.26](doc:r7.01.26). It makes it possible to evaluate the strains and the anisotropic damage (cracking) of the affected structures. It comprises a Rankine criterion in tension and a Drücker–Prager criterion in compression. The two criteria are associated with an evolution law leading to a softening constitutive behaviour.

#### 8.22.1 Operands

##### 8.22.1.1 Operands related to the damage model

♦ ENDO_MC

Brittleness parameter of the concrete in compression.

♦ ENDO_MT

Brittleness parameter of the concrete in tension.

♦ ENDO_SIGUC

Equivalent stress of the concrete in compression. The unit of this parameter is homogeneous to a stress.

♦ ENDO_SIGUT

Equivalent stress of the concrete in tension. The unit of this parameter is homogeneous to a stress.

♦ ENDO_DRUPRA

This term is a characteristic of the compression criterion. It corresponds to the angle in **radians** of the Drucker–Prager criterion.

##### 8.22.1.2 Operands related to the creep model

♦ FLUA_SPH_KR

Delayed bulk modulus for the reversible spherical creep. The unit of this parameter is homogeneous to a stress.

♦ FLUA_SPH_NR

Viscosity coefficient for the reversible spherical creep. The unit of this parameter is homogeneous to a stress*time.

♦ FLUA_SPH_NI

Viscosity coefficient for the irreversible spherical creep. The unit of this parameter is homogeneous to a stress*time.

♦ FLUA_DEV_KR

Delayed bulk modulus for the reversible deviatoric creep. The unit of this parameter is homogeneous to a stress.

♦ FLUA_DEV_NR

Viscosity coefficient for the reversible deviatoric creep. The unit of this parameter is homogeneous to a stress*time.

♦ FLUA_DEV_NI

Viscosity coefficient for the irreversible deviatoric creep. The unit of this parameter is homogeneous to a stress*time.

◊ FLUA_KAPPAI

Coefficient of exponential increase of the irreversible viscosity for the viscous non-linearity. The unit of this parameter is homogeneous to a strain. This parameter being optional, if it is not specified, the irreversible-creep viscosity is considered linear.

**Note:**

If FLUA_KAPPAI is not specified, then the viscosity is irremediably considered linear. Conversely, if it has been given, it must continue to appear in DEFI_MATERIAU for the restarts of the analysis.

##### 8.22.1.3 Operands related to the autogenous shrinkage and to the formation of the gel

♦ PW_A, PW_B

For the unsaturated media, these coefficients are those of Van Genuchten.

♦ GEL_MG

Elastic modulus of the gel. The unit of this parameter is homogeneous to a stress.

♦ GEL_BG

Biot coefficient of the gel.

♦ GEL_VG

Maximum volume of gel which can be created by the chemical reaction; it corresponds to the theoretical volume of gel created per unit volume of concrete kept in saturated conditions for an infinite time.

♦ GEL_A0

Extent from which the initial connected porosity is filled.

♦ GEL_ALPHA0

Progress kinetics identified at Tref.

♦ GEL_TREF

Reference temperature for GEL_ALPHA0, in the equation of the chemical progress. The unit of this parameter is homogeneous to degrees Celsius $[°C]$  and is converted into degrees Kelvin $[K]$  during the solution (addition of $273.15[°C]$ ).

♦ GEL_EAR

It is the ratio between the activation energy of the reaction and the perfect-gas constant. The unit of this parameter is homogeneous to degrees Kelvin $[K]$ . The universal perfect-gas constant is the product of Avogadro's number and Boltzmann's constant.

This product is worth $R\phantom{\rule{2em}{0ex}}=\phantom{\rule{2em}{0ex}}8,3144621\phantom{\rule{2em}{0ex}}[J].{[\mathit{mol}]}^{-1}.{[K]}^{-1}$ .

♦ GEL_SR0

Saturation threshold from which the evolution of the chemical reaction becomes possible.

♦ RAG_EPSI0

Viscous strain for the AAR.

### 8.23 Keyword BETON_BURGER

The creep model BETON_BURGER assumes a decomposition between the spherical and deviatoric components: the strains induced by the spherical stresses are purely spherical and the strains induced by the deviatoric stresses are purely deviatoric [R7.01.35](doc:r7.01.35). Moreover, the basic-creep strain is assumed proportional to the internal relative humidity:

Spherical part: ${\epsilon}^{s}=h\cdot f\left({\sigma}^{s}\right)$  and, deviatoric part: $\underline{\underline{{\epsilon}^{d}}}=h\cdot f\left(\stackrel{~}{\underline{\underline{\sigma}}}\right)$

where $h$ denotes the internal relative humidity.

The constitutive model BETON_BURGER is a model based on the model BETON_UMLV [R7.01.06](doc:r7.01.06) in order to describe the basic creep of concretes. It is particularly suited to multiaxial configurations by not presupposing the value of the creep Poisson's ratio. The evolutions introduced concern the taking into account of a creep consolidation expressed by a non-linear term on the long-term behaviour of the model. Moreover, the spherical and deviatoric parts are now built in an identical way, leaving the possibility of controlling the apparent creep Poisson's ratio.

The spherical and deviatoric parts are described by equivalent rheological chains, a so-called Burger chain. This model is initially built following a Kelvin–Voigt stage (reversible part) coupled in series to a Maxwell body (irreversible part).

The model also makes it possible to take into account the effect of the temperature on the creep strains via a law of Arrhenius type.

The elastic mechanical characteristics E and NU must be defined in parallel under the keyword ELAS. The values under the keywords ELAS will be compared with those entered under the keyword BETON_BURGER. If they are different, a fatal error will be emitted.

If the keyword ELAS is not entered, code_aster will do so automatically by taking the elastic characteristics of the keyword BETON_BURGER.

#### 8.23.1 Operands


```text
YoungModulus    =    E
```


$E$ Young's modulus. This operand is mandatory because of the use of MFront


```text
PoissonRatio    =    NU
```


$\nu$ Poisson's ratio. This operand is mandatory because of the use of MFront


```text
K_RS =    K_RS
```


${k}_{r}^{s}$ apparent stiffness associated with the reversible spherical part of the creep strains


```text
K_RD = K_RD
```


${k}_{r}^{d}$ apparent stiffness associated with the reversible deviatoric part of the creep strains


```text
ETA_RS    = ETA_RS
```


${\eta}_{r}^{s}$ apparent viscosity associated with the reversible spherical strains


```text
ETA_IS    = ETA_IS
```


${\eta}_{i}^{s}$ apparent viscosity associated with the irreversible spherical strains


```text
ETA_RD    = ETA_RD
```


${\eta}_{r}^{d}$ viscosity of the reversible deviatoric strains


```text
ETA_ID    = ETA_ID
```


${\eta}_{i}^{d}$ viscosity of the irreversible deviatoric strains


```text
KAPPA = KAPPA
```


$\kappa$ term affecting the long-term viscosity (${\eta}_{i}^{s}$ and ${\eta}_{i}^{d}$) of the material


> QSR_K = Eac/R


${E}_{\mathit{ac}}/R$ is in general worth $4700.K$ . ($R$  is the perfect-gas constant).


```text
TEMP_0_C = T0
```


Reference temperature in the Arrhenius law. The reference temperature ${T}_{0}$ is **in degrees Celsius** , and converted into kelvin during the solution.


```text
ETA_FD = ETA_FD
```


makes it possible to take into account the desiccation creep according to the law of Bazant.

Note:

*The desorption curve giving the hygrometry* $h$ *as a function of the water concentration* $C$ *must be entered under the keyword* *BETON_DESORP.*

### 8.24 Keyword BETON_AGEING

The model BETON_AGEING takes up to a large extent the assumptions of the model BETON_BURGER. It is nevertheless distinguished from it:

- by being a linear viscoelastic model,
- by taking into account an ageing effect on the viscosity of the Maxwell chain,
- by being implemented in a THHM framework.

This last characteristic entails in particular that the relative humidity is not a command variable (unlike the practice of a chained analysis). It is computed by means of the capillary pressure and the temperature, nodal unknowns in a THHM modelling (see [R7.01.11](doc:r7.01.11)).

The model BETON_AGEING is integrated in MFront, with no need to resort to internal variables. Its reference documentation is identical to that of the model BETON_BURGER ([R7.01.35](doc:r7.01.35)).

### 8.25 Keyword FLUA_PORO_BETON

This model is used to estimate the constitutive behaviour of structures subjected to delayed creep strains [R7.01.30]. The delayed strains of the solid skeleton are called basic creep strains. They may be permanent or reversible, depending on the underlying phenomena. In the model, the constitutive equations of basic creep are always used coupled to a plastic-strain model which ensures the compatibility of the stress field with the strength criteria. In practice, the basic-creep model involves two usual rheological modules: a Kelvin–Voigt stage for the reversible viscoelastic creep and a plastic Maxwell stage for the permanent creep. The effects of the pore pressure are considered through the poromechanical framework.

The units of the material parameters are given in R7.01.30.

#### 8.25.1 Operands

##### 8.25.1.1 Operands related to the mechanical constitutive behaviour

♦ RT

Strength in tension.

♦ RC

Strength in compression.

♦ DELT

Coefficient of taking into account of the confinement.

##### 8.25.1.2 Operands related to the creep model

♦ TREF

Reference temperature for the creep.

♦ TAUK

Characteristic time of the Kelvin module.

♦ YKSY

Ratio: Kelvin stiffness / Young's modulus.

♦ TAUM

Characteristic time of the Maxwell module.

♦ EKFL

Characteristic strain of the creep potential.

♦ XFLU

Non-linearity coefficient.

♦ NRJM

Activation energy of creep.

♦ DFMX

Creep damage.

##### 8.25.1.3 Operands related to shrinkage/creep in desiccation

♦ PORO

Porosity of the material.

◊ MSHR

Van Genuchten modulus.

◊ MVGN

Van Genuchten exponent.

◊ BSHR

Biot coefficient of the capillary pressure.

◊ SFLD

Characteristic desiccation stress.

##### 8.25.1.4 Operands related to the thermal damage

♦ DT80

Damage at 80°C.

♦ TSTH

Threshold temperature for the thermal damage.

##### 8.25.1.5 Operands related to the hydration

♦ HYDR

Extent of hydration.

♦ HYDS

Solidification threshold of the extent.

### 8.26 Keyword ENDO_PORO_BETON

This model is used to estimate the constitutive behaviour of structures subjected to damage of mechanical origin (in tension, compression or shear) or thermal [R7.01.30]. The cracking of the concrete is described there by means of a non-linear model coupling plastic criteria and damages, all anisotropic. These damages make the link between the total stress and the effective stress in the sense of damage theory. In tension, the Rankine criteria make it possible to reproduce the localised structural macro-cracks. In compression, the Drucker–Prager criterion makes it possible to establish the shear damage, in connection with the dilatancy arising from the non-associated flow based on the Drucker–Prager criterion.

The units of the material parameters are given in R7.01.30.

#### 8.26.1 Operands

##### 8.26.1.1 Operands related to the mechanical constitutive behaviour

♦ RT

Strength in tension.

♦ EPT

Strain at the tension peak.

♦ RC

Strength in compression.

♦ EPC

Strain at the compression peak.

♦ DELT

Coefficient of taking into account of the confinement.

♦ BETA

Dilatancy for the non-associated plastic flow of Drucker–Prager.

♦ REF

Crack-reclosing stress.

♦ EKDC

Characteristic plastic strain of the Drucker–Prager damage.

♦ GFT

Cracking energy in tension.

♦ GFR

Energy of reclosing of the tension cracks.

♦ DIM3

Size of the element in the 3rd direction (for the AXIS or 2D case).

##### 8.26.1.2 Operands related to the thermal damage

♦ DT80

Damage at 80°C.

♦ TSTH

Threshold temperature for the thermal damage.

##### 8.26.1.3 Operands related to the hydration

♦ HYDR

Extent of hydration.

♦ HYDS

Solidification threshold of the extent.

### 8.27 Keyword FLUA_ENDO_PORO

This model is used to estimate the constitutive behaviour of structures subjected to delayed creep strains and to damage of mechanical origin (in tension, compression or shear) or thermal in a coupled manner [R7.01.30](doc:r7.01.30). This law takes up the constitutive laws FLUA_PORO_BETON and ENDO_PORO_BETON, adding to them couplings between the physical phenomena.

The units of the material parameters are given in R7.01.30.

#### 8.27.1 Operands

##### 8.27.1.1 Operands related to the mechanical constitutive behaviour

♦ RC

♦ EPC

Strain at the compression peak.

♦ DELT

Coefficient of taking into account of the confinement.

♦ BETA

Dilatancy for the non-associated plastic flow of Drucker–Prager.

♦ REF

Crack-reclosing stress.

♦ EKDC

Characteristic plastic strain of the Drucker–Prager damage.

♦ GFT

Cracking energy in tension.

♦ GFR

Energy of reclosing of the tension cracks.

♦ DIM3

Size of the element in the 3rd direction (for the AXIS or 2D case).

##### 8.27.1.2 Operands related to the creep model

♦ TREF

Reference temperature for the creep.

♦ TAUK

Ratio: Kelvin stiffness / Young's modulus.

♦ TAUM

##### 8.27.1.5 Operands related to the hydration

### 8.28 Keyword RGI_BETON

This model is used to estimate the constitutive behaviour of structures subjected to internal swelling reactions (ISR), whether they come from the alkali–aggregate reaction (AAR) or from the DEF (delayed ettringite formation) arising from the ISR (internal sulphate reaction) [R7.01.30](doc:r7.01.30). The volume of expansive product created and computed by chemical models makes it possible to compute the pore pressure which, combined with the external loading, makes it possible to evaluate the diffuse anisotropic cracking by means of anisotropic cracking criteria and hardening laws. It should be noted that the whole of the phenomena and of the equations of the law FLUA_ENDO_BETON and therefore a fortiori of FLUA_PORO_BETON and of ENDO_PORO_BETON are reused in RGI_BETON. Indeed, the proper taking into account of the swelling phenomena requires the taking into account of creep and of damage in a coupled manner.

The units of the material parameters are given in R7.01.30.

#### 8.28.1 Operands

##### 8.28.1.1 Operands related to the alkali–aggregate reaction (AAR)

♦ VRAG

Maximum volume ratio of AAR.

♦ TAUG

Characteristic time of AAR.

♦ SRSG

♦ TRAG

Reference temperature at which the test is carried out.

♦ NRJG

Activation energy for the kinetics of the AAR.

##### 8.28.1.2 Operands related to the internal sulphate reaction (ISR)

♦ NRJD

♦ TTRD

♦ TDID

♦ TFID

♦ CNAD

♦ CNAK

♦ CNAB

Concentration in alkalis blocking the precipitation of DEF.

♦ EXND

Exponent of the dissolution–temperature coupling.

♦ EXMD

Exponent of the coupling precipitation–fixation of the aluminiums.

♦ TTKF

Threshold temperature of fixation of the aluminiums in hydrogarnets.

♦ NRJF

Energy of fixation of the aluminiums in hydrogarnets.

##### 8.28.1.3 Operands related to the internal swelling reactions (ISR)

♦ KRGI

♦ EKRG

◊ ALAT

◊ HPEV

Multiplicative coefficient of the hardening of the tensile strength for a large swelling of ISR (>0.4%).

♦ KGEL

### 8.29 Keyword RGI_BETON_BA

This model is an extension of the law RGI_BETON integrating reinforcements in order to model the constitutive behaviour of reinforced concrete. It takes up the same parameters as well as additional parameters for the definition of the reinforcements. The definitions of the parameters common to RGI_BETON are not given again.

#### 8.29.1 Operands

##### 8.29.1.1 Global operands

♦ NREN

Number of reinforcements (between 1 and 5)

♦ YOUM

Young's modulus of the concrete alone.

♦ NUM

Poisson's ratio of the concrete alone.

##### 8.29.1.2 Operands related to a given reinforcement

The following operands exist for i from 1 to 5. The parameters without default values are mandatory if NREN is greater than or equal to i.

◊ ROAi

Density of reinforcement i.

◊Ei

Young's modulus of reinforcement i.

◊SYi

Elastic limit of reinforcement i.

◊TYRi

Stress at the steel/concrete interface of reinforcement i.

◊VRi1

x-coordinate of the direction of reinforcement i.

◊VRi2

y-coordinate of the direction of reinforcement i.

◊VRi3

z-coordinate of the direction of reinforcement i.

◊D_SIGM_EPSI i

Hardening modulus of reinforcement i.

◊TAUKi

Reversible characteristic time for the Kelvin creep of reinforcement i.

◊TAUMi

Irreversible characteristic time for the Maxwell creep of reinforcement i.

◊EKRi

Characteristic strain for the relaxation of reinforcement i.

◊SKRi

Characteristic stress for the fitting of EKRi. (The default value is in MPa)

◊ATRi

Reference value for the thermal-activation coefficient

◊CTMi

Coupling coefficient for the impact of the stresses on the activation energy

◊XFLi

Exponent for the thermal activation of the relaxation

◊PREi

Initial prestress (imposed as initial stress at the first step)

◊TTRi

Reference temperature for the fitting of TAUMi (°C)

◊XNRi

Non-linear relaxation coefficient

◊MUSi

Threshold beyond which the thermal activation becomes a function of the load ratio

◊YKYi

Coefficient of reduction of reversible relaxation evaluated at TTRi

### 8.30 Factor keyword ENDO_LOCA_EXP / ENDO_LOCA_EXP_FO

Definition of the parameters of the constitutive law ENDO_LOCA_EXP [R7.01.42](doc:r7.01.42), which describes the homogeneous cracking of a concrete structure via a local quasi-brittle isotropic damage model.

#### 8.30.1 Operands KAPPA and P

These are the internal parameters of the model which define the hardening, see [R7.01.42](doc:r7.01.42). Their identification is taken care of by the command DEFI_MATER_GC [U4.42.07](doc:u4.42.07), from quantities more accessible experimentally.

#### 8.30.2 Operands SIGC, SIG0, BETA0

These are the internal parameters of the model which define the shape of the loading surface (to within a homothety), see [R7.01.42](doc:r7.01.42). It relies on the Von Mises stress and the exponential of the stress tensor and compares well with experimental results on concrete in biaxial loading. The parameter BETA0 is of a more numerical nature and its only interest is to make the elasticity domain bounded, including for hydrostatic compressions; the default value fulfils this role well, with no effect on the shape of the domain in the zones of interest.

Here too, the command DEFI_MATER_GC [U4.42.07](doc:u4.42.07) makes it possible to identify these parameters from quantities accessible experimentally (limits in tension and in compression).

#### 8.30.3 Operand REST_RIGIDITE

Stiffness restoration is active for the directions of strain in compression. In order to avoid an abrupt change of regime on passing from tension to compression, a function S' regularises the jump in stiffness, see [R7.01.42](doc:r7.01.42). The parameter REST_RIGIDITE, positive, controls this regularisation; it corresponds to the coefficient γ of the function S'. A value of 0 leads to not restoring the stiffness (i.e. the model is without stiffness restoration) whereas a very large value amounts to practically dispensing with the regularisation. The command DEFI_MATER_GC [U4.42.07](doc:u4.42.07) facilitates the quantification of this parameter by stipulating what proportion of the stiffness is restored for a strain level corresponding to the initial threshold in compression.

### 8.31 Factor keyword ENDO_LOCA_TC / ENDO_LOCA_TC_FO

Definition of the parameters of the constitutive law ENDO_LOCA_TC [R7.01.47](doc:r7.01.47). It takes up the fundamentals of the models ENDO_ISOT_BETON [R7.01.04](doc:r7.01.04) and ENDO_LOCA_EXP [R7.01.42](doc:r7.01.42) and enriches them by introducing:

- a rationalised damage threshold, with a view to optimising numerical robustness;
- an effect of the damage in compression, so as to bound the stresses there, without however seeking to model the post-peak phase. The threshold stress in compression tends monotonically towards a limit, the compressive strength. which describes the homogeneous cracking of a concrete structure via a local quasi-brittle isotropic damage model.

#### 8.31.1 Operands ENER_TRAC_RUPT_N and COEF_ECRO_TRAC

These are the internal parameters of the model which define the hardening (see [R7.01.47](doc:r7.01.47) ). ENER_TRAC_RUPT_N is the volumetric energy at fracture in confined tension, and COEF_ECRO_TRAC, called P in ENDO_LOCA_EXP ([R7.01.42](doc:r7.01.42)), is the hardening coefficient which makes it possible to define the post-peak behaviour in tension of the concrete. A value of COEF_ECRO_TRAC of -0.2 leads to quasi-linear (negative) hardening, whereas values between 1 and 4 are more representative of the hollowing of the tensile curves. In the absence of a tensile curve with which to identify this parameter, a recommended value is 1.5.

It is possible to use DEFI_MATER_GC ([U4.42.07](doc:u4.42.07)) in order to determine the values of the parameters of ENDO_LOCA_TC, by means of characteristic data of the concrete or via the recommendations of the FIB Model Code.

#### 8.31.2 Operands FC, FT, SIGM_COMP_SEUIL

These are the internal parameters of the model which define the shape of the loading surface (to within a homothety), see [R7.01.47](doc:r7.01.47).
FC is the maximum stress reached in compression (peak stress with $FC > 2*Ft$).
FT is the tensile limit of the concrete ($FT > 0$).
SIGM_COMP_SEUIL corresponds to the stress of loss of linearity of the response of the concrete. It is denoted in the reference notes $\sigma^{0}$. ($2\,f_{t} \leq \sigma^{0} < f_{c}$)

#### 8.31.3 Operand TAU_REGU_VISC

This parameter makes it possible to introduce viscosity in order to facilitate convergence. Its use requires an advanced knowledge of modelling. It is optional and, by default, it is not entered. For further information, one should refer to [R7.01.47](doc:r7.01.47).
## 9 Metallurgical-mechanical constitutive behaviours

For the metallurgical constitutive behaviour (cf. [R4.04.01](doc:r4.04.01)), three constitutive laws
are available: a law characteristic of the metallurgical transformations of steel, of
steel with tempering phases, and a law characteristic of zirconium alloys.

Steel may comprise (at most) seven different metallurgical phases:

- four cold phases (denoted $\alpha_\alpha$), which are ferrite, pearlite, bainite
and martensite
- one hot phase, austenite (denoted $\gamma$)
- two cold tempering phases, for bainite and for martensite

Zircaloy may comprise (at most) three different metallurgical phases:

- Two cold phases: a pure $\alpha$ phase and a mixed $\alpha$ phase
- One hot $\beta$ phase

For the mechanical constitutive behaviour taking the metallurgical transformations into account, there
exist two models.

The first model (cf. [R4.04.02](doc:r4.04.02)) can be used for steel and for
Zircaloy. The desired material is chosen by activating, in the factor keyword
[COMPORTEMENT](doc:u4.54.11) of the non-linear operators, the keyword RELATION_KIT, which takes the value
ACIER or ZIRC. The various relations relating to this model are identical for these
two materials (the same phenomena are treated) but the number of phases
present is different.

The second model (cf. [R4.04.05](doc:r4.04.05)) is available only for Zircaloy
(`RELATION_KIT=’ZIRC’`) and corresponds to the keyword META_LEMA_ANI under [COMPORTEMENT](doc:u4.54.11).

### 9.1 Factor keyword META_ACIER

Parameters to be supplied for the metallurgy of steel.

#### 9.1.1 Parameters for phase changes

TRC is a concept of type table produced by the operator [DEFI_TRC](doc:u4.43.04)
and containing all of the information supplied by the CCT diagrams (Continuous
Cooling Transformation) of the steel under consideration.

AR3 is the quasi-static temperature of the start of decomposition of austenite on cooling.

**Note**

One must be consistent between this value and the value corresponding to the cooling curve
given by the command [DEFI_TRC](doc:u4.43.04) . Beyond a difference of 10°C, an
alarm is issued and it is considered that the results could be wrong.

ALPHA is the coefficient $\alpha$ of the Koïstinen-Marbürger law expressing the quantity
of martensite formed as a function of temperature

$$
{Z}_{\alpha_{m}}=1-\exp(\alpha ({M}_{s}-T))
$$

MSO is the temperature of the start of the martensitic transformation ${M}_{s,0}$ when
the latter is total. In this case ${M}_{s}={M}_{s0}$.

AC1 is the quasi-static temperature of the start of transformation into austenite on heating.

AC3 is the quasi-static temperature of the end of transformation into austenite.

TAUX_1 is the value of the "delay" function (cf. [R4.04.01](doc:r4.04.01))
$\tau_1(T)$ appearing in the model of austenitic transformation at the temperature AC1.

TAUX_3 is the value of the "delay" function (cf. [R4.04.01](doc:r4.04.01))
$\tau_3(T)$ appearing in the model of austenitic transformation at the temperature AC3.

The evolution of the proportion of austenite is then defined by:

$$
\dot{Z}=\frac{Z-{Z}_{\text{eq}}\left(T\right)}{\tau \left(T\right)}
$$

with ${Z}_{\text{eq}}\left(T\right)$ which therefore has the shape of [Curve].

![Figure](/figures/u4.43.01/Forme1.gif)

*Curve ${Z}_{\text{eq}}\left(T\right)$*

and $\tau \left(T\right)$  which has the shape of [Curve].

![Figure](/figures/u4.43.01/Forme2.gif)

*Curve $\tau \left(T\right)$*

#### 9.1.2 Parameters for the grain size

The following four parameters lead to the computation of the grain size if they are supplied:

$$
\frac{d}{{dt}}(d)=\frac{1}{\lambda}\left (\frac{1}{d}-\frac{1}{{d}_{\lim}} \right)
$$

with $\lambda ={\lambda}_{0}\cdot \exp \left(\frac{{Q}_{\text{app}}}{R T} \right )$ and
${d}_{\lim}={d}_{10}\exp \left (-\frac{{W}_{\text{app}}}{R T} \right )$ .

LAMBDA0 is the material parameter ${\lambda}_{0}$

QSR_K is the activation-energy parameter $\frac{{Q}_{\text{app}}}{R T}$

D10 is the material parameter ${d}_{10}$

WSR_K is the activation-energy parameter $\frac{{W}_{\text{app}}}{R T}$

### 9.2 Factor keyword META_ACIER_REVENU

Parameters to be supplied for the metallurgy of steel with its tempering phases.

#### 9.2.1 Parameters for phase changes

A law of Johnson-Mehl-Avrami type is considered in order to describe the evolution of the percentage
of tempered phase ${\tilde{Z}}^{r}_{\alpha_{\alpha}}$ as a function of the holding time
$t^{*}$ at a temperature $T^{m}$ for the two tempering phases (bainite and martensite).

$$
{\tilde{Z}}^{r}_{\alpha_{\alpha}}(t^{*})=\left ( 1 - \exp\left\lbrace   -b  ({t^{*}})^n  \right\rbrace  \right )
$$

TEMP is the tempering temperature $T^{r}$ and TEMP_MAINTIEN is the holding temperature
$T^{m}$ appearing in the identification of the parameters.

BAINITE_B and BAINITE_N are the coefficients $b$ and $n$ for the case of bainite
${\alpha_{\alpha}}=b$.

MARTENSITE_B and MARTENSITE_N are the coefficients $b$ and $n$ for the case of
martensite ${\alpha_{\alpha}}=m$.

### 9.3 Factor keyword META_ZIRC

Parameters to be supplied for the metallurgy of Zircaloy (cf. [R4.04.04](doc:r4.04.04)).

```text
META_ZIRC = _F(
    ◆ TDEQ = float,
    ◆ N = float,
    ◆ K = float,
    ◆ T1C = float,
    ◆ T2C = float,
    ◆ AC = float,
    ◆ M = float,
    ◆ QSR_K = float,
    ◆ T1R = float,
    ◆ T2R = float,
    ◆ AR = float,
    ◆ BR = float,
)
```

- $\alpha$ : cold hexagonal close-packed phase
- $\beta$ : hot body-centred cubic phase

**It is recalled that the material contains two phases:**

TDEQ is the temperature of the start of the transformation $\alpha \iff \beta$ at equilibrium.

N and K are material parameters relating to the model giving the proportion of $\beta$ as
a function of temperature, at equilibrium.

T1C is the temperature of the start of the transformation of $\alpha$ into $\beta$ on heating.

T2C is a material parameter appearing in the computation of the temperature of the start of
the transformation of $\alpha$ into $\beta$ on heating.

AC and M are material parameters appearing in the model of evolution of
$\beta$ on heating.

T2R is a material parameter appearing in the computation of the temperature of the start of
the transformation of $\beta$ into $\alpha$ on cooling.

AR and BR are material parameters appearing in the model of evolution of
$\beta$ on cooling.

QSR_K is the Arrhenius constant expressed in degrees Kelvin.

### 9.4 Factor keyword DURT_META

Definition of the characteristics relating to the computation of the hardness associated with the metallurgy of steels.

The hardness is computed using a linear mixture law on the micro-hardness of the constituents:

The model is written $H_{V}=\sum_{k}{Z}_{k}{H_{V}}_{k}$ with $H_{V}$ the hardness (here
Vickers, for example) of the multiphase point, ${Z}_{k}$ the proportion of phase $k$ and
${H_{V}}_{k}$ the hardness of phase $k$ .

F1_DURT is the micro-hardness of the cold phase $\mathrm{F1}$ (ferrite for steel).

F2_DURT is the micro-hardness of the cold phase $\mathrm{F2}$ (pearlite for steel).

F3_DURT is the micro-hardness of the cold phase $\mathrm{F3}$ (bainite for steel).

F4_DURT is the micro-hardness of the cold phase $\mathrm{F4}$ (martensite for steel).

C_DURT is the micro-hardness for the hot phase (austenite for steel).

F3_REVENU_DURT is the micro-hardness of the cold *tempered* phase $\mathrm{F3}$ (bainite for steel).

F4_REVENU_DURT is the micro-hardness of the cold *tempered* phase $\mathrm{F4}$ (martensite for steel).

### 9.5 Factor keywords ELAS_META, ELAS_META_FO

Definition of the elastic characteristics, of expansion and of elastic limits for the modelling of a material undergoing metallurgical transformations (see [R4.04.02](doc:r4.04.02) or [R4.04.05](doc:r4.04.05)). These coefficients may either be constant with respect to temperature, ELAS_META, or depend on temperature, ELAS_META_FO (parameter ’TEMP’).

Some coefficients depend on the metallurgical structure (parameter ’META’).

**Note:**

Concerning the model META_LEMA_ANI, the thermal expansion is written in the classical way without distinction of the phases. Consequently, the keywords ‘C_ALPHA’, ‘PHASE_REFE’ and ‘EPSF_EPSC_TREF’ are mandatory but are not taken into account in the equations. Only the expansion coefficient ‘F_ALPHA’ is considered.

This model is a law without a threshold, so the elastic limits and the mixture law are not useful.

**Note:**

Concerning the other models, for a steel one supplies at most 5 elastic limits; for Zircaloy one supplies at most three.

#### 9.5.1 Operands

```text
E = young
```

Young's modulus, identical for all the metallurgical phases.

```text
NU = nu
```

Poisson's ratio, identical for all the metallurgical phases.

```text
F_ALPHA = fal
```

Mean coefficient of thermal expansion of the cold phases.

```text
C_ALPHA = cal
```

Mean coefficient of thermal expansion of the hot phase.

```text
PHASE_REFE    =    /    ’CHAUD’
                   /    ’FROID’
```

Choice of the reference metallurgical phase (hot phase or cold phase).

Indeed, in order to define the zero thermal strain, one must define the reference temperature ${T}_{\mathrm{ref}}$ (defined in AFFE_MATERIAU) and the reference metallurgical phase, so that the thermal strain is considered to be zero at ${T}_{\mathrm{ref}}$ and in the reference metallurgical state.

```text
EPSF_EPSC_TREF    =    deltae
```

Strain of the non-reference phase with respect to the reference phase at the temperature ${T}_{\mathrm{ref}}$ : it expresses the difference in compactness between the face-centred cubic crystallographic structures (austenitic type) and the body-centred cubic structures (ferritic type).

```text
TEMP_DEF_ALPHA    =    Tda
```

Temperature with respect to which the expansion coefficient is defined. In the case where C_ALPHA is a function, this operand is mandatory.

```text
PRECISION = eps
```

This real number indicates with what precision a temperature $T$ is close to the reference temperature (cf. [§3.1.4]).

```text
F1_SY    =    F1sy
```

Elastic limit of cold phase 1 for a plastic constitutive behaviour.

```text
F2_SY    =    F2sy
```

Elastic limit of cold phase 2 for a plastic constitutive behaviour.

```text
F3_SY    =    F3sy
```

Elastic limit of cold phase 3 for a plastic constitutive behaviour.

```text
F4_SY    =    F4sy
```

Elastic limit of cold phase 4 for a plastic constitutive behaviour.

```text
C_SY    =    Fsy
```

Elastic limit of the hot phase for a plastic constitutive behaviour.

```text
SY_MELANGE    =    f
```

Function used for the mixture law on the elastic limit of the multiphase material for a plastic constitutive behaviour.

${\sigma}_{y}=\left(1-f\left(z\right)\right){\sigma}_{y}^{\gamma}+f\left(z\right){\sigma}_{y}^{\alpha}$

```text
F1_S_VP    =    F1svp
```

Elastic limit of cold phase 1 for a viscous constitutive behaviour.

```text
F2_S_VP    =    F2svp
```

Elastic limit of cold phase 2 for a viscous constitutive behaviour.

```text
F3_S_VP    =    F3svp
```

Elastic limit of cold phase 3 for a viscous constitutive behaviour.

```text
F4_S_VP    =    F4svp
```

Elastic limit of cold phase 4 for a viscous constitutive behaviour.

```text
C_S_VP        =    Csvp
```

Elastic limit of the hot phase for a viscous constitutive behaviour.

```text
S_VP_MELANGE    =    Svp
```

Function used for the mixture law on the elastic limit of the multiphase material for a viscous constitutive behaviour.

${\sigma}_{c}=\left(1-f\left(z\right)\right){\sigma}_{c}^{\gamma}+f\left(z\right){\sigma}_{c}^{\alpha}$

### 9.6 Factor keyword META_ECRO_LINE

Definition of five hardening moduli used in the modelling of the phenomenon of linear isotropic hardening of a material undergoing metallurgical phase changes (see [R4.04.02](doc:r4.04.02)). These moduli depend on the temperature.

#### 9.6.1 Operands

```text
F1_D_SIGM_EPSI    =    dsde1
```

Slope of the tensile curve for cold phase 1.

```text
F2_D_SIGM_EPSI    =    dsde2
```

Slope of the tensile curve for cold phase 2.

```text
F3_D_SIGM_EPSI    =    dsde3
```

Slope of the tensile curve for cold phase 3.

```text
F4_D_SIGM_EPSI    =    dsde4
```

Slope of the tensile curve for cold phase 4.

```text
C_D_SIGM_EPSI        =    dsdec
```

Slope of the tensile curve for the hot phase.

![Figure](/figures/u4.43.01/10000000000002D6000001E90DCCCD948845BAFA.png)

Young's modulus $E$ is to be specified by the keywords META_ELAS or META_ELAS_FO.

### 9.7 Factor keyword META_TRACTION

Definition of five tensile curves used in the modelling of the phenomenon of non-linear isotropic hardening of a material undergoing metallurgical phase changes (see [R4.04.02](doc:r4.04.02)). The tensile curves may possibly depend on the temperature.

#### 9.7.1 Operands

```text
SIGM_F1    =    r_p1
```

Isotropic hardening curve $R$ as a function of the cumulative plastic strain $p$ for cold phase 1 .

```text
SIGM_F2    =    r_p2
```

Isotropic hardening curve $R$ as a function of the cumulative plastic strain $p$ for cold phase 2 .

```text
SIGM_F3    =    r_p3
```

Isotropic hardening curve $R$ as a function of the cumulative plastic strain $p$ for cold phase 3 .

```text
SIGM_F4    =    r_p4
```

Isotropic hardening curve $R$ as a function of the cumulative plastic strain $p$ for cold phase 4 .

```text
SIGM_C        =    r_p c
```

Isotropic hardening curve $R$ as a function of the cumulative plastic strain $p$  for the hot phase.

**Note:**

*Take care, this is not the curve* $\sigma$ *as a function of* $\varepsilon$ *but the curve* $R$ *as a function of* $p$ *. One passes from one to the other by carrying out the following computations:*

> $R=\sigma -\mathrm{limite}d’\mathrm{élasticité},p=\varepsilon –(\sigma /E).$

### 9.8 Factor keyword META_VISC_FO

Definition of the viscous parameters of the viscoplastic constitutive law with metallurgy taken into account (see [R4.04.02](doc:r4.04.02)). The viscoplastic model of Norton-Hoff type comprises 5 parameters; the classical parameters $\eta$ , $n$ of the power flow law, the elastic limit of viscous flow, and the parameters $C$ and $m$ relating to the recovery of hardening of viscous origin. These parameters depend on the temperature and on the metallurgical structure.

The elastic-limit parameters are defined in ELAS_META.

#### 9.8.1 Operands F1_ETA/F2_ETA/F3_ETA/F4_ETA/C_ETA

```text
F1_ETA        =    eta1
```

Parameter $\eta$ of the viscoplastic flow law, for cold phase 1.

```text
F2_ETA        =    eta2
```

Parameter $\eta$ of the viscoplastic flow law, for cold phase 2.

```text
F3_ETA    =    eta3
```

Parameter $\eta$ of the viscoplastic flow law, for cold phase 3.

```text
F4_ETA        =    eta4
```

Parameter $\eta$ of the viscoplastic flow law, for cold phase 4.

```text
C_ETA    =    etac
```

Parameter $\eta$ of the viscoplastic flow law, for the hot phase.

#### 9.8.2 Operands F1_N/F2_N/F3_N/F4_N/C_N

```text
F1_N    =    n1
```

Parameter $n$ of the viscoplastic flow law, for cold phase 1.

```text
F2_N    =    n2
```

Parameter $n$ of the viscoplastic flow law, for cold phase 2.

```text
F3_N    =    n3
```

Parameter $n$ of the viscoplastic flow law, for cold phase 3.

```text
F4_N    =    n4
```

Parameter $n$ of the viscoplastic flow law, for cold phase 4.

```text
C_N        =    n5
```

Parameter n of the viscoplastic flow law, for the hot phase.

#### 9.8.3 Operands F1_C/F2_C/F3_C/F4_C/C_C

```text
F1_C    =    C1
```

Parameter $C$ relating to the recovery of hardening of viscous origin, for cold phase 1.

```text
F2_C    =    C2
```

Parameter $C$ relating to the recovery of hardening of viscous origin, for cold phase 2.

```text
F3_C    =    C3
```

Parameter $C$ relating to the recovery of hardening of viscous origin, for cold phase 3.

```text
F4_C    =    C4
```

Parameter $C$ relating to the recovery of hardening of viscous origin, for cold phase 4.

```text
C_C        =    C5
```

Parameter $C$ relating to the recovery of hardening of viscous origin, for the hot phase.

#### 9.8.4 Operands F1_M/F2_M/F3_M/F4_M/C_M

```text
F1_M    =    m1
```

Parameter $m$ relating to the recovery of hardening of viscous origin, for cold phase 1.

```text
F2_M    =    m2
```

Parameter $m$ relating to the recovery of hardening of viscous origin, for cold phase 2.

```text
F3_M    =    m3
```

Parameter $m$ relating to the recovery of hardening of viscous origin, for cold phase 3.

```text
F4_M    =    m4
```

Parameter $m$ relating to the recovery of hardening of viscous origin, for cold phase 4.

```text
C_M        =    m5
```

Parameter $m$ relating to the recovery of hardening of viscous origin, for the hot phase.

### 9.9 Factor keyword META_PT

Definition of the characteristics used in the modelling of the transformation plasticity of a material which undergoes metallurgical phase changes (see [R4.04.02](doc:r4.04.02)).

The model is the following: $\Delta {\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{pt}}=\frac{3}{2}\sigma \sum_{i=1}^{i=4}{K}_{i}{F}_{i}^{'}({Z}_{i})\langle \Delta {Z}_{i}\rangle$

#### 9.9.1 Operands

```text
F1_K = Kf, F2_K = Kp, F3_K = Kb, F4_K = Km
```

Constants ${K}_{i}$ used in the transformation-plasticity model, for the various cold phases. For steel = ferritic, pearlitic, bainitic and martensitic phase.

```text
F1_D_F_META=F’f,    F2_D_F_META=F’p,    F3_D_F_META=F’b,                                                F4_D_F_META=F’m,
```

Functions ${F}_{i}^{'}$ used in the transformation-plasticity model, for the various cold phases. For steel: ferritic, pearlitic, bainitic and martensitic phase.

### 9.10 Factor keyword META_RE

Definition of the characteristics used in the modelling of the phenomenon of hardening recovery of a material which undergoes metallurgical phase changes (see [R4.04.02](doc:r4.04.02)).

#### 9.10.1 Operands

```text
C_F1_THETA=Tgf,    C_F2_THETA=Tgp,    C_F3_THETA=Tgb,                                                 C_F4_THETA=Tgm
```

Constants characterising the proportion of hardening transmitted during the transformation of the hot phase C into a cold phase. For steel; transformation of austenite into ferrite, pearlite, bainite and martensite. Thus, $\theta =0$ corresponds to total recovery and $\theta =1$ to total transmission of the hardening.

```text
F1_C_THETA=Tfg,    F2_C_THETA=Tpg,    F3_C_THETA=Tbg,                                                F4_C_THETA=Tmg
```

Constants characterising the proportion of hardening transmitted during the transformation of the cold phases into the hot phase. For steel; transformation of ferrite, of pearlite, of bainite and of martensite into austenite. Thus, $\theta =0$  corresponds to total recovery and $\theta =1$ to total transmission of the hardening.

### 9.11 Keyword META_LEMA_ANI

Definition of the parameters of the law META_LEMA_ANI (cf. [R4.04.05](doc:r4.04.05)), an elasto-viscous law without a threshold with an anisotropic constitutive behaviour. Briefly, the model is written either in the cylindrical frame $(r,\theta ,z)$ , or in the Cartesian frame $(\mathit{Ox},\mathit{Oy},\mathit{Oz})$ :

Partition of the strains: $\epsilon ={\epsilon}^{e}+\alpha \Delta T\mathrm{Id}+{\epsilon}^{v}$

Flow law of the viscous strain: $\dot{{\epsilon}^{v}}=\dot{p}\frac{M:\sigma }{{\sigma}_{\mathit{eq}}}$

Hill criterion: ${\sigma}_{\mathit{eq}}=\sqrt{\sigma :M:\sigma }$

Hill matrix $\mathrm{M}$ *in cylindrical coordinates* :

${\underline{M}}_{(r,\theta ,z)}=\left[\begin{array}{cccccc}{M}_{\mathit{rrrr}}& {M}_{\mathit{rr}\theta \theta }& {M}_{\mathit{rrzz}}& 0& 0& 0\\ {M}_{\mathit{rr}\theta \theta }& {M}_{\theta \theta \theta \theta }& {M}_{\theta \theta zz}& 0& 0& 0\\ {M}_{\mathit{rrzz}}& {M}_{\theta \theta zz}& {M}_{\mathit{zzzz}}& 0& 0& 0\\ 0& 0& 0& {M}_{r\theta r\theta }& 0& 0\\ 0& 0& 0& 0& {M}_{\mathit{rzrz}}& 0\\ 0& 0& 0& 0& 0& {M}_{\theta z\theta z}\end{array}\right]$

$\text{avec}\lbrace \begin{array}{ccc}{M}_{\mathit{rrrr}}+{M}_{\mathit{rr}\theta \theta }+{M}_{\mathit{rrzz}}& =& 0\\ {M}_{\mathit{rr}\theta \theta }+{M}_{\theta \theta \theta \theta }+{M}_{\theta \theta zz}& =& 0\\ {M}_{\mathit{rrzz}}+{M}_{\theta \theta zz}+{M}_{\mathit{zzzz}}& =& 0\end{array}$

or else, in Cartesian coordinates:

${\underline{M}}_{(x,y,z)}=\left[\begin{array}{cccccc}{M}_{\mathit{xxxx}}& {M}_{\mathit{xxyy}}& {M}_{\mathit{xxzz}}& 0& 0& 0\\ {M}_{\mathit{xxyy}}& {M}_{\mathit{yyyy}}& {M}_{\mathit{yyzz}}& 0& 0& 0\\ {M}_{\mathit{xxzz}}& {M}_{\mathit{yyzz}}& {M}_{\mathit{zzzz}}& 0& 0& 0\\ 0& 0& 0& {M}_{\mathit{xyxy}}& 0& 0\\ 0& 0& 0& 0& {M}_{\mathit{xzxz}}& 0\\ 0& 0& 0& 0& 0& {M}_{\mathit{yzyz}}\end{array}\right]$

$\text{avec}\lbrace \begin{array}{ccc}{M}_{\mathit{xxxx}}+{M}_{\mathit{xxyy}}+{M}_{\mathit{xxzz}}& =& 0\\ {M}_{\mathit{xxyy}}+{M}_{\mathit{yyyy}}+{M}_{\mathit{yyzz}}& =& 0\\ {M}_{\mathit{xxzz}}+{M}_{\mathit{yyzz}}+{M}_{\mathit{zzzz}}& =& 0\end{array}$

Mixture law on the matrix $\mathrm{M}$ :

$\begin{array}{c}M=\lbrace \begin{array}{ccc}{M}^{c}& \text{si}& 0.00⩽{Z}_{f}⩽0.01\\ {M}^{2}={Z}_{f}{M}^{1}+(1-{Z}_{f}){M}^{c}& \text{si}& 0.01⩽{Z}_{f}⩽0.99\\ {M}^{1}& \text{si}& 0.99⩽{Z}_{f}⩽1.00\end{array}\\ {Z}_{f}={Z}_{1}+{Z}_{2};\text{}{Z}_{c}={Z}_{3}=1-{Z}_{f}\end{array}$

Equivalent strain rate: $\dot{p}={\left(\frac{{\sigma}_{\mathit{eq}}}{{\mathit{ap}}^{m}}\right)}^{n}{e}^{-Q/T}$

or, equivalently: ${\sigma}_{\mathit{eq}}=\underset{\text{contrainte visqueuse}{\sigma}_{v}}{\underset{⏟}{a{\left({e}^{Q/T}\right)}^{1/n}{p}^{m}{\dot{p}}^{1/n}}}={\sigma}_{v}$

Mixture law on the viscous stress ${\sigma}_{v}$ :

${\sigma}_{\mathit{eq}}={\sigma}_{v}=\sum_{i=1}^{3}{f}_{i}({Z}_{\alpha}){\sigma}_{\mathit{vi}}$ with ${\sigma}_{\mathit{vi}}={a}_{i}{\left({e}^{{Q}_{i}/T}\right)}^{1/{n}_{i}}{p}^{{m}_{i}}{\dot{p}}^{1/{n}_{i}}$

> **Note** :

in the isotropic case, one has:

$\begin{array}{}{M}_{\mathrm{rrrr}}={M}_{\theta \theta \theta \theta }={M}_{\mathrm{zzzz}}=1\\ {M}_{\mathrm{r\theta r\theta }}={M}_{\mathrm{rzrz}}={M}_{\theta z\theta z}=0.75\end{array}$

> $\begin{array}{c}{M}_{\mathit{xxxx}}={M}_{\mathit{yyyy}}={M}_{\mathit{zzzz}}=1\\ {M}_{\mathit{xyxy}}={M}_{\mathit{xzxz}}={M}_{\mathit{yzyz}}=0.75\end{array}$

The choice of the type of coordinates (cylindrical or Cartesian) is made respectively by detection of the keyword F_MRR_RR or of the keyword F_MXX_XX.

#### 9.11.1 Operands

The table below summarises the correspondences between the symbols of the equations and the keywords.

| Symbol in the equations | Keyword |
| --- | --- |
| $\mathrm{a1}$ , $\mathrm{a2}$ , $\mathrm{a3}$ | ’F1_A’, ’F2_A’, ’C_A’ |
| $\mathrm{m1}$ , $\mathrm{m2}$ , $\mathrm{m3}$ | ’F1_M’, ’F2_M’, ’C_M’ |
| $\mathrm{n1}$ , $\mathrm{n2}$ , $\mathrm{n3}$ :math:`` | ’F1_N’, ’F2_N’, ’C_N’ |
| $\mathrm{Q1}$ , $\mathrm{Q2}$ , $\mathrm{Q3}$ | ’F1_Q’, ’F2_Q’, ’C_Q’ |

The Hill matrix is known either for the cold phase (1) ’F_M**_**’, or for the hot phase (3) ’C_M**_**’.

> **Note:**

*The coefficients* ’F1_Q’, ’F2_Q’ *and* ’C_Q’ *are in degrees Kelvin.*
