### 10.2 Factor keyword THM_INIT

For all the thermo-hydro-mechanical constitutive behaviours, it makes it possible to describe the initial state of the structure (cf. [R7.01.11](doc:r7.01.11) and [R7.01.14](doc:r7.01.14)).

In order to understand these data properly, one must distinguish the unknowns at the nodes, which we call ${\left\lbrace u\right\rbrace }^{\mathit{ddl}}$ , and the values defined under the keyword THM_INIT, which we call ${p}^{\mathit{ref}}$ and ${T}^{\mathit{ref}}$ .

${\left\lbrace u\right\rbrace }^{\mathrm{ddl}}=\left\lbrace \begin{array}{}{u}_{x}\\ {u}_{y}\\ {u}_{z}\\ {\mathrm{PRE1}}^{\mathrm{ddl}}\\ {\mathrm{PRE2}}^{\mathrm{ddl}}\end{array}\right\rbrace$

The meaning of the unknowns $\mathrm{PRE1}$ and $\mathrm{PRE2}$ varies according to the models. Denoting by ${p}_{w}$ the water pressure, ${p}_{\mathrm{ad}}$ the dissolved-air pressure, ${p}_{l}$  the liquid pressure ${p}_{l}={p}_{w}+{p}_{\mathrm{ad}}$ , ${p}_{\mathrm{as}}$ the dry-air pressure, ${p}_{\mathrm{vp}}$  the vapour pressure, ${p}_{g}={p}_{\mathrm{as}}+{p}_{\mathrm{vp}}$ the total gas pressure and ${p}_{c}={p}_{g}-{p}_{l}$ the capillary pressure (also called suction), one has the following meanings of the unknowns $\mathrm{PRE1}$ and $\mathrm{PRE2}$ :

| Constitutive behaviour KIT | LIQU_SATU | LIQU_VAPE | LIQU_GAZ_ATM | GAZ | LIQU_VAPE_GAZ | LIQU_GAZ | LIQU_AD_GAZ_VAPE | LIQU_AD_GAZ |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| $\mathrm{PRE1}$ | ${p}_{l}$ | ${p}_{l}$ | $-{p}_{l}$ | ${p}_{g}$ | ${p}_{c}={p}_{g}-{p}_{l}$ | ${p}_{c}={p}_{g}-{p}_{l}$ | ${p}_{c}={p}_{g}-{p}_{l}$ | ${p}_{c}={p}_{g}-{p}_{l}$ |
| $\mathrm{PRE2}$ |  |  |  |  | ${p}_{g}$ | ${p}_{g}$ | ${p}_{g}$ | ${p}_{g}$ |

One may refer to [§4.4.3] of the documentation [U4.51.11](doc:u4.51.11).

One then defines the "total" pressures and temperature by:

$p={p}^{\mathrm{ddl}}+{p}^{\mathrm{ref}};T={T}^{\mathrm{ddl}}+{T}^{\mathrm{ref}}$

The values written by IMPR_RESU are the nodal unknowns ${p}^{\mathrm{ddl}}\text{et}{T}^{\mathrm{ddl}}$ . Likewise, the boundary conditions must be expressed with respect to the nodal unknowns.

On the other hand, it is the total pressures and temperature which are used in the constitutive laws $\frac{P}{\rho}=\frac{R}{M}T$ for perfect gases, $\frac{d{\rho}_{l}}{{\rho}_{l}}=\frac{{\mathrm{dp}}_{l}}{{K}_{l}}-3{\alpha}_{l}\mathrm{dT}$ for the liquid and in the saturation/capillary-pressure relation.

Note that the nodal values may be initialised by the keyword ETAT_INIT of the command STAT_NON_LINE or by a combination of the two (this may allow an easy conversion from kelvin into degrees, for example).

#### 10.2.1 Operand TEMP

Reference temperature ${T}^{\mathit{ref}}$ expressed in kelvin. By default it is taken equal to zero. Take care: the value of the **initial** temperature $T={T}^{\text{ddl}}+{T}^{\text{ref}}$ must be strictly greater than zero.

**The value of the reference temperature entered under the keyword** **VALE_REF of the command** **AFFE_VARC is ignored.**

#### 10.2.2 Operand PRE1

By default it is taken equal to zero.

For the constitutive behaviours: LIQU_SATU, LIQU_VAPE, reference liquid pressure.

For the constitutive behaviour: GAZ, reference gas pressure. In this case the initial gas pressure $p={p}^{\text{ddl}}+{p}^{\text{ref}}$ must be non-zero.

For the constitutive behaviour: LIQU_GAZ_ATM, reference liquid pressure with a change of sign.

For the constitutive behaviours: LIQU_VAPE_GAZ, LIQU_AD_GAZ_VAPE, LIQU_AD_GAZ and LIQU_GAZ, reference capillary pressure.

#### 10.2.3 Operand PRE2

By default it is taken equal to zero. For the constitutive behaviours: LIQU_VAPE_GAZ, LIQU_AD_GAZ_VAPE, LIQU_AD_GAZ and LIQU_GAZ, reference gas pressure. The **initial** gas pressure $p={p}^{\text{ddl}}+{p}^{\text{ref}}$ *must be non-zero* .

#### 10.2.4 Operand PORO/PRES_VAPE/DEGR_SATU

```text
PORO        = poro
```

Initial porosity ${\Phi}_{0}$ .

**Note**

For the modellings AXIS_JHMS and PLAN_JHMS, this value has no meaning and is not used during the analysis.

```text
PRES_VAPE    = pvap
```

For the constitutive behaviours: LIQU_VAPE_GAZ, LIQU_VAPE, LIQU_AD_GAZ_VAPE and LIQU_GAZ, initial vapour pressure.

```text
DEGR_SATU    = ds
```

For all the unsaturated constitutive behaviours: initial degree of saturation (henceforth useless, given for information).

### 10.3 Factor keyword THM_LIQU

This keyword concerns all the THM constitutive behaviours involving a liquid (cf. [R7.01.11](doc:r7.01.11)).

#### 10.3.1 Operand RHO

Density of the liquid for the pressure defined under the keyword PRE1 of the factor keyword THM_INIT.

#### 10.3.2 Operand UN_SUR_K

Inverse of the compressibility ${K}_{l}$ of the liquid. A zero value means an incompressible liquid.

#### 10.3.3 Operand ALPHA

Coefficient of thermal expansion ${\alpha}_{l}$ of the liquid.

If ${p}_{l}$ denotes the pressure of the liquid, ${\rho}_{l}$ its density and $T$ the temperature, the constitutive relation of the liquid is: $\frac{d{\rho}_{l}}{{\rho}_{l}}=\frac{{\mathrm{dp}}_{l}}{{K}_{l}}-3{\alpha}_{l}\mathrm{dT}$ .

#### 10.3.4 Operand CP

Specific heat at constant pressure of the liquid.

#### 10.3.5 Operands VISC/D_VISC_TEMP

```text
VISC            = vi
```

Dynamic viscosity of the liquid (reminder of the units: [pressure] . [time]). Function of the temperature.

```text
D_VISC_TEMP    = dvi
```

Derivative of the viscosity of the liquid with respect to temperature. Function of the temperature. The user must ensure consistency with the function associated with VISC.

### 10.4 Factor keyword THM_GAZ

This factor keyword concerns all the THM constitutive behaviours involving a gas (cf. [R7.01.11](doc:r7.01.11)). For the constitutive behaviours involving both a liquid and a gas, and when evaporation of the liquid is taken into account, the coefficients supplied here concern the dry gas. The properties of the vapour are supplied under the keyword THM_VAPE_GAZ.

#### 10.4.1 Operand MASS_MOL

Molar mass of the dry gas ${M}_{\mathrm{gs}}.$

If ${p}_{\mathrm{gs}}$ denotes the pressure of the dry gas, ${\rho}_{\mathrm{gs}}$ its density, $R$ the perfect-gas constant and $T$  the temperature, the constitutive behaviour of the dry gas is: $\frac{{p}_{\mathrm{gs}}}{{\rho}_{\mathrm{gs}}}=\frac{\mathrm{RT}}{{M}_{\mathrm{gs}}}$ .

#### 10.4.2 Operand CP

Specific heat at constant pressure of the dry gas.

#### 10.4.3 Operand VISC

Viscosity of the dry gas. Function of the temperature.

#### 10.4.4 Operand D_VISC_TEMP

Derivative with respect to temperature of the viscosity of the dry gas. Function of the temperature.

The user must ensure consistency with the function associated with VISC.

### 10.5 Factor keyword THM_VAPE_GAZ

This factor keyword concerns all the THM constitutive behaviours involving both a liquid and a gas, and taking evaporation of the liquid into account (cf. [R7.01.11](doc:r7.01.11)). The coefficients supplied here concern the vapour.

#### 10.5.1 Operand MASS_MOL

MASS_MOL        = m

Molar mass of the vapour ${M}_{\mathrm{vp}}$ .

If ${M}_{\mathrm{vp}}$ denotes the vapour pressure, ${\rho}_{\text{vp}}$ its density, the perfect-gas constant $R$ and $T$ the temperature, the constitutive behaviour of the vapour is: $\frac{{p}_{\mathrm{vp}}}{{\rho}_{\mathrm{vp}}}=\frac{\mathrm{RT}}{{M}_{\mathrm{vp}}}.$

#### 10.5.2 Operand CP

```text
CP                = cp
```

Specific heat at constant pressure of the vapour.

#### 10.5.3 Operand VISC

```text
VISC            = v
```

Viscosity of the vapour. Function of the temperature.

#### 10.5.4 Operand D_VISC_TEMP

```text
D_VISC_TEMP    = dvi
```

Derivative with respect to temperature of the viscosity of the vapour. Function of the temperature.

The user must ensure consistency with the function associated with VISC.

### 10.6 Factor keyword THM_AIR_DISS

This factor keyword concerns the THM constitutive behaviour THM_AD_GAZ_VAPE taking dissolution of air in the liquid into account (cf. [R7.01.11](doc:r7.01.11)). The coefficients supplied here concern the dissolved air.

#### 10.6.1 Operand CP

```text
CP                = cp
```

Specific heat at constant pressure of the dissolved air.

#### 10.6.2 Operand COEF_HENRY

```text
COEF_HENRY    = h
```

Henry constant ${K}_{H}$ , a function of temperature, making it possible to relate the molar concentration of dissolved air ${C}_{\mathrm{ad}}^{\mathrm{ol}}$ ($\mathrm{moles}/{m}^{3}$ ) to the dry-air pressure:

${C}_{\mathrm{ad}}^{\mathrm{ol}}=\frac{{p}_{\mathrm{as}}}{{K}_{H}}$

### 10.7 Factor keyword THM_DIFFU

Mandatory for all the THM constitutive behaviours (cf. [R7.01.11](doc:r7.01.11)). The user must ensure the consistency of the functions and of their derivative.

#### 10.7.1 Operands R_GAZ/RHO/CP/BIOT_COEF

```text
R_GAZ = rgaz
```

Perfect-gas constant.

RHO     = rho

For the hydromechanical constitutive behaviours, homogenised density. In the static cases, it is useless to supply the density in the elastic constitutive behaviour (cf. section  4.1.3 ).

```text
CP    = cp
```

For the thermal constitutive behaviours, specific heat at constant stress of the solid alone.

```text
BIOT_COEF    = bio
```

Biot coefficient. Must be less than or equal to $1$ .

#### 10.7.2 Operands BIOT_L/BIOT_T/BIOT_N

Replaces BIOT_COEF in the anisotropic case. In the case of transverse isotropy (3D), BIOT_L and BIOT_N are respectively the Biot coefficients in the directions L and N (perpendicular to the plane of isotropy). In the orthotropic case in 2D, one defines the Biot coefficients in the three directions L, T, N: BIOT_L, BIOT_T and BIOT_N.

**Note:**  if one chooses this anisotropic version of the Biot coefficients, one must take care to choose likewise the anisotropic version of the elasticity. Otherwise an error message will be issued.

#### 10.7.3 Operands SATU_PRES/D_SATU_PRES

For the constitutive behaviours of unsaturated materials (LIQU_VAPE_GAZ, LIQU_VAPE, LIQU_AD_GAZ, LIQU_AD_GAZ_VAPE, LIQU_GAZ, LIQU_GAZ_ATM).

```text
SATU_PRES    = sp
```

Saturation isotherm, a function of the capillary pressure.

```text
D_SATU_PRES= dsp
```

Derivative of the saturation with respect to the pressure.

#### 10.7.4 Operands PESA_X/PESA_Y/PESA_Z/PESA_MULT

```text
PESA_X = px,PESA_y = py,PESA_z = pz,
```

Gravity along $x$ , $y$ or $z$ , used only if the modelling chosen in AFFE_MODELE includes 1 or 2 pressure variables.

```text
PESA_MULT = fpesa
```

Time function as a factor of the gravity components PESA_X, PESA_Y and PESA_Z. Optional; by default it is constant and equal to 1.

#### 10.7.5 Operand PERM_IN

Intrinsic permeability ${K}_{int}$ , whose dimension is that of an area: a function of the porosity $\varphi$ (in the isotropic case). In studies, the user may express the dependence of the intrinsic permeability on $\varphi$ by the following usual cubic law, with the help of a previously defined function:

$\frac{{K}_{int}(\varphi )}{{K}_{{int}_{0}}}=\begin{array}{c}\mathrm{si}\varphi -{\varphi}_{0}<0:1\\ \mathrm{si}0<\varphi -{\varphi}_{0}<{10}^{-2}:1+\chi {(\varphi -{\varphi}_{0})}^{3}\\ \mathrm{si}{10}^{-2}<\varphi -{\varphi}_{0}:1+\chi \ast {10}^{-6}\end{array}$

Other laws are of course possible.

The permeability in the usual sense $K$ , whose dimension is that of a velocity, is computed in the following way:

$K=\frac{{K}_{int}{K}_{\mathrm{rel}}}{\mu}{\rho}_{l}g$ where ${K}_{int}$ is the intrinsic permeability, ${K}_{\mathrm{rel}}$ the permeability relative to the liquid (dimensionless), $\mu$ the dynamic viscosity of the liquid, ${\rho}_{l}$ the density of the liquid and $g$ the acceleration of gravity. ${K}_{int}$ is in fact a diagonal tensor; in the isotropic case its three components are equal to the value supplied.

#### 10.7.6 Operands PERMIN_L/PERMIN_T/PERMIN_N

For the definition of the planes of isotropy one will refer to  Error: Reference source not found and  Error: Reference source not found . In the case of transverse isotropy (3D), PERMIN_L and PERMIN_T are respectively the intrinsic permeabilities in the directions L and N (perpendicular to the plane of isotropy). In the orthotropic case in 2D, one defines the permeabilities in the planes L and T: PERMIN_L and PERMIN_T.

#### 10.7.7 Operands PERM_LIQU/D_PERM_LIQU_SATU

For the constitutive behaviours of unsaturated materials (LIQU_VAPE_GAZ, LIQU_VAPE, LIQU_AD_GAZ, LIQU_AD_GAZ_VAPE, LIQU_GAZ, LIQU_GAZ_ATM).

Permeability relative to the liquid (dimensionless): a function of the saturation and its derivative with respect to the saturation .

#### 10.7.8 Operands PERM_GAZ/D_PERM_SATU_GAZ/D_PERM_PRES_GAZ

For the constitutive behaviours of unsaturated materials (LIQU_VAPE_GAZ, LIQU_VAPE, LIQU_AD_GAZ, LIQU_AD_GAZ_VAPE, LIQU_GAZ).

Permeability relative to the gas (dimensionless): a function of the saturation and of the gas pressure, and its derivatives with respect to the saturation and to the gas pressure.

#### 10.7.9 Operands VG_N/VG_PR/VG_SR

For the constitutive behaviours of unsaturated liquid-gas materials with two constituents and two unknowns (LIQU_VAPE_GAZ, LIQU_AD_GAZ, LIQU_AD_GAZ_VAPE, LIQU_GAZ,) and in the case where the hydraulic law is HYDR_VGM or HYDR_VGC (see doc. U4.51.11), they denote respectively the parameters $N$ , $\mathit{Pr}$ , and $\mathit{Sr}$ of the Mualem–Van Genuchten law used to define the capillary pressure and the relative permeabilities to water and to gas.

#### 10.7.10 Operands VG_SMAX/VG_SATUR

For the constitutive behaviours of unsaturated liquid-gas materials with two constituents and two unknowns (LIQU_VAPE_GAZ, LIQU_AD_GAZ, LIQU_AD_GAZ_VAPE, LIQU_GAZ,) and in the case where the hydraulic law is HYDR_VGM or HYDR_VGC (see document [U4.51.11](doc:u4.51.11)).

```text
VG_SMAX = smax
```

denotes the maximum saturation for which the Mualem–Van Genuchten law is applied. Beyond this saturation the Mualem–Van Genuchten curves are interpolated (see document [R7.01.11](doc:r7.01.11)). This value must be very close to 1.

```text
VG_SATUR = stur
```

Beyond the saturation defined by VG_SMAX, the saturation is multiplied by this corrective factor. This value must be very close to 1 (see document [R7.01.11](doc:r7.01.11)).

#### 10.7.11 Operands FICKV_T/FICKV_S/FICKV_PG/FICKV_PV

For the constitutive behaviours LIQU_VAPE_GAZ and LIQU_AD_GAZ_VAPE, Fick coefficient as a function of temperature for the diffusion of vapour in the gaseous mixture. The Fick coefficient possibly being a function of the saturation, the temperature, the gas pressure and the vapour pressure, one defines it as a product of 4 functions: FICKV_T, FICKV_S, FICKV_PG, FICKV_VP. In the case of LIQU_VAPE_GAZ and LIQU_AD_GAZ_VAPE, only FICKV_T is mandatory.

#### 10.7.12 Operands D_FV_T/D_FV_PG

For the constitutive behaviours LIQU_VAPE_GAZ and LIQU_AD_GAZ_VAPE.

Derivative of the coefficient FICKV_T with respect to temperature.

Derivative of the coefficient FICKV_PG with respect to the gas pressure.

#### 10.7.13 Operands FICKA_T/FICKA_S/FICKA_PA/FICKA_P

For the constitutive behaviour LIQU_AD_GAZ_VAPE, Fick coefficient as a function of temperature for the diffusion of dissolved air in the liquid mixture. The Fick coefficient possibly being a function of the saturation, the temperature, the dissolved-air pressure and the liquid pressure, one defines it as a product of 4 functions: FICKA_T, FICKA_S, FICKV_PA, FICKV_PL. In the case of LIQU_AD_GAZ_VAPE, only FICKA_T is mandatory.

#### 10.7.14 Operand D_FA_T

For the constitutive behaviour LIQU_AD_GAZ_VAPE, derivative of the coefficient FICKA_T with respect to temperature.

#### 10.7.15 Operands LAMB_T/LAMB_S/LAMB_PHI/LAMB_CT

LAMB_T = lambt

Multiplicative part of the thermal conductivity of the mixture depending on the temperature (cf. [R7.01.11](doc:r7.01.11)). This operand is mandatory in the case of a modelling with thermal analysis.

LAMB_S = lambs, LAMB_PHI = lambp

Multiplicative part (equal to 1 by default) of the thermal conductivity of the mixture depending respectively on the saturation and on the porosity.

LAMB_CT = lambct

Part of the thermal conductivity of the mixture which is constant and additive (cf. [R7.01.11](doc:r7.01.11)). This constant is equal to zero by default.

#### 10.7.16 Operands LAMB_TL/ LAMB_TN/LAMB_TT

Replaces LAMB_T in the anisotropic case. In the case of transverse isotropy (3D), LAMB_TL and LAMB_TT are respectively the conductivities in the directions L and N (perpendicular to the plane of isotropy). In the orthotropic case in 2D, one defines the conductivities in the planes L and T: LAMB_TL and LAMB_TN.

#### 10.7.17 Operands LAMB_C_L/ LAMB_C_N/LAMB_C_T

Replaces LAMB_CT in the anisotropic case. In the case of transverse isotropy (3D), LAMB_C_L and LAMB_C_T are respectively the conductivities in the directions L and N (perpendicular to the plane of isotropy). In the orthotropic case in 2D, one defines the conductivities in the planes L and T: LAMB_C_L and LAMB_C_N.

#### 10.7.18 Operands D_LB_T/D_LB_S/D_LB_PHI

D_LB_T = dlambt

Derivative with respect to temperature of the part of the thermal conductivity of the mixture which depends on the temperature.

D_LB_S = dlambs, D_LB_PHI = dlambp

Derivative of the part of the thermal conductivity of the mixture which depends respectively on the saturation and on the porosity.

#### 10.7.19 Operands D_LB_TL/D_LB_TN/D_LB_TT

In the anisotropic case, derivatives with respect to temperature of LAMB_TL, LAMB_TN and LAMB_TT respectively.

#### 10.7.20 Operand EMMAG

Storage coefficient. This coefficient is taken into account only in the cases of modellings without mechanics. It relates the variation of porosity to the variation of liquid pressure.

#### 10.7.21 Operand PERM_END

Permeability as a function of damage, used by the mechanical constitutive behaviours with damage.

#### 10.7.22 Operands EPAI/A0/SHUTTLE/S_BJH/W_BJH

For the constitutive behaviours of unsaturated liquid-gas materials with two constituents and two unknowns (LIQU_VAPE_GAZ, LIQU_AD_GAZ, LIQU_AD_GAZ_VAPE, LIQU_GAZ, LIQU_GAZ_ATM) and in the case where the hydraulic law is HYDR_TABBAL (see doc. U 2 . 04 . 05 ). These parameters are those of the poromechanical model for severe drying defined in the thesis of G. El Tabbal . They are used to define respectively the thickness of the adsorbed layer EPAI, the specific surface A0 of the material, the Shuttleworth parameter SHUTTLE and the functions output from the BJH computation: S_BJH and W_BJH .

### 10.8 Keyword MOHR_COULOMB

The Mohr-Coulomb model is an elastoplastic model used in soil mechanics and is especially suited to sandy materials. The document [:ref:` **R7.01.28**  < **R7.01.28** >`] describes the corresponding equations. This model may be used independently of the THM constitutive behaviours. The elastic characteristics must be defined under the keyword ELAS.

#### 10.8.1 Operands PHI/ANGDIL/COHESION

```text
PHI = phi
```

Friction angle (in degrees). The value must lie between 0 and 60 degrees.

```text
ANGDIL = angdil
```

Dilatancy angle (in degrees). The value must lie between 0 and 60 degrees.

```text
COHESION = cohes
```

Cohesion of the material (in pascals – if SI units).

### 10.9 Keyword CAM_CLAY

The Cam-Clay model is an elastoplastic model used in soil mechanics and is especially suited to clayey materials. The model presented here is called modified Cam-Clay. The document [R7.01.14](doc:r7.01.14) describes the corresponding equations. This model may be used independently of the THM constitutive behaviours. The elastic characteristics must be defined under the keyword ELAS.

#### 10.9.1 Operands MU/LAMBDA/KAPA

```text
MU = mu
```

Elastic shear modulus.

```text
LAMBDA = lambda
```

Compressibility coefficient (plastic slope in a hydrostatic compression test).

```text
KAPA = kapa
```

Elastic swelling coefficient (elastic slope in a hydrostatic compression test).

#### 10.9.2 Operand M

Slope of the critical-state line.

#### 10.9.3 Operand PORO

Initial porosity. If CAM_CLAY is used under RELATION_KIT, the keyword PORO supplied under CAM_CLAY and under THM_INIT must be the same.

#### 10.9.4 Operands PRES_CRIT/KCAM

```text
PRES_CRIT    = prescr
```

The critical pressure, equal to half the consolidation pressure.

```text
KCAM = kcam
```

Initial pressure corresponding to the initial porosity, generally equal to atmospheric pressure. This parameter must be positive ($\mathrm{kcam}>0.$ ).

#### 10.9.5 Operand PTRAC

Amount of hydrostatic tensile stress tolerated, or shift of the ellipse towards the left on the axis of the hydrostatic stresses. This parameter must be negative (ptrac < 0.).

### 10.10 Factor keyword CJS

The law (Cambou, Jaffani, Sidoroff) is a constitutive law for soils. It comprises three mechanisms: one corresponds to non-linear elasticity, another corresponds to plastification for isotropic stress states, and the third mechanism corresponds to a plastification linked to a deviatoric stress state. The document [R7.01.13](doc:r7.01.13) describes the corresponding equations in detail.

The elastic characteristics must be defined under the keyword ELAS.

The CJS law covers three possible forms (CJS1, CJS2 and CJS3), according to whether or not one allows activation of the non-linear mechanisms.

The table below gives the mechanisms activated for the three levels CJS1, CJS2 and CJS3:

|  | **Elastic mechanism** | **Isotropic plastic mechanism** | **Deviatoric plastic mechanism** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| CJS1 | linear | not activated | activated, perfect plasticity |
| CJS2 | non-linear | activated | activated, isotropic hardening |
| CJS3 | non-linear | activated | activated, kinematic hardening |

**Note**

By adopting the correspondence of the parameters for the limit states, it is possible to use the constitutive behaviour CJS1 to model a Mohr-Coulomb law in soil mechanics.

The various coefficients are to be supplied or not according to the level that one wishes to use, in accordance with the table below (F for optional, O for mandatory and nothing for not applicable).

| Symbol | ${Q}_{\mathrm{init}}$ | ${R}_{\mathrm{init}}$ | $n$ | ${K}^{p}$ | $\gamma$ | $\beta$ | ${R}_{c}$ | $A$ |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Keyword | Q_INIT | R_INIT | N_CJS | KP | PCO = pcoGAMMA_CJS | BETA_CJS | RC | A_CJS |
| CJS1 | F |  |  |  | O | O |  |  |
| CJS2 | F | F | O | O | O | O | O | O |
| CJS3 | F |  | O | O | O | O | O |  |

| Symbol | $b$ | ${R}_{m}$ | $\mu$ | ${p}_{\mathrm{co}}$ | $c$ | ${P}_{a}$ |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Keyword | B_CJS | RM | M_CJS | PCO | C_CJS | PA |
| CJS1 |  | O |  |  |  | O |
| CJS2 |  | O |  |  |  | O |
| CJS3 | O | O | O | O | O | O |

The user's attention is drawn to the fact that, for one and the same material, the same coefficient may take different values according to the level used. The level used is never supplied; it is indicated by the fact that certain coefficients are supplied or not.

Furthermore, the keyword ELAS must mandatorily be supplied when one uses the CJS law (under one of its three levels). The definition of Young's modulus and of Poisson's ratio makes it possible to compute the coefficients ${K}_{o}^{e}$ and ${G}_{o}$ .

#### 10.10.1 Operands BETA_CJS/RM

For levels CJS1, CJS2, CJS3.

```text
BETA_CJS     = beta
```

Parameter $\beta$ . Controls the variation of plastic volume in the deviatoric mechanism.

```text
RM    = rm
```

Maximum value of the opening of the deviatoric reversibility domain.

#### 10.10.2 Operands N_CJS/KP/RC

For levels CJS2 and CJS3.

```text
N_CJS    = n
```

Controls the dependence of the elastic moduli on the mean stress:

$K={K}_{o}^{e}{(\frac{{I}_{1}+{Q}_{\mathrm{init}}}{{\mathrm{3P}}_{a}})}^{n}\text{}G={G}_{o}{(\frac{{I}_{1}+{Q}_{\mathrm{init}}}{{\mathrm{3P}}_{a}})}^{n}$

```text
KP    = kp
```

Plastic compressibility modulus:

${\dot{Q}}_{\mathrm{iso}}={K}^{p}\dot{q}={K}_{o}^{p}{(\frac{{Q}_{\mathrm{iso}}}{{P}_{a}})}^{n}\dot{q}$

```text
RC = rc
```

Critical value of the variable $R$ :

${\dot{e}}_{v}^{\mathrm{dp}}=-\beta (\frac{{s}_{\mathrm{II}}}{{s}_{\mathrm{II}}^{c}}-1)\frac{∣{s}_{ij}{\dot{e}}_{ij}^{\mathrm{dp}}∣}{{s}_{\mathrm{II}}}\text{}{s}_{\mathrm{II}}^{c}=-\frac{{R}_{c}{I}_{1}}{h({\theta}_{s})}$

#### 10.10.3 Operands A_CJS/R_INIT

For level CJS2.

```text
A_CJS    = a
```

Controls the isotropic hardening of the deviatoric mechanism;

$R=\frac{{\mathrm{AR}}_{m}r}{{R}_{m}+\mathrm{Ar}}$

```text
R_INIT        = r
```

Initial value of the variable $R$ . At the first computation time, if the initial value of $R$ is zero, either because one has not defined an initial state of the internal variables by the keyword ETAT_INIT of STAT_NON_LINE, or because this initial state is zero, one will take as the initial value that defined by the keyword R_INIT of DEFI_MATERIAU.

#### 10.10.4 Operands B_CJS/C_CJS/PCO/MU_CJS

For level CJS3.

```text
B_CJS    = b
```

Controls the kinematic hardening of the deviatoric mechanism:

${\dot{X}}_{ij}=-\frac{1}{b}{\dot{\lambda}}^{d}\left[\mathrm{dev}(\frac{\partial {f}^{d}}{\partial {X}_{ij}})-{I}_{1}f{X}_{ij}\right]{(\frac{{I}_{1}}{3{P}_{a}})}^{-1.5}$

```text
C_CJS    = c
```

Controls the evolution of the critical pressure: ${p}_{c}={p}_{\mathrm{co}}\exp(-c{\varepsilon}_{v})$ .

```text
PCO     = pco
```

Initial critical pressure: ${p}_{c}={p}_{\mathrm{co}}\exp(-c{\varepsilon}_{v})$ .

```text
MU_CJS        = mu
```

Controls the failure value of the variable $R$ : ${R}_{r}={R}_{c}+m\ln(\frac{3{p}_{c}}{{I}_{1}})$ .

#### 10.10.5 Operands GAMMA_CJS/PA/Q_INIT

For levels CJS1, CJS2 and CJS3.

```text
GAMMA_CJS    = g
```

Controls the shape of the criterion: $h({\theta}_{s})={(1+\gamma \cos(3{\theta}_{s}))}^{1/6}={(1+\gamma \sqrt{54}\frac{\det(\underline{\underline{s}})}{{s}_{\mathrm{II}}^{3}})}^{1/6}$

```text
PA = pa
```

Atmospheric pressure. Must be given negative.

```text
Q_INIT = q
```

Numerical parameter making it possible to render a zero stress state admissible. It may also be used to define a cohesion, at least for level CJS1. One will use the formula: ${Q}_{\mathit{init}}=-3c\mathit{cotan}(\phi )$

### 10.11 Factor keyword LAIGLE

The LAIGLE law [R7.01.15](doc:r7.01.15) is a rheological constitutive model for the modelling of rocks. The latter are characterised by the following three parameters:

- $a$ , which defines the influence of the dilatancy component in the constitutive behaviour at large strains. This parameter depends on the level of alteration of the rock,
- $s$ , which defines the cohesion of the medium. It is therefore representative of the damage of the rock,
- $m$ is a function of the mineralogical nature of the rock, and is associated with a substantial body of feedback from experience.

The elastic characteristics must be defined under the keyword ELAS.

#### 10.11.1 Operands GAMMA_ULT/GAMMA_E

```text
GAMMA_ULT    = gamma_ult
```

Parameter ${\gamma}_{\mathrm{ult}}$ : deviatoric plastic strain corresponding to the plateau.

```text
GAMMA_E =    gamma_e
```

Parameter ${\gamma}_{e}$ : deviatoric plastic strain corresponding to the complete disappearance of the cohesion.

#### 10.11.2 Operand M_ULT/M_E/A_E/M_PIC

```text
M_ULT    = m_ult
```

Parameter ${m}_{\mathrm{ult}}$ : value of $m$ of the ultimate criterion reached at ${\gamma}_{\mathrm{ult}}$ .

```text
M_E     =    m_e
```

Parameter ${m}_{e}$ : value of $m$ of the intermediate criterion reached at ${\gamma}_{e}$ .

```text
A_E     =    a_e
```

Parameter ${a}_{e}$ : value of $a$ of the intermediate criterion reached at ${\gamma}_{e}$ .

```text
M_PIC    =    m_pic
```

Parameter ${m}_{\mathrm{pic}}$ : value of $m$ of the peak criterion reached at the stress peak.

#### 10.11.3 Operands A_PIC/ETA/SIGMA_C

```text
A_PIC    =    a_pic
```

Parameter ${a}_{\mathrm{pic}}$ : value of the exponent $a$ at the stress peak.

```text
ETA     =    eta
```

Parameter $\eta$ : exponent regulating the hardening.

```text
SIGMA_C = sigma_c
```

Parameter ${s}_{c}$ : uniaxial compressive strength.

#### 10.11.4 Operands GAMMA/KSI

GAMMA = gamma, KSI = ksi

Parameters $\gamma$ and $\xi$ : parameters governing the dilatancy.

A condition to be complied with is that the ratio $\gamma /\xi$ remains less than $1$ . In the case of very strong hard rocks, subjected to relatively low confining stresses, the variation of the dilatancy $\sin\psi$ (as a function of the stress state – see [R7.01.15](doc:r7.01.15)) may tend towards $\gamma /\xi$ , which justifies this condition.

#### 10.11.5 Operand GAMMA_CJS

Parameter ${\gamma}_{\mathit{cjs}}$ : shape parameter of the loading surface in the deviatoric plane.

#### 10.11.6 Operand SIGMA_P1

Parameter ${\sigma}_{\mathit{pl}}$ : intersection of the intermediate criterion and of the peak criterion.

#### 10.11.7 Operand PA

Atmospheric pressure. Must be given positive.

**Note:**

*The parameters* *M_E,* *A_E,* *A_PIC,* *SIGMA_P1,* *SIGMA_C and* *MPIC are dependent on one another through the relation:* ${m}_{e}=\frac{{\sigma}_{c}}{{\sigma}_{\mathrm{pl}}}{({m}_{\mathrm{pic}}\frac{{\sigma}_{\mathrm{pl}}}{{\sigma}_{c}}+1)}^{\frac{{a}_{\mathrm{pic}}}{{a}_{e}}}.$ *This dependence is checked within the code.*

### 10.12 Factor keyword LETK

The rheological model L&K (Laigle and Kleine) is an elasto-viscoplastic constitutive law called here LETK [R7.01.24](doc:r7.01.24). It relies on concepts of elastoplasticity and of viscoplasticity. Elastoplasticity is characterised by a positive hardening in the pre-peak regime and a negative hardening in the post-peak regime.

Among the parameters one finds:

- parameters which appear in the hardening functions relating to the various elastoplastic or viscous thresholds, such as $a$ , $s$ and $m$ ,
- parameters linked to the viscous criteria,
- parameters linked to dilatancy,
- parameters linked to the strength of the material in compression and in tension.

The elastic characteristics must be defined under the keyword ELAS.

#### 10.12.1 Operands PA/NELAS/SIGMA_C/H0_EXT

```text
PA             =     pa S_0            =    s0
```

Parameter $\mathrm{Pa}$ : atmospheric pressure.

```text
NELAS         =    nelas
```

Parameter ${n}_{\mathrm{elas}}$ : exponent of the law of variation of the elastic moduli $K$ and $G$ .

```text
SIGMA_C     =     sigc
```

Parameter ${\sigma}_{c}$ : uniaxial compressive strength (the unit of a stress)..

```text
H0_EXT     =     h0
```

Parameter ${H}_{\mathrm{0ext}}$ : parameter governing the tensile strength

#### 10.12.2 Operand GAMMA_CJS/XAMS

```text
GAMMA_CJS    =    gcjs
XAMS         =    xams
```

Parameter ${\gamma}_{\text{cjs}}$ : shape parameter of the criterion in the deviatoric plane (between 0 and 1).

Parameter ${x}_{\text{ams}}$ : non-zero parameter appearing in the pre-peak hardening laws.

#### 10.12.3 Operand ETA/A_0/A_E/A_PIC

```text
ETA            =    eta
```

Parameter $h$ : non-zero parameter appearing in the post-peak hardening laws.

```text
A_0            =    a0
```

Parameter ${a}_{0}$ : value of $a$ on the damage threshold.

```text
A_E            =    ae
```

Parameter ${a}_{e}$ : value of $a$ on the intermediate threshold.

```text
A_PIC        =    ap
```

Parameter ${a}_{\mathit{pic}}$ : value of $a$ on the peak threshold.

#### 10.12.4 Operands S_0/S_E/M_0/M_E/M_PIC/M_ULT

```text
S_0            =    s0
```

Parameter ${s}_{0}$ : value of $s$ on the damage threshold.

```text
S_E            =    se
```

Parameter ${s}_{e}$ : value of $s$ on the intermediate threshold.

```text
M_0            =    m0
```

Parameter ${m}_{0}$ : value of $m$ on the damage threshold.

```text
M_E            =    me
```

Parameter ${m}_{e}$ : value of $m$ on the intermediate threshold.

```text
M_PIC        =    mp
```

Parameter ${m}_{\mathrm{pic}}$ : value of $m$ on the peak threshold.

```text
M_ULT        =    mult
```

Parameter ${m}_{\mathrm{ult}}$ : value of $m$ on the residual threshold.

#### 10.12.5 Operands XI_E/XI_PIC/MV_MAX/XIV_MAX

```text
XI_E        =    xie
```

Parameter ${\xi}_{e}$ : hardening level on the intermediate threshold.

```text
XI_PIC        =    xip
```

Parameter ${\xi}_{\mathrm{pic}}$ : hardening level on the peak threshold.

```text
MV_MAX        =    mvmx
```

Parameter ${m}_{v-max}$ : value of $m$ on the viscoplasticity threshold.

```text
XIV_MAX    =    xivmx
```

Parameter ${\xi}_{v-\max}$ : hardening level required to reach the maximum viscoplastic threshold.

#### 10.12.6 Operands A/N

```text
A            =    A
```

Parameter $A$ : parameter characterising the amplitude of the creep rate (in ${s}^{-1}$  or ${\mathrm{jour}}^{-1}$ ).

```text
N            =    n
```

Parameter $n$ : exponent appearing in the formula governing the creep kinetics.

#### 10.12.7 Operand SIGMA_P1

```text
SIGMA_P1    =    sp1
```

Parameter ${\sigma}_{\mathit{P1}}$ : corresponds to the abscissa of the intersection point of the cleavage limit and of the peak threshold.

#### 10.12.8 Operands MU0_V and XI0_V

```text
MU0_V        =    mu0v, XI0_V        =    xi0v
```

Parameters ${\mu}_{\mathrm{0v}}$ and ${\xi}_{\mathrm{0v}}$ : parameters governing the dilatancy of the pre-peak and viscoplastic mechanisms

The conditions to be complied with for these parameters are:

${\mu}_{\mathrm{0v}}<{\xi}_{\mathrm{0v}}$ or $\lbrace \begin{array}{c}{\mu}_{0v}>{\xi}_{0v}\hfill \\ \frac{{s}_{\mathit{pic}}^{{a}_{\mathit{pic}}}}{{s}_{0}^{{a}_{0}}}\le \frac{1+{\mu}_{0v}}{{\mu}_{0v}-{\xi}_{0v}}\end{array}$ with ${s}^{\mathit{pic}}=1$

#### 10.12.9 Operands MU1 and XI1

```text
MU1 =    mu1, XI1 = xi1
```

Parameters ${\mu}_{1}$ and ${\xi}_{1}$ : parameters governing the dilatancy of the post-peak mechanisms. A condition to be complied with is that the ratio ${\mu}_{1}/{\xi}_{1}$ remains less than or equal to 1.

### 10.13 Factor keyword DRUCK_PRAGER

The DRUCK_PRAGER criterion [R7.01.16](doc:r7.01.16) is a constitutive model for soil mechanics; it is defined by the relation:

${\sigma}_{\mathit{eq}}+\alpha {I}_{1}-R(p)\le 0$

where:

${\sigma}_{\mathrm{eq}}$ is a function of the deviator of the effective stresses ${\sigma}^{'}$ ,

${I}_{1}=\mathrm{Tr}({\sigma}^{'})$ is the trace of the effective stresses,

$\alpha$ is a pressure-dependence coefficient,

$R(p)$ is a function of the cumulative plastic strain.

In the linear case (associated law DRUCK_PRAGER and non-associated law DRUCK_PRAG_N_A), the function $R$ is given by: $\begin{array}{cc}0<p<{p}_{\mathit{ult}}& R(p)=\mathit{hp}+{\sigma}_{y}\hfill \\ p\ge {p}_{\mathit{ult}}& R(p)=h{p}_{\mathit{ult}}+{\sigma}_{y}\hfill \end{array}$ .

In the parabolic case (associated law DRUCK_PRAGER), $R(p)={\sigma}_{y}f(p)$ where the function $f(p)$ is given by:

$\begin{array}{cc}0<p<{p}_{\mathit{ult}}& f\left(p\right)={\left(1-\left(1-\sqrt{\frac{{\sigma}_{y\mathit{ult}}}{{\sigma}_{y}}}\right)\frac{p}{{p}_{\mathit{ult}}}\right)}^{2}\hfill \\ p\ge {p}_{\mathit{ult}}& f\left(p\right)=\frac{{\sigma}_{y\mathit{ult}}}{{\sigma}_{y}}\hfill \end{array}$

> In the parabolic case (non-associated law DRUCK_PRAG_N_A), the function $R$ is given by:

$\begin{array}{cc}0<p<{p}_{\mathit{ult}}& R(p)=({\sigma}_{Y}-{\sigma}_{\mathit{ult}}){(\frac{p}{{p}_{\mathit{ult}}})}^{2}+2({\sigma}_{Y}-{\sigma}_{\mathit{ult}})\frac{p}{{p}_{\mathit{ult}}}+{\sigma}_{Y}\hfill \\ p\ge {p}_{\mathit{ult}}& R(p)={\sigma}_{\mathit{ult}}\hfill \end{array}$

> In the exponential case (non-associated law DRUCK_PRAG_N_A), the function $R$ is given by:

$R(p)=({\sigma}_{Y}-{\sigma}_{\mathit{ult}})\exp(\frac{-p}{{p}_{c}})+{\sigma}_{\mathit{ult}}$

> In the case of a non-associated constitutive law DRUCK_PRAG_N_A, it is also necessary to define a flow potential (in addition to the criterion defined above). This flow potential is written:

$G(\sigma ,p)={\sigma}_{\mathit{eq}}+\beta (p){I}_{1}$

where $\beta (p)$ is a function of the cumulative plastic strain

> In the cases of linear and parabolic hardening, this function $\beta (p)$ is written:

$\begin{array}{cc}0<p<{p}_{\mathit{ult}}& \beta (p)={\beta}_{0}(1-\frac{p}{{p}_{\mathit{ult}}})\hfill \\ p\ge {p}_{\mathit{ult}}& \beta (p)=0\hfill \end{array}$

> In the case of exponential hardening, this function $\beta (p)$ is written:

$\beta (p)=({\beta}_{0}-{\beta}_{\mathit{ult}})\exp(\frac{-p}{{p}_{c}})+{\beta}_{\mathit{ult}}$

#### 10.13.1 Operand ECROUISSAGE

```text
ECROUISSAGE    =/‘LINEAIRE‘,/‘PARABOLIQUE‘,/‘EXPONENTIEL‘
```

Makes it possible to define the type of hardening desired.

#### 10.13.2 Operand ALPHA

```text
ALPHA    = alpha
```

Denotes the pressure-dependence coefficient. One recalls that the operand ALPHA is related to the friction angle $\mathrm{\phi }$ by the relation: $\alpha =\frac{2.\sin(\mathrm{\phi })}{3-\sin(\mathrm{\phi })}$ .

#### 10.13.3 Operand P_ULTM

```text
P_ULTM    = p_ult
```

Denotes the ultimate cumulative plastic strain for linear and parabolic hardening (operand ECROUISSAGE =‘LINEAIRE‘,/‘PARABOLIQUE‘) .

#### 10.13.4 Operand SY

```text
SY    = sy
```

Denotes the plastic stress. This operand is linked to the combination of the cohesion coefficient $C$ with the friction angle $\varphi$ in the following way: $S=\frac{6C\cos(\varphi )}{3-\sin(\varphi )}$ .

#### 10.13.5 Operand H

```text
H =    h
```

Denotes the hardening modulus, $h<0$ if the law is softening. This operand is mandatory for hardening of linear type (operand ECROUISSAGE =‘LINEAIRE‘) .

#### 10.13.6 Operand SY_ULTM

```text
SY_ULTM =    sy_ult
```

Denotes the ultimate stress. This operand is mandatory for hardening of parabolic type (operand ECROUISSAGE =‘PARABOLIQUE‘ and ‘EXPONENTIEL') .

#### 10.13.7 Operand DILAT

```text
DILAT = beta_0
```

Denotes the initial dilatancy (for the non-associated law DRUCK_PRAG_N_A) . One recalls that the operand DILAT is related to the dilatancy angle ${\psi}_{0}$ by the relation: ${\beta}_{0}=\frac{2.\sin({\psi}_{0})}{3-\sin({\psi}_{0})}$

#### 10.13.8 Operand DILAT_ULTM

```text
DILAT_ULTM = beta_ult
```

Denotes the final dilatancy (for the non-associated law DRUCK_PRAG_N_A and ECROUISSAGE =‘EXPONENTIEL‘).

### 10.14 Factor keyword VISC_DRUC_PRAG

The rheological model VISC_DRUC_PRAG is an elasto-viscoplastic constitutive law [R7.01.22](doc:r7.01.22). It is characterised by a viscoplastic mechanism which hardens between three thresholds: elastic, peak and ultimate. The elastoplasticity is of Drucker-Prager type with positive hardening in the pre-peak regime and negative hardening in the post-peak regime, and the viscoplasticity is a power law of Perzyna type.

Among the parameters one finds:

- parameters which appear in the hardening functions relating to the various elastic, peak and ultimate thresholds, " $\alpha$ ", " $R$ " and " $\beta$ " ,
- parameters linked to the creep law, " $A$ " and " $n$ ",
- the cumulative viscoplastic strains corresponding to each of the thresholds ${p}_{\mathit{pic}}$ and ${p}_{\mathit{ult}}$ ;
- a reference pressure " ${P}_{\mathit{ref}}$ "

The elastic characteristics must be defined under the keyword ELAS.

#### 10.14.1 Operands PREF/N/A/P_PIC/P_ULT

```text
PREF     =     pref
```

Parameter ${P}_{\mathrm{ref}}$ : reference pressure (unit of a stress)

```text
N     =    n
```

Parameter $n$ : exponent of the creep law

```text
A     =     a
```

Parameter $A$ : viscoplastic parameter (in ${s}^{-1}$ or ${\mathrm{jour}}^{-1}$ )

```text
P_PIC     =     ppic
```

Parameter ${p}_{\mathrm{pic}}$ : cumulative viscoplastic strain at the level of the peak threshold

```text
P_ULT     =     pult
```

Parameter ${p}_{\mathrm{ult}}$ : cumulative viscoplastic strain at the level of the ultimate threshold

#### 10.14.2 Operands ALPHA_0/ALPHA_PIC/ALPHA_ULT

```text
ALPHA_0 =     alpha0
```

Parameter ${\alpha}_{0}$ : parameter of the cohesion function $\alpha (p)$ at the level of the elastic threshold

```text
ALPHA_PIC =     alphapic
```

Parameter ${\alpha}_{\mathrm{pic}}$ : parameter of the cohesion function $\alpha (p)$ at the level of the peak threshold

```text
ALPHA_ULT =     alphault
```

Parameter ${\alpha}_{\mathrm{ult}}$ : parameter of the cohesion function $\alpha (p)$ at the level of the ultimate threshold

#### 10.14.3 Operands R_0/R_PIC/R_ULT

```text
R_0 =     r0
```

Parameter ${R}_{0}$ : parameter of the hardening function $R(p)$ at the level of the elastic threshold (in units of stress $\mathit{Pa}$ or $\mathit{MPa}$ )

```text
R_PIC =     rpic
```

Parameter ${R}_{\mathrm{pic}}$ : parameter of the hardening function $R(p)$ at the level of the peak threshold (in units of stress $\mathit{Pa}$ or $\mathit{MPa}$ )

```text
R_ULT =     rult
```

Parameter ${R}_{\mathrm{ult}}$ : parameter of the hardening function $R(p)$ at the level of the ultimate threshold (in units of stress $\mathit{Pa}$ or $\mathit{MPa}$ )

#### 10.14.4 Operands BETA_0/BETA_PIC/BETA_ULT

```text
BETA_0     =     beta0
```

Parameter ${\beta}_{0}$ : parameter of the dilatancy function $\beta (p)$ at the level of the elastic threshold

```text
BETA_PIC =     betapic
```

Parameter ${\beta}_{\mathrm{pic}}$ : parameter of the dilatancy function $\beta (p)$ at the level of the peak threshold

```text
BETA_ULT =     betault
```

Parameter ${\beta}_{\mathrm{ult}}$ : parameter of the dilatancy function $\beta (p)$ at the level of the ultimate threshold

### 10.15 Factor keyword BARCELONE

The Barcelona model describes the elastoplastic constitutive behaviour of unsaturated soils coupled to the hydraulic constitutive behaviour (cf. [R7.01.17](doc:r7.01.17) for more detail). This model reduces to the Cam-Clay model in the saturated case. Two criteria are involved: a mechanical plasticity criterion (that of Cam-Clay) and a hydraulic criterion controlled by the suction (or capillary pressure). It can be used only within the framework of the THHM and HHM constitutive behaviours. The characteristics required for the model must be given under this keyword and under the keywords CAM_CLAY and ELAS.

It is therefore mandatory to supply the parameters of the keywords CAM_CLAY and ELAS.

#### 10.15.1 Operands MU/PORO/LAMBDA/KAPA/M

```text
MU    = mu
```

Elastic shear modulus.

```text
PORO =    poro
```

Porosity associated with an initial pressure and linked to the initial void ratio: $n=\frac{{e}_{0}}{1+{e}_{0}}$ .

```text
LAMBDA    = lambda
```

Compressibility coefficient (plastic slope in a hydrostatic compression test).

```text
KAPA =    kapa
```

Elastic swelling coefficient (elastic slope in a hydrostatic compression test).

```text
M =    m
```

Slope of the critical-state line.

#### 10.15.2 Operands PRES_CRIT and PA

```text
PRES_CRIT    = pc,PA =    pa
```

Critical pressure equal to half the consolidation pressure, and atmospheric pressure.

#### 10.15.3 Operands R/BETA/KC

```text
R =    r, BETA =    beta
```

Dimensionless coefficients appearing in the expression: $\lambda ({p}_{c})=\lambda (0)\left[(1-r)exp(-\beta {p}_{c})+r\right]$

```text
KC=    kc
```

Dimensionless parameter controlling the increase of the cohesion with the suction (capillary pressure).

#### 10.15.4 Operands PCO_INIT/KAPAS/LAMBDAS/ALPHAB

```text
PC0_INIT    = Pc0(0)
```

Initial threshold of the capillary pressure (homogeneous to stresses).

```text
KAPAS    = Kappas
```

Dimensionless stiffness coefficient associated with the change of suction in the elastic domain.

```text
LAMBDAS =    Lambdas
```

Compressibility coefficient linked to a variation of suction in the plastic domain. (dimensionless).

```text
ALPHAB    = alphab
```

Coefficient of correction of the normality of the plastic flow, cf. [R7.01.17](doc:r7.01.17).

Optional and dimensionless corrective term making it possible to take experimental results better into account. By default, it is computed as a function of the slope of the critical-state line, of the swelling coefficient and of the compressibility coefficient.

### 10.16 Factor keyword HUJEUX

Elastoplastic constitutive law in soil mechanics (granular geomaterials: sandy clays, normally consolidated or over-consolidated, gravels…). This model is a multi-criterion model which comprises a non-linear elastic mechanism, 3 deviatoric plastic mechanisms and one isotropic plastic mechanism (see [R7.01.23](doc:r7.01.23)).

The elastic mechanical characteristics E, NU and ALPHA must be defined in parallel under the keyword ELAS. The Hujeux law exhibiting a non-linear elastic constitutive behaviour, the values of these parameters are associated with the reference pressure PREF of the Hujeux law.

#### 10.16.1 Operands N/BETA/B/D/PHI

```text
N =     n
```

Value of the parameter characteristic of the non-linear elastic power law, lying between 0 and 1.

```text
BETA =    beta
```

Value of the coefficient of plastic volumetric compressibility or of the critical-state law, (positive).

```text
B=    b
```

Value of the parameter influencing the loading function in the plane $(P',Q)$ , lying between 0 (Mohr-Coulomb) and 1 (Cam-Clay).

```text
D = d
```

Value of the parameter characterising the distance between the critical-state line and the isotropic consolidation line, (positive).

```text
PHI     =    phi
```

Value of the parameter characterising the internal friction angle, in degrees.

#### 10.16.2 Operands ANGDIL/PCO/PREF

```text
ANGDIL     =    angdil
```

Value of the parameter characterising the dilatancy angle, in degrees.

```text
PCO     =    pco
```

Value of the initial reference critical pressure, (negative).

```text
PREF =    pref
```

Value of the reference confining pressure, (negative).

Operands ACYC/AMON/CCYC/CMON

A.. code-block:: text

> CYC = acyc,AMON = amon,CCYC = ccyc,CMON = cmon

Values of the hardening parameters of the deviatoric plastic mechanisms, in cyclic and in monotonic loading, and of the plastic consolidation mechanisms, in cyclic and in monotonic loading, respectively.

#### 10.16.3 Operands RD_ELA/RI_ELA

```text
RD_ELA = rdela, RI_ELA = riela,
```

Values of the initial radii of the thresholds of the monotonic deviatoric mechanism and of the monotonic consolidation mechanism, respectively, lying between 0 and 1.

```text
RD_ELA = rdela, RI_ELA = riela,
```

Values of the initial radii of the thresholds of the monotonic deviatoric mechanism and of the monotonic consolidation mechanism, respectively, lying between 0 and 1.

#### 10.16.4 Operands RD_CYC/RI_CYC

```text
RD_CYC = rdcyc, RI_CYC = ricyc
```

Values of the initial radii of the thresholds of the cyclic deviatoric mechanism and of the cyclic consolidation mechanism, respectively, lying between 0 and 1.

#### 10.16.5 Operands RHYS/RMOB/XM/DILA/PTRAC

```text
RHYS = rhys
```

Value of the parameter defining the size of the hysteretic domain.

```text
RMOB = rmob
```

Value of the parameter defining the size of the mobilised domain.

```text
XM = xm
```

Value of the control parameter in the hysteretic domain.

```text
DILA = dila
```

Value of the dilatancy coefficient, lying between 0 and 1.

```text
PTRAC = ptrac
```

cohesion of the material, homogeneous to a stress (positive or zero value). Makes it possible to shift the loading surface towards $p>0$ in order to take a slight tension in the material into account.

Constitutive law in rock mechanics of modified HOEK-BROWN law type (cf. [R7.01.18](doc:r7.01.18)

The elastic mechanical characteristics E, NU and ALPHA must be defined in parallel under the keyword ELAS.
