### 10.18 Factor keyword GonfElas

Constitutive law in rock mechanics — written under MFront — making it possible to describe the constitutive behaviour of materials of the "swelling clay" type (bentonite). This model was developed at the LAEGO. It is a non-linear elastic model relating the net stress to the swelling pressure, which itself depends on the suction (or capillary pressure). It can be used only within the framework of the THHM and HHM constitutive behaviours.

The elastic mechanical characteristics E, NU and ALPHA must be defined in parallel under the keyword ELAS.

The GonfElas law is a constitutive model for swelling clays (bentonite type); it is defined by the relation:

$d\tilde{\sigma}={K}_{0}d{\varepsilon}_{V}+b(1+\frac{s}{A}){e}^{-{\beta}_{m}{(\frac{s}{A})}^{2}}\mathrm{ds}$

with $\tilde{\sigma}$ : net stress (trace) $\sigma =\tilde{\sigma}-{p}_{g}$ .

| In the saturated domain: | $d\tilde{\sigma}={K}_{0}d{\varepsilon}_{V}-{\mathrm{bdp}}_{w}+{\mathrm{dp}}_{g}$ |
| --- | --- |
| Or alternatively: | $d\tilde{\sigma}={K}_{0}d{\varepsilon}_{V}-{\mathrm{bdp}}_{c}+(1-b){\mathrm{dp}}_{g}$ |

${K}_{0}$ is the bulk modulus of the material

$b$ is the Biot coefficient

$A$ is a parameter homogeneous to a pressure

${\beta}_{m}$ is a dimensionless parameter

$s$ the suction (or capillary pressure)

From there, the identification is carried out by seeking the swelling pressure.

Let ${P}_{\mathrm{gf}}$ be the expected swelling pressure and let ${P}_{\mathrm{gf}}({s}_{0})$ be the swelling pressure found by the model when one re-saturates a specimen in a test at blocked strain and starting from a suction ${s}_{0}$ .

It is easy to see that: $\frac{{P}_{\mathit{gf}}({s}_{0})}{A}=\frac{\sqrt{\pi}}{2\sqrt{{\beta}_{m}}}\mathit{Erf}\left(\frac{{s}_{0}}{A}\sqrt{{\beta}_{m}}\right)+\frac{1}{2{\beta}_{m}}\left(\begin{array}{c}1-{e}^{-{\beta}_{m}{\left(\frac{{s}_{0}}{A}\right)}^{2}}\\ \phantom{\rule{2em}{0ex}}\end{array}\right)$

One must have ${P}_{\mathrm{gf}}={P}_{\mathrm{gf}}^{\infty}$ . One knows that $\mathrm{Erf}(\infty )=1$ and therefore: $\frac{{P}_{\mathit{gf}}({s}_{0})}{A}=\frac{\sqrt{\pi}}{2\sqrt{{\beta}_{m}}}+\frac{1}{2{\beta}_{m}}$

For more details, one will refer to [R7.01.41](doc:r7.01.41).

#### 10.18.1 Operand YoungModulus and PoissonRatio

Classical elasticity parameters (Young's modulus and Poisson's ratio).

#### 10.18.2 Operand BiotCoef

Biot coefficient. Take care to use the same one as that supplied in the THM module.

#### 10.18.3 Operand betaM

Dimensionless material parameter corresponding to the ${\beta}_{m}$ of the law above. The identification is carried out by seeking the swelling pressure.

#### 10.18.4 Operand ReferencePressure

Parameter homogeneous to a pressure corresponding to the $A$ of the law above.

### 10.19 Factor keyword JOINT_BANDIS

Constitutive law of a hydraulic joint in rock mechanics. In the direction normal to the joint, the constitutive behaviour is given by

$d\sigma {'}_{n}=-{K}_{ni}\frac{\mathrm{dU}}{{(1-\frac{U}{{U}_{\max}})}^{\gamma}}$

$\sigma {'}_{n}$ is the effective normal stress.

${K}_{ni}$ is the initial normal stiffness.

$U$ is the crack closure (opening at zero loading minus the current opening).

${U}_{\max}$ is the asymptotic closure of the crack (at infinite stress).

$\gamma$ is a material parameter.

In the tangential direction, the constitutive behaviour is linear elastic:

$\sigma {'}_{t}={K}_{t}[[{u}_{t}]]$

#### 10.19.1 Operand K

Normal stiffness at zero loading ${K}_{ni}$ (stress per unit length).

#### 10.19.2 Operand DMAX

Asymptotic closure ${D}_{\max}$ (length).

#### 10.19.3 Operand GAMA

Dimensionless material parameter $\gamma$ .

#### 10.19.4 Operand KT

Tangential stiffness ${K}_{t}$ (stress per unit length).

### 10.20 Factor keyword THM_RUPT

Constitutive law for cracks with hydro-mechanical coupling (see [R7.02.15](doc:r7.02.15)).

When the rock masses surrounding the crack are impermeable, the flow is no longer well defined on the unopened joint finite elements. In this case, one replaces the displacement jump by a fictitious crack opening ${\varepsilon}_{\text{fict}}$ which makes it possible to regularise the flow and to transfer to the crack tip the boundary condition written at the end of the cracking path.

One may also define a Biot modulus $N$ for the cohesive zone.

#### 10.20.1 Operand OUV_FICT

Fictitious crack opening ${\varepsilon}_{\text{fict}}$ (length).

#### 10.20.2 Operand UN_SUR_N

Inverse of the Biot modulus of the crack $N$ (stress per unit length).

### 10.21 Factor keyword Iwan

Elastoplastic constitutive law in soil mechanics adapted to cyclic deviatoric constitutive behaviour. The constitutive law of Iwan [R7.01.38](doc:r7.01.38) makes it possible to reproduce the degradation curves of the shear modulus. The law is calibrated from the parameters of a hyperbolic model of the form:

$\frac{\text{G}}{{\text{G}}_{max}}=\frac{1}{1+{\left(\frac{\text{γ}}{{\text{γ}}_{\mathit{ref}}}\right)}^{n}}$

The elastic mechanical characteristics E and NU must be defined in parallel under the keyword ELAS. The values under the keyword ELAS will be compared with those entered under the keyword Iwan. If they are different, a fatal error will be issued.

If the keyword ELAS is not supplied, this will be done automatically by taking the elastic characteristics of the keyword Iwan.

#### 10.21.1 Operand YoungModulus

Young's modulus.

#### 10.21.2 Operand PoissonRatio

Poisson's ratio.

#### 10.21.3 Operand HypDistorsion

Value of the shear strain ${\text{γ}}_{\mathit{ref}}=2{\text{ε}}_{ij}$ of the hyperbolic law. It is the value for which $\text{G}={\text{G}}_{max}/2$ on the constitutive curve.

#### 10.21.4 Operand HypExponent

Exponent of the hyperbolic law.

### 10.22 Factor keyword LKR

The *LKR* model (Laigle, Kleine and Raude) is a thermo-elasto-(visco)plastic constitutive law [R7.01.40](doc:r7.01.40). It relies on concepts drawn from the theories of elastoplasticity and of viscoplasticity. The plastic mechanism is characterised by positive hardening in the pre-peak regime and negative hardening in the post-peak regime. Temperature influences the plastic and viscoplastic hardenings.

The elastic characteristics must be defined under the keyword ELAS.

The reader will refer to the reference documentation [R7.01.40](doc:r7.01.40) for the meaning, the intervals of definition and the default values (if the parameter is optional) of each parameter specific to the factor keyword LKR, whose syntax is detailed at the beginning of the document.

### 10.23 Factor keyword MohrCoulombAS

Elastoplastic constitutive law in soil mechanics (granular geomaterials) adapted to monotonic loadings, written under MFront. This elastoplastic constitutive law with a smoothed Mohr-Coulomb loading surface [R7.01.43](doc:r7.01.43) makes it possible, for example, to analyse the bearing capacity of a geotechnical structure. The proposed smoothing of the Mohr-Coulomb criterion, compared with the original law [R7.01.28](doc:r7.01.28), is intended to increase the robustness of the implicit numerical integration. The data required for the material field also include the smoothing parameters. The elastic mechanical characteristics E and NU must be defined in parallel under the keyword ELAS. The values under the keyword ELAS will be compared with those entered under the keyword MohrCoulombAS. If they are different, a fatal error will be issued.

If the keyword ELAS is not supplied, this will be done automatically by taking the elastic characteristics of the keyword MohrCoulombAS.

#### 10.23.1 Operands

##### 10.23.1.1 Operand YoungModulus

Young's modulus.

##### 10.23.1.2 Operand PoissonRatio

Poisson's ratio.

##### 10.23.1.3 Operand Cohesion

Cohesion (positive scalar).

##### 10.23.1.4 Operand FrictionAngle

Friction angle ${\varphi}_{\mathit{pp}}$ (supplied in °).

##### 10.23.1.5 Operand DilatancyAngle

Dilatancy angle $\psi$ (supplied in °), which must be less than or equal to the internal friction angle ${\varphi}_{\mathit{pp}}$ . When $\psi ={\varphi}_{\mathit{pp}}$ , the plastic flow law becomes associated.

##### 10.23.1.6 Operand TransitionAngle

Parameter associated with the smoothing: transition Lode angle ${\theta}_{T}$ (supplied in °). It must be strictly less than 30°. A typical value may be taken equal to 25°.

##### 10.23.1.7 Operand TensionCutOff

Positive parameter associated with the smoothing: tension cut-off ${a}_{\mathit{tt}}$ . If ${a}_{\mathit{tt}}=0$ , the criterion recovers the apex of the Mohr-Coulomb pyramid, and therefore it is no longer smoothed.

##### 10.23.1.8 Operand HardeningCoef

Positive or zero parameter describing the hardening acting on the cohesion as a function of the equivalent plastic strain. If it is strictly positive, one will in general take a small value if the aim is to reduce the unstable response regime, cf. [R7.01.43](doc:r7.01.43).

### 10.24 Factor keyword NLH_CSRM

The constitutive law NLH_CSRM makes it possible to model the elasto-viscoplastic constitutive behaviour of cohesive geomaterials. It will eventually replace the laws LETK and LKR.

The scope of use of the model NLH_CSRM is for the moment limited to the **isotropic mechanical** constitutive behaviour. Taking **structural anisotropy** into account and the dependence of the hardenings on **temperature** — effective in the latest version of the LKR model — will be the subject of forthcoming updates; likewise the dependence of the criteria on the third invariant of the stress deviator (Lode angle).

The model NLH_CSRM falls within the **thermodynamic framework of generalised standard materials** with two irreversible mechanisms,

- **Plastic mechanism** — **instantaneous** irreversible constitutive behaviour: hardening and/or softening, contracting or dilating volumetric constitutive behaviour, influence of the mean stress on the brittle character of the response in compression,…;
- **Viscoplastic mechanism** — **delayed** irreversible constitutive behaviour (of Perzyna type): primary, secondary and tertiary creep (failure).

A **coupling between these two mechanisms** makes it possible — among other things — to represent the dependence of the maximum strength of certain geomaterials on the loading rate.

This law has been integrated implicitly via the Mfront tool. Its reference documentation bears the key [r7.01.46](doc:r7.01.46).

YoungModulus and PoissonRatio are also to be supplied in the factor keyword ELAS.

### 10.25 Factor keyword MCC

The constitutive model MCC makes it possible to represent qualitatively the phenomenology of granular soils under monotonic loadings, taking into account the effects of dilatancy/contractancy, of softening/hardening, and of the critical state. The MCC model falls within the framework of generalised standard materials. It updates the formulation and the numerical solution method of the law CAM_CLAY [R7.01.14](doc:r7.01.14), while remaining faithful to the elements of phenomenology predicted by the latter, in order, eventually, to replace it.

The parameters of the MCC model are seven in total.
Three define the isotropic non-linear elastic constitutive behaviour; three represent the initial reversibility domain. A combined kinematic-isotropic hardening carried by the volumetric plastic strain is governed by a last parameter.

The constitutive equations are solved using a direct implicit time-integration method, implemented in MFront.

The reference documentation of the MCC model bears the key [R7.01.48](doc:r7.01.48).

### 10.26 Factor keyword CSSM

The constitutive model CSSM ("Critical State Soil Model") is a combination of two models aimed at representing respectively the monotonic and the cyclic drained constitutive behaviour of soils. The first component relies on the modified Cam-Clay model, which describes the phenomena of dilatancy or contractancy as well as confinement effects under monotonic and drained loadings. The second component is a multi-mechanism model of Iwan type,
designed to represent the cyclic non-linear constitutive behaviour of soils at low strain levels. The model complies with the framework of generalised standard materials.

The constitutive model CSSM comprises a total of eleven parameters: two to describe the reversible (elastic) constitutive behaviour, five for the irreversible constitutive behaviour linked to the modified Cam-Clay component, and three for the irreversible constitutive behaviour linked to the Iwan component. A last parameter weights the contributions of the two components to the shear constitutive behaviour of the CSSM model.

The constitutive equations are solved using a direct implicit time-integration method, implemented in MFront.

The reference documentation of the CSSM model bears the key [R7.01.49](doc:r7.01.49).

## 11 Constitutive behaviours specific to 1D elements

It makes it possible to model a constitutive behaviour with linear isotropic hardening, but with different elastic limits and hardening moduli in tension and in compression. This is used by the 1D constitutive model VMIS_ASYM_LINE, usable for bar elements.

The elastic constitutive behaviour in tension and in compression is the same: the same Young's modulus.

There are two isotropic-hardening domains defined by ${R}_{T}$ and ${R}_{C}$ . The two domains are independent of one another. We adopt an index $T$ for tension and $C$ for compression.

| ${\sigma}_{\mathrm{YT}}$ | Limit force in tension. In absolute value. |
| --- | --- |
| ${\sigma}_{\mathrm{YC}}$ | Limit force in compression. In absolute value. |
| ${p}_{T}$ | Cumulative plastic strain in tension. Algebraic value. |
| ${p}_{C}$ | Cumulative plastic strain in compression. Algebraic value. |
| ${E}_{\mathrm{TT}}$ | Hardening slope in tension. |
| ${E}_{\mathrm{TC}}$ | Hardening slope in compression. |

The equations of the constitutive model are:

$\lbrace \begin{array}{c}{\dot{\epsilon}}^{p}=\dot{\epsilon}-\stackrel{.}{\stackrel{⏞}{{E}^{-1}\sigma }}-{\dot{\epsilon}}^{\mathit{th}}\\ {\dot{\epsilon}}^{p}={\dot{\epsilon}}_{C}^{p}+{\dot{\epsilon}}_{T}^{p}\hfill \\ {\dot{\epsilon}}_{C}^{p}=\dot{{p}_{C}}\frac{\sigma}{\left|\sigma \right|}\hfill \\ \sigma -{R}_{T}({p}_{T})\le 0\hfill \\ -\sigma -{R}_{C}({p}_{C})\le 0\hfill \end{array}$         $\begin{array}{c}\text{avec}\hfill \\ (\begin{array}{c}\dot{{p}_{C}}=0\text{si}-\sigma -{R}_{C}({p}_{C})<0\hfill \\ \dot{{p}_{C}}\ge 0\text{si}-\sigma ={R}_{C}({p}_{C})\hfill \\ \dot{{p}_{T}}=0\text{si}\sigma -{R}_{T}({p}_{T})<0\hfill \\ \dot{{p}_{T}}\ge 0\text{si}\sigma ={R}_{T}({p}_{T})\hfill \end{array}\end{array}$



where:

${\dot{\varepsilon}}_{C}^{p}$ : plastic strain rate in compression,

${\dot{\varepsilon}}_{T}^{p}$ : plastic strain rate in tension.

${\varepsilon}_{\mathrm{th}}$ : strain of thermal origin: ${\varepsilon}_{\mathrm{th}}=\alpha (T-{T}_{\mathrm{ref}})$ . $\alpha$ is defined under ELAS.

One notes that one cannot have plastification in tension and in compression simultaneously: either $\dot{{p}_{C}}=0$ , or $\dot{{p}_{T}}=0$ , or both are zero.

## 12 Parameters for particular constitutive behaviours

### 12.1 Factor keyword LEMAITRE_IRRA

Characteristics (specific to irradiation) of the creep of fuel rods or assemblies (constitutive behaviour LEMAITRE_IRRA).
The elastic characteristics must be defined under the keyword ELAS or ELAS_FO.

The uniaxial form of the growth law is:

${\varepsilon}_{g}(t)=f(T,{\Phi}_{t})$

where $f$ is a function of the temperature $T$ expressed in $°C$ and of the fluence ${\Phi}_{t}$ expressed in ${10}^{24}\mathit{neutrons}/{m}^{2}$.

For the 2D and 3D modellings, the growth law is written (cf. [R5.03.08](doc:r5.03.08)):

${\varepsilon}_{g}(t)=f(T,{\Phi}_{t}){\varepsilon}_{g}^{0}$

with: ${\epsilon}_{g}^{0}={\left(\begin{array}{ccc}1& 0& 0\\ 0& 0& 0\\ 0& 0& 0\end{array}\right)}_{{R}_{1}}$

One must then define, with the help of the operand ANGL_REP of the keyword MASSIF of the operator [AFFE_CARA_ELEM](doc:u4.42.01), the local axes corresponding to the frame ${R}_{1}$ (see [U4.42.01](doc:u4.42.01)). This operand expects 3 nautical angles, of which only the first 2 are used (the third may therefore be arbitrary).

The growth parameters are supplied under the keyword GRAN_FO.

One supplies the four keywords QSR_K, BETA, PHI_ZERO, L (the other creep parameters are identical to those of the constitutive behaviour LEMAITRE) and the creep constitutive behaviour is then as follows:

$\begin{array}{ccc}\dot{p}={\left[\frac{{\sigma}_{\mathrm{eq}}}{{p}^{1/m}}\right]}^{n}{(\frac{1}{K}\frac{\Phi}{{\Phi}_{0}}+L)}^{\beta}{e}^{\frac{-Q}{R(T+{T}_{0})}}& \text{}& ({T}_{O}=273,15°)\end{array}$

where $\Phi ` is the neutron flux computed from the fluence (see [:ref:`R5.03.08<r5.03.08>$]). $T$ is in $°C$.

In the case where one wishes the constitutive behaviour not to depend on the fluence, but nevertheless to include the term in $\exp(-Q/\mathrm{RT})$, it is possible to use the keyword LEMAITRE_IRRA in STAT_NON_LINE by supplying the keyword LEMAITRE_IRRA in [DEFI_MATERIAU](doc:u4.43.01). One must then imperatively assign UN_SUR_K, A, B, S to zero and PHI_ZERO to one. Under these conditions, it is not necessary to define a fluence field.

### 12.2 Factor keyword DIS_GRICRA

This keyword makes it possible to define the parameters associated with the non-linear constitutive behaviour of the connection between the grid and the rod in a fuel assembly modelled by a discrete element (cf. [R5.03.17](doc:r5.03.17)). The constitutive behaviour usable in the commands [STAT_NON_LINE](doc:u4.51.03) and [DYNA_NON_LINE](doc:u4.53.01) from these parameters is DIS_GRICRA.

The input parameters of this law are the following:

- Constitutive behaviour in axial sliding: 5 parameters (of which 2 optional parameters, purely numerical)

  1. normal stiffness of the discrete element KN_AX;
  2. tangential stiffness (in the sliding direction) KT_AX;
  3. Coulomb friction coefficient COUL_AX;
  4. clamping force F_SER (sliding limit = COUL_AX x F_SER);
  5. hardening parameter ET_AX; the constitutive law may be likened to perfect plasticity. The hardening parameter serves only to ensure convergence of the analysis and is optional, with a default value of ${10}^{-7}$;
- Constitutive behaviour in rotation: 8 parameters (of which one purely numerical parameter)

  1. successive slopes PEN1, PEN2, PEN3 and PEN4 of the curve $Moment = f(angle)$;
  2. angles ANG1, ANG2 and ANG3 of the inflection points of the curve;
  3. hardening parameter ET_ROT (parameter serving only to ensure convergence of the analysis; a default value of ${10}^{-7}$ is assigned to it).
- Elastic constitutive behaviour in parallel: 2 or 3 parameters

  - KP: Normal stiffness (that is, along the axis $x$ of the local frame of the element) of an elastic spring placed in parallel with the discrete element (optional parameter).
  - KT1: Tangential stiffness (along the axis $y$ of the local frame of the element) of an elastic spring placed in parallel with the discrete element (optional parameter).
  - KT2: Tangential stiffness (along the axis $z$ of the local frame of the element) of an elastic spring placed in parallel with the discrete element (optional parameter).
  - KT: Tangential stiffness (along the axes $y$ and $z$ of the local frame of the element) of an elastic spring placed in parallel with the discrete element (optional parameter). If this keyword is present, the keywords KT1 and KT2 must not be used (and conversely), and one then has KT1=KT2=KT.

The clamping forces may vary as a function of temperature and of irradiation. These dependencies are assigned to the slopes PEN1, PEN2 and PEN3 for the constitutive behaviour in rotation and to the clamping force F_SER for the constitutive behaviour in axial sliding. The dependency functions are defined directly in the form of a FORMULE in the command file.

- constitutive behaviour relying on a discrete element with 2 nodes (modelling `DIS_TR`) with degrees of freedom in translation and in rotation
- contact with Coulomb friction for the translational degrees of freedom, modelled by an elastoplastic model
- non-linear constitutive law in rotation based on geometric and physical considerations (cf. [R5.03.17](doc:r5.03.17))

The names of the parameters followed by the suffix _FO make it possible to supply the value in the form of a function.

A certain number of additional parameters, available for this constitutive behaviour but which do not appear in the present document, are explained in [V6.04.131](doc:v6.04.131).

![Figure](/figures/u4.43.01/10000000000002C00000013D7C38966E02944D08.png)

### 12.3 Factor keyword DIS_CONTACT

This keyword makes it possible to define the parameters associated with the constitutive behaviours DIS_CHOC and DIS_CONTACT, non-linear impact behaviours with Coulomb friction associated with the discrete elements (cf. [R5.03.17](doc:r5.03.17)) for modellings `DIS_T`, `DIS_TR`, `2D_DIS_T`, `2D_DIS_TR` relying on mesh cells `POI1` or `SEG2` (discrete element with 1 or 2 nodes).

#### 12.3.1 Operands

**RIGI_NOR = Kn**

Value of the normal impact stiffness. If `RIGI_NOR` is present, it is this value which is taken into account. If it is not present, the discrete elements to which this material is assigned must have their stiffness defined elsewhere (for example with the help of the command [AFFE_CARA_ELEM](doc:u4.42.01) with the keywords `DISCRET`, `2D_DISCRET` or RIGI_PARASOL).

**RIGI_TAN = Kt**

Value of the tangential impact stiffness.

**AMOR_NOR = Cn**

Value of the normal impact damping.

**AMOR_TAN = Ct**

Value of the tangential impact damping.

**COULOMB = mu**

Value of the friction coefficient.

**DIST_1 = dist1**

Characteristic distance of material surrounding the first impact node.

**DIST_2 = dist2**

Characteristic distance of material surrounding the second impact node (impact between two moving structures).

**JEU = d0**

Distance between the impact node and an obstacle which is not modelled (case of an impact between a moving structure and a rigid and fixed obstacle).

**KP = kp**

Normal stiffness (that is, along the axis $x$ of the local frame of the element) of an elastic spring placed in parallel with the discrete element (optional parameter).

**KT1 = kt1**

Tangential stiffness (along the axis $y$ of the local frame of the element) of an elastic spring placed in parallel with the discrete element (optional parameter).

**KT2 = kt2**

Tangential stiffness (along the axis $z$ of the local frame of the element) of an elastic spring placed in parallel with the discrete element (optional parameter).

**KT = kt**

Tangential stiffness (along the axes $y$ and $z$ of the local frame of the element) of an elastic spring placed in parallel with the discrete element (optional parameter). If this keyword is present, the keywords KT1 and KT2 must not be used (and conversely), and one then has KT1=KT2=KT.

**INST_COMP_INIT = (t0,t1)**

When one uses the constitutive behaviour DIS_CONTACT, cf. [R5.03.17](doc:r5.03.17), and the discrete element is initially compressed and in contact, it may be necessary, in order to start from a balanced state, to simulate this progressive application of compression. This keyword makes it possible to define a ramp; if `INST` is the computation time instant:
$\min\left( \max\left(0.0, (INST-t_0)∕(t_1-t_0)\right),1.0 \right)$.
The value obtained multiplies the distance between the nodes or that between the node and the obstacle. The distance therefore varies between 0 at ${t}_{0}$ and its final value at ${t}_{1}$. During this phase friction is not activated. At the end of this phase, if the discrete element is compressed, the Coulomb threshold is $\mu .N$ where $\mathit{Fx}$ is the compressive force in the discrete element.

**CONTACT = ['1D'|'COIN_2D']**

Makes it possible to define the frame in which the contact is treated. By default the `CONTACT` is managed in `1D` (along the local axis of the discrete element).
The option `COIN_2D` makes it possible to change the mode of computation of the interpenetration of the 2 solids. In this case this interpenetration is computed in the global frame, and it is no longer a norm.
Cf. [R5.03.17](doc:r5.03.17)

**PRECISION = ( yz, xz, xy ) 3 mandatory values.**

When CONTACT='COIN_2D', it is possible to define a precision. It is used to detect the non-flatness of the discrete element, and is to be given in the unit of the mesh.
The 3 values given under the keyword make it possible to carry out the flatness test of the discrete element, respectively in the planes YZ, XZ, XY. The test is therefore carried out to within a precision, which may be different along the axes. An error is triggered: if the plane of the discrete element is not found, if the discrete element is detected in several planes or if the discrete element lies along an axis.

### 12.4 Factor keyword DIS_CHOC_ENDO

This keyword makes it possible to define the parameters associated with the non-linear impact constitutive behaviours CHOC_ENDO and CHOC_ENDO_PENA associated with the discrete elements for modellings `DIS_T` relying on mesh cells `SEG2` (discrete element with 2 nodes).

#### 12.4.1 Operands

**FX = fx**

Threshold function depending on the relative displacement of the nodes of the discrete element.

**RIGI_NOR = rn**

Function describing the variation of the stiffness depending on the relative displacement of the nodes of the discrete element. This function is mandatorily decreasing.

**AMOR_NOR = an**

Function describing the damping depending on the relative displacement of the nodes of the discrete element.

In every case the damping is taken into account in the force (cf. [R5.03.17](doc:r5.03.17)).

**CRIT_AMOR= ['INCLUS'|'EXCLUS']**

The structural damping may be taken into account in 2 different ways. Either it is taken into account in the computation of the threshold, in which case the force in the discrete element (if the damping is different from 0) is always less than the threshold. One therefore has a reduction of the threshold as a function of the impact velocity. Or it is excluded from the computation of the threshold, in which case the force in the discrete element may be equal to the threshold.

In every case the damping is taken into account in the force (cf. [R5.03.17](doc:r5.03.17)).

**DIST_1 = dist1**

Characteristic distance of material surrounding the first impact node (impact between two moving structures).

**DIST_2 = dist2**

Characteristic distance of material surrounding the second impact node (impact between two moving structures).

**KP = kp**

Normal stiffness (that is, along the axis $x$ of the local frame of the element) of an elastic spring placed in parallel with the discrete element (optional parameter).

**KT1 = kt1**

Tangential stiffness (along the axis $y$ of the local frame of the element) of an elastic spring placed in parallel with the discrete element (optional parameter).

**KT2 = kt2**

Tangential stiffness (along the axis $z$ of the local frame of the element) of an elastic spring placed in parallel with the discrete element (optional parameter).

**KT = kt**

Tangential stiffness (along the axes $y$ and $z$ of the local frame of the element) of an elastic spring placed in parallel with the discrete element (optional parameter). If this keyword is present, the keywords KT1 and KT2 must not be used (and conversely), and one then has KT1=KT2=KT.

### 12.5 Factor keyword DIS_CHOC_ELAS

This keyword makes it possible to define the parameters associated with the non-linear impact constitutive behaviours CHOC_ELAS_TRAC associated with the discrete elements (cf. [R5.03.17](doc:r5.03.17)) for modellings DIS_T relying on mesh cells SEG2 (discrete element with 2 nodes).

#### 12.5.1 Operands

**FX = fx**

Non-linear function depending on the relative displacement of the nodes of the discrete element.

**DIST_1 = dist1**

Characteristic distance of material surrounding the first impact node (impact between two moving structures).

**DIST_2 = dist2**

Characteristic distance of material surrounding the second impact node (impact between two moving structures).

**KP = kp**

Normal stiffness (that is, along the axis $x$ of the local frame of the element) of an elastic spring placed in parallel with the discrete element (optional parameter).

**KT1 = kt1**

Tangential stiffness (along the axis $y$ of the local frame of the element) of an elastic spring placed in parallel with the discrete element (optional parameter).

**KT2 = kt2**

Tangential stiffness (along the axis $z$ of the local frame of the element) of an elastic spring placed in parallel with the discrete element (optional parameter).

**KT = kt**

Tangential stiffness (along the axes $y$ and $z$ of the local frame of the element) of an elastic spring placed in parallel with the discrete element (optional parameter). If this keyword is present, the keywords KT1 and KT2 must not be used (and conversely), and one then has KT1=KT2=KT.

### 12.6 Factor keyword DIS_ECRO_CINE

These material parameters of an elastoplastic constitutive behaviour with non-linear kinematic hardening, cf. [R5.03.17](doc:r5.03.17), are to be used with the discrete elements 2D_DIS_TR, 2D_DIS_T, DIS_TR, DIS_T (cf. operator AFFE_MODELE [U4.41.01](doc:u4.41.01)). The law is built component by component of the wrench of the forces resulting on the discrete element: there is no coupling between the force components (forces and torques), on which one may define different characteristics; only the diagonal characteristics are affected by the constitutive behaviour. The elastic stiffness ${K}_{e}$ (which is also used by the non-linear algorithm for the prediction) of this constitutive law is given via the keywords K_T_D_L, K_TR_D_L, K_T_D_N, K_TR_D_N of the command [AFFE_CARA_ELEM](doc:u4.42.01) :

The quantities are all expressed in the local frame of the element; it is mandatory to specify the keyword REPERE='LOCAL' in [AFFE_CARA_ELEM](doc:u4.42.01). The orientation of the discrete element may be carried out in [AFFE_CARA_ELEM](doc:u4.42.01) with the usual rules by using the keyword ORIENTATION.

Use of the constitutive law is made in [STAT_NON_LINE](doc:u4.51.03) or [DYNA_NON_LINE](doc:u4.53.01) under the keyword [COMPORTEMENT](doc:u4.51.11) with RELATION='DISC_ECRO_CINE'.

One notes that this constitutive law is not compatible with an alternating cyclic loading.

#### 12.6.1 Operands

**LIMY_DX = fy_dx,**

${F}_{y}^{x}$ : elastic limit in the force direction $x$

**KCIN_DX = kx_dx,**

${k}_{x}$ : kinematic-hardening "stiffness" in the force direction $x$

**PUIS_DX = n_dx,**

${n}_{x}$ : power, defining the shape of the monotonic curve in the force direction

**LIMU_DX = fu_dx,**

${F}_{u}^{x}$ : kinematic-hardening limit, defining the plateau of the monotonic curve in the force direction $x$

**KP = kp**

Normal stiffness (that is, along the axis $x$ of the local frame of the element) of an elastic spring placed in parallel with the discrete element (optional parameter).

**KT1 = kt1**

Tangential stiffness (along the axis $y$ of the local frame of the element) of an elastic spring placed in parallel with the discrete element (optional parameter).

**KT2 = kt2**

Tangential stiffness (along the axis $z$ of the local frame of the element) of an elastic spring placed in parallel with the discrete element (optional parameter).

**KT = kt**

Tangential stiffness (along the axes $y$ and $z$ of the local frame of the element) of an elastic spring placed in parallel with the discrete element (optional parameter). If this keyword is present, the keywords KT1 and KT2 must not be used (and conversely), and one then has KT1=KT2=KT.

### 12.7 Factor keyword DIS_VISC

This non-linear viscoelastic constitutive behaviour is to be used with the discrete elements (cf. [R5.03.17](doc:r5.03.17)) 2D_DIS_TR, 2D_DIS_T, DIS_TR, DIS_T (cf. operator [AFFE_MODELE](doc:u4.41.01)). This constitutive behaviour affects only the local degree of freedom $\mathit{dx}$ of the element. The value of the elastic stiffness ${K}_{e}$ (which is also used by the non-linear algorithm for the prediction) is given via the keywords K_T_D_L, K_TR_D_L, K_T_D_N, K_TR_D_N of the command [AFFE_CARA_ELEM](doc:u4.42.01).

This viscous constitutive law is usable with the operators [STAT_NON_LINE](doc:u4.51.03) and [DYNA_NON_LINE](doc:u4.53.01), under the keyword [COMPORTEMENT](doc:u4.51.11) with RELATION='DIS_VISC'.

The quantities are all expressed in the local frame of the element; it is mandatory to specify REPERE='LOCAL' in [AFFE_CARA_ELEM](doc:u4.42.01). The orientation of the discrete element may be carried out in [AFFE_CARA_ELEM](doc:u4.42.01) with the usual rules by using the keyword orientation.

#### 12.7.1 Operands

The constitutive behaviour DIS_VISC is a non-linear viscoelastic rheological constitutive behaviour, of extended Zener type, making it possible to represent the constitutive behaviour of a uniaxial damper, applicable to the axial degree of freedom of the discrete elements with two nodes (mesh cell `SEG2`) or of the discrete elements with one node (mesh cell `POI1`).

![Figure](/figures/u4.43.01/10000000000002C1000001D6AFD194FD6F4BA377.png)

For the local direction $x$ (and only that one) of the discrete element, one supplies 5 coefficients. Their units must be consistent with the unit of the forces, the unit of the lengths and the unit of time of the problem.

- K1: elastic stiffness of element 1 of the rheological model,
- K2: elastic stiffness of element 2 of the rheological model,
- K3: elastic stiffness of element 3 of the rheological model,
- UNSUR_K1: elastic flexibility of element 1 of the rheological model,
- UNSUR_K2: elastic flexibility of element 2 of the rheological model,
- UNSUR_K3: elastic flexibility of element 3 of the rheological model,
- PUIS_ALPHA: power of the viscous constitutive behaviour of the element $\alpha$,
- $C$: coefficient of the viscous constitutive behaviour of the element.

The conditions to be complied with for these coefficients are (in particular in order to ensure positivity and finiteness of the tangent matrix):

${K}_{1}⩾{10}^{-8}$ ; $1/{K}_{1}⩾0$ ; ${K}_{3}⩾{10}^{-8}$ ; $1/{K}_{3}⩾0$

$1/{K}_{2}⩾{10}^{-8}$ ; ${K}_{2}⩾0$ ; $C\ge {10}^{-8}$ ; ${10}^{-8}\le \alpha \le 1$

Furthermore, one cannot have simultaneously: $1/{E}_{1}=0$, $1/{E}_{3}=0$ and ${E}_{2}=0$, that is the case of the damper alone.

- KP: Normal stiffness (that is, along the axis $x$ of the local frame of the element) of an elastic spring placed in parallel with the discrete element (optional parameter).
- KT1: Tangential stiffness (along the axis $y$ of the local frame of the element) of an elastic spring placed in parallel with the discrete element (optional parameter).
- KT2: Tangential stiffness (along the axis $z$ of the local frame of the element) of an elastic spring placed in parallel with the discrete element (optional parameter).
- KT: Tangential stiffness (along the axes $y$ and $z$ of the local frame of the element) of an elastic spring placed in parallel with the discrete element (optional parameter). If this keyword is present, the keywords KT1 and KT2 must not be used (and conversely), and one then has KT1=KT2=KT.

### 12.8 Factor keyword DIS_ECRO_TRAC

The constitutive behaviour DIS_ECRO_TRAC is a non-linear constitutive behaviour, making it possible to represent the constitutive behaviour of a uniaxial device, along the local axis $x$ and/or in the tangent plane $yz$, of the discrete elements with two nodes (mesh cell `SEG2`) and/or of the discrete elements with one node (mesh cell POI1).
The elastoplastic constitutive behaviour is given by the curve $F_X=\mathit{fonction}(\Delta U_x)$ for the axial direction and the curve $F_{tan}=\mathit{fonction}(\Delta U_{tan})$ for the tangential direction:

- for a `SEG2`, $\Delta U$ represents the relative displacement of the 2 nodes in the local frame of the element.
- for a `POI1`, $\Delta U$ represents the absolute displacement of the node in the local frame of the element.
- for a `SEG2` or a `POI1`, $F$ represents the force expressed in the local frame of the element.

This constitutive law is usable with the operators [STAT_NON_LINE](doc:u4.51.03) and [DYNA_NON_LINE](doc:u4.53.01), under the keyword [COMPORTEMENT](doc:u4.51.11) with RELATION='DIS_ECRO_TRAC'.

The quantities are all expressed in the local frame of the element. The orientation of the discrete element may be carried out in [AFFE_CARA_ELEM](doc:u4.42.01) with the usual rules by using the keyword orientation.

#### 12.8.1 Operand FX

The only data item required is the function describing the non-linear constitutive behaviour. This function must comply with the following criteria:

- It is a function in the sense of code_aster: defined with the operator [DEFI_FONCTION](doc:u4.31.02),
- The interpolations on the axes of the abscissae and of the ordinates are linear,
- The name of the abscissa when the function is defined is DX,
- The extensions to the left and to the right of the function are excluded,
- The function must be defined by **at least**  3 points,
- The first point is $(\mathrm{0.0,}0.0)$ and must be given,
- The function must be strictly increasing.
- The derivative of the function must be less than or equal to its derivative at the point $(\mathrm{0.0,0}.0)$.

Examples of definition of the function:

```text
LesX = (0.0 , 0.2 , 0.3 , 0.50)
LesY = (0.0 , 500.0 , 800.0 , 900.0)

fctsy1 = DEFI_FONCTION(NOM_PARA='DX',
    ABSCISSE = LesX,
    ORDONNEE = LesY,
)

fctsy2 = DEFI_FONCTION(NOM_PARA='DX',
    VALE = (0.00,   0.0,
            0.20, 500.0,
            0.30, 800.0,
            0.50, 900.0),
)
```

The first two points of the function make it possible to define the elastic slope of the constitutive behaviour. The units of the abscissae and of the ordinates must be consistent with those of the problem:

- The unit of the abscissae must be homogeneous to displacements,
- The unit of the function must be homogeneous to forces.

#### 12.8.2 Operand FTAN

The only data item required is the function describing the non-linear constitutive behaviour. This function must comply with the following criteria:

- It is a function in the sense of code_aster: defined with the operator [DEFI_FONCTION](doc:u4.31.02),
- The interpolations on the axes of the abscissae and of the ordinates are linear,
- The name of the abscissa when the function is defined is DTAN,
- The extensions to the left and to the right of the function are excluded,
- The function must be defined by **exactly**  3 points,
- The first point is $(\mathrm{0.0,}0.0)$ and must be given,
- The function must be strictly increasing.
- The derivative of the function must be less than or equal to its derivative at the point $(\mathrm{0.0,0}.0)$.

Examples of definition of the function:

```text
LesX = (0.0 , 0.2 , 0.3)
LesY = (0.0 , 500.0 , 800.0)

fctsy1 = DEFI_FONCTION( NOM_PARA = 'DTAN',
    ABSCISSE = LesX,
    ORDONNEE = LesY,
)

fctsy2 = DEFI_FONCTION( NOM_PARA = 'DX',
    VALE = (0.00,   0.0,
            0.20, 500.0,
            0.30, 800.0),
)
```

The first two points of the function make it possible to define the elastic slope of the constitutive behaviour. The units of the abscissae and of the ordinates must be consistent with those of the problem:

- The unit of the abscissae must be homogeneous to displacements,
- The unit of the function must be homogeneous to forces.

#### 12.8.3 Addition of an elastic spring in parallel

It may be useful to add an elastic element parallel to the discrete element, in particular in order to avoid zero pivots. This may be done via the following parameters:

- KP: Normal stiffness (that is, along the axis $x$ of the local frame of the element) of an elastic spring placed in parallel with the discrete element (optional parameter).
- KT1: Tangential stiffness (along the axis $y$ of the local frame of the element) of an elastic spring placed in parallel with the discrete element (optional parameter).
- KT2: Tangential stiffness (along the axis $z$ of the local frame of the element) of an elastic spring placed in parallel with the discrete element (optional parameter).
- KT: Tangential stiffness (along the axes $y$ and $z$ of the local frame of the element) of an elastic spring placed in parallel with the discrete element (optional parameter). If this keyword is present, the keywords KT1 and KT2 must not be used (and conversely), and one then has KT1=KT2=KT.

### 12.9 Factor keyword DIS_BILI_ELAS

This factor keyword makes it possible to assign a bilinear elastic constitutive behaviour to discrete elements in the 3 directions of translation.

This constitutive behaviour is to be used with the discrete elements (cf. [R5.03.17](doc:r5.03.17)), 2D_DIS_T, DIS_T (cf. operator [AFFE_MODELE](doc:u4.41.01)). The law is built component by component; there is therefore no coupling between the force components, on which one may define different characteristics; only the diagonal characteristics are affected by the constitutive behaviour. The value of the elastic stiffness ${K}_{e}$ (which is used only by the non-linear algorithm for the prediction) of this constitutive law is given via the keywords K_T_D_L, K_T_D_N of the command [AFFE_CARA_ELEM](doc:u4.42.01).

This constitutive law is usable with the operators [STAT_NON_LINE](doc:u4.51.03) and [DYNA_NON_LINE](doc:u4.53.01), under the keyword [COMPORTEMENT](doc:u4.51.11) with RELATION='DISC_BILI_ELAS'.

The quantities are all expressed in the local frame of the element. The orientation of the discrete element may be carried out in the command [AFFE_CARA_ELEM](doc:u4.42.01) with the usual rules by using the keyword orientation.

#### 12.9.1 Operands

The constitutive law is bilinear elastic and requires 3 characteristics. The units of the characteristics must be consistent with those of the problem analysed: $\mathrm{k1}$ and $\mathrm{k2}$ are homogeneous to a force per displacement, $\mathrm{Fp}$ is homogeneous to a force.

![Figure](/figures/u4.43.01/1000000000000181000000FE84694A41E46D03EB.png)

**KDEB_DX = k1_dx, KDEB_DY = k1_dy, KDEB_DZ = k1_dz**

The stiffness of the constitutive behaviour when the force in the discrete element is less than $\mathrm{Fp}$.

**KFIN_DX = k2_dx, KFIN_DY = k2_dy, KFIN_DZ = k2_dz**

The stiffness of the constitutive behaviour when the force in the discrete element is greater than $\mathrm{Fp}$.

**FPRE_DX = fp_dx, FPRE_DY = fp_dy, FPRE_DZ = fp_dz**

The force which defines the transition between the 2 linear constitutive behaviours.

**KP = kp**

Normal stiffness (that is, along the axis $x$ of the local frame of the element) of an elastic spring placed in parallel with the discrete element (optional parameter).

**KT1 = kt1**

Tangential stiffness (along the axis $y$ of the local frame of the element) of an elastic spring placed in parallel with the discrete element (optional parameter).

**KT2 = kt2**

Tangential stiffness (along the axis $z$ of the local frame of the element) of an elastic spring placed in parallel with the discrete element (optional parameter).

**KT = kt**

Tangential stiffness (along the axes $y$ and $z$ of the local frame of the element) of an elastic spring placed in parallel with the discrete element (optional parameter). If this keyword is present, the keywords KT1 and KT2 must not be used (and conversely), and one then has KT1=KT2=KT.

### 12.10 Factor keyword ASSE_CORN

Description of the material characteristics associated with the constitutive behaviour of a bolted assembly [R5.03.32](doc:r5.03.32).

#### 12.10.1 Operands

In the following figure, the plane $\pi$ represents the plane of the assembly. The axis of the bolts is perpendicular to this plane. The reader will refer to [AFFE_CARA_ELEM](doc:u4.42.01) for the orientation of the frame ${R}_{L}$ defining the plane of the assembly.

![Figure](/figures/u4.43.01/1000000000000418000001B03F1BE60711349447.png)

The constitutive relation of the assembly is:

- non-linear in translation along $x$ and in rotation about $y$.
- linear along the other degrees of freedom: $\mathrm{DY},\mathrm{DZ},\mathrm{DRX},\mathrm{DRZ}$

Constitutive behaviours in tension along the axis $x$ and in rotation about the axis $y$.

![Figure](/figures/u4.43.01/100000000000063F0000019762ECCB017D788A48.png)

The constitutive behaviour of the connection is considered linear in the other directions:

**KY**

Stiffness in translation along $Y$

**KZ**

Stiffness in translation along $Z$

**KRX**

Stiffness in rotation about $X$

**KRZ**

Stiffness in rotation about $Z$

**R_P0**

Slope at the origin, or unloading slope

**KP = kp**

Normal stiffness (that is, along the axis $x$ of the local frame of the element) of an elastic spring placed in parallel with the discrete element (optional parameter).

**KT1 = kt1**

Tangential stiffness (along the axis $y$ of the local frame of the element) of an elastic spring placed in parallel with the discrete element (optional parameter).

**KT2 = kt2**

Tangential stiffness (along the axis $z$ of the local frame of the element) of an elastic spring placed in parallel with the discrete element (optional parameter).

**KT = kt**

Tangential stiffness (along the axes $y$ and $z$ of the local frame of the element) of an elastic spring placed in parallel with the discrete element (optional parameter). If this keyword is present, the keywords KT1 and KT2 must not be used (and conversely), and one then has KT1=KT2=KT.

### 12.11 Factor keyword ARME

Description of the material characteristics associated with the constitutive behaviour of an overhead-line cross-arm.

The arm of each cross-arm of a broken phase, represented by a discrete element, has a non-linear force–displacement constitutive behaviour constituted by the difference between the maximum displacement dlp of the end of the cross-arm in the plastic phase and the elastic limit displacement $dle$.

#### 12.11.1 Operands

**KYE = kye**

Elastic slope up to a limit force.

**DLE = dle**

Limit displacement of the elastic strain.

**KYP = kyp**

Plastic slope up to the limit displacement DLP.

**DLP = dlp**

Limit displacement of the plastic strain 0.

**KYG = kyg**

Unloading slope.

![Figure](/figures/u4.43.01/100000000000041500000286B3E9959E48C5A7BE.png)

**KP = kp**

Normal stiffness (that is, along the axis $x$ of the local frame of the element) of an elastic spring placed in parallel with the discrete element (optional parameter).

**KT1 = kt1**

Tangential stiffness (along the axis $y$ of the local frame of the element) of an elastic spring placed in parallel with the discrete element (optional parameter).

**KT2 = kt2**

Tangential stiffness (along the axis $z$ of the local frame of the element) of an elastic spring placed in parallel with the discrete element (optional parameter).

**KT = kt**

Tangential stiffness (along the axes $y$ and $z$ of the local frame of the element) of an elastic spring placed in parallel with the discrete element (optional parameter). If this keyword is present, the keywords KT1 and KT2 must not be used (and conversely), and one then has KT1=KT2=KT.

### 12.12 Factor keyword RELAX_ACIER

The phenomenon of relaxation of the steels used in prestressing is covered by regulations. The main regulations are: BPEL83, NF-EN-1992-1-1 October 2005, AFCEN-ETCC-2010,

One wishes to be able to model strains which will vary slowly in the course of time, in particular in order to take into account the creep of the concrete and the variations of temperature. One also wishes to take into account the influence of temperature on the relaxation phenomena.

In regulatory terms, it would be possible to take into account the effect of the creep of the concrete and of the thermal strain by making a linear combination of the various phenomena (cf. the regulations for more details). This approach is incompatible with a finite-element analysis.

In order for the relaxation law to be usable in a finite-element code for structural analyses with variations of loadings such as: creep of the concrete, re-tensioning of the cables, taking into account of the influence of temperature, … it must be incremental and thermodynamically correct.

The formulation adopted is based on that proposed by J. Lemaitre:

$\sigma =E.{\epsilon}^{e}$     $\epsilon ={\epsilon}^{e}+{\epsilon}^{\mathit{an}}$

${\dot{\varepsilon}}^{\mathrm{an}}=\langle \frac{\sigma –R({\varepsilon}^{\mathrm{an}})}{{f}_{\mathrm{prg}}.k} \rangle^{n}$ with $R \left( {\epsilon}^{an} \right)=  \large {\frac{{f}_{prg}.c.{\epsilon}^{an}}{ {\left(1+{\left(b.{\epsilon}^{an} \right)}^{nr}\right)}^{\frac{1}{nr}}}}$

The constitutive law is 1D, and available only for modellings of bar type, which are used to model the prestressing cables in the operator [DEFI_CABLE_BP](doc:u4.42.04).

In order to take into account the influence of temperature on the relaxation, all the coefficients of the law may be functions of the temperature.

**Note:**

The parameters $c$, $b$, $n$ and $\mathit{nr}$ are dimensionless, and therefore independent of the units used during the study. $k$ is made dimensionless with respect to ${f}_{\mathit{prg}}$ and therefore with respect to the stresses, but not with respect to time. Indeed ${\dot{\epsilon}}^{\mathit{an}}$ is homogeneous to ${[s]}^{-1}$, if the unit of time is the second. Therefore if one knows $k$ for a rate in a given unit of time, it is necessary to convert its value with respect to the unit of time used during the study.

#### 12.12.1 Operands

**$\bf f_{prg}$**

Failure stress of the cable. This quantity is optional, because it may also be defined in the materials BPEL_ACIER or ETCC_ACIER and in that case ${f}_{\mathit{prg}}$ is a constant. The value/function given under RELAX_ACIER_CABL takes priority.

**ECOU_K, ECOU_N**

Correspond respectively to the coefficients k, n, in the equation.

**ECRO_N, ECRO_B, ECRO_C**

Correspond respectively to the coefficients n, b, c, in the equation.

### 12.13 Factor keyword CABLE_GAINE_FROT

This material concerns only the elements CABLE_GAINE. It makes it possible to define the frictional constitutive behaviour between a cable and its sheath or between a cable and the surrounding concrete. It is possible to consider a sliding, frictional or bonded cable. For its use, see the constitutive behaviour KIT_CG in U4.51.11.

#### 12.13.1 Operands

**TYPE**

This operand makes it possible to define whether it is a sliding, frictional or bonded cable.

**PENA_LAGR = pen,**

This operand defines the penalisation coefficient to be taken into account. (See R3.08.10, §6.1 for more details).

**FROT_LINE = fl**

Straight friction coefficient.

**FROT_COURB = fc**

Curved friction coefficient.

### 12.14 Factor keyword FONDA_SUPERFI

The non-linear elastoplastic constitutive behaviour of a rectangular shallow foundation subjected to a static or seismic three-dimensional loading uses the parameters defined for this material, cf. [R5.03.17](doc:r5.03.17) and [R5.03.31](doc:r5.03.31). This constitutive law applies to the 3D discrete elements made up of a single node in translation and rotation (`DIS_TR`) assigned a diagonal stiffness matrix (K_TR_D_N) by means of the relation FONDATION called by the operators for solving non-linear problems [STAT_NON_LINE](doc:u4.51.03) or [DYNA_NON_LINE](doc:u4.53.01).

#### 12.14.1 Operands

**LONG_X**

Length lx along the local axis x of the foundation (mandatory).

**LONG_Y**

Length ly along the local axis y of the foundation (mandatory).

**PHI = phi**

Friction angle in degrees at the soil/foundation interface (mandatory).

**COHESION = cohe**

Cohesion at the soil/foundation interface (homogeneous to a translational stiffness, optional). Equal to 0 by default.

**RAID_GLIS**

Stiffness of the hardening of sliding rg (optional). Equal to 0 by default.

**GAMMA_REFE**

Parameter gr of the kinematic hardening (homogeneous to the inverse of a distance, optional). Equal to 0 by default.

**CP_SERVICE**

Elastic limit force cps of the foundation (mandatory).

**CP_ULTIME**

Ultimate limit force cpu of the foundation (optional). Equal to 0 by default.

**DEPL_REFE**

Reference settlement dr for the isotropic hardening of the bearing-capacity criterion (optional). Equal to 0 by default.

**RAID_CP_[X|Y|RX|RY] = rcp[x|y|rx|ry]**

Stiffness of the kinematic hardening in translation or in rotation along x or y of the bearing-capacity criterion (homogeneous to a translational stiffness, optional). Equal to 0 by default.

**GAMMA_CP_[X|Y|RX|RY]= gcp[x|y|rx|ry]**

Parameter of the kinematic hardening in translation or in rotation along x or y of the bearing-capacity criterion (homogeneous to the inverse of a distance, optional). Equal to 0 by default.

**DECOLLEMENT**

Parameter for activation of the uplift mechanism if equal to 'OUI'. (Optional and equal to 'NON' by default).

**KP = kp**

Normal stiffness (that is, along the axis $x$ of the local frame of the element) of an elastic spring placed in parallel with the discrete element (optional parameter).

**KT1 = kt1**

Tangential stiffness (along the axis $y$ of the local frame of the element) of an elastic spring placed in parallel with the discrete element (optional parameter).

**KT2 = kt2**

Tangential stiffness (along the axis $z$ of the local frame of the element) of an elastic spring placed in parallel with the discrete element (optional parameter).

**KT = kt**

Tangential stiffness (along the axes $y$ and $z$ of the local frame of the element) of an elastic spring placed in parallel with the discrete element (optional parameter). If this keyword is present, the keywords KT1 and KT2 must not be used (and conversely), and one then has KT1=KT2=KT.

### 12.15 Factor keyword JONC_ENDO_PLAS

These parameters serve to define, from the analysis of the reinforced-concrete cross-section: geometry and material characteristics, cf. [R5.03.17](doc:r5.03.17), the non-linear constitutive behaviour of a connection in out-of-plane bending, of the junction between a wall and a floor or a raft, in reinforced concrete, cf. [U2.02.03](doc:u2.02.03), to be applied to discrete elements of type `DIS_TR` on mesh cells `SEG2`, with two nodes. The moments $M$, just like the stiffnesses, are therefore understood in units of torque (length * force).

#### 12.15.1 Operands

**KE**

Elastic rotational stiffness ${K}_{e}>0$. One will take the same value as that supplied in [AFFE_CARA_ELEM](doc:u4.42.01) (DISCRET=(_F(CARA='K_TR_D_*',REPERE='LOCAL',...),),).

**KP**

Plastic hardening slope ${K}_{p}\le {K}_{e}$.

**KDP**

Tangent stiffness ${K}_{d}^{+}\in \left[{K}_{e},{K}_{p}\right]$ in the damaging phase for positive bending.

**KDM**

Tangent stiffness ${K}_{d}^{-}\in \left[{K}_{e},{K}_{p}\right]$ in the damaging phase for negative bending.

**RDP**

Damage-threshold rotation ${\theta}_{d}^{+}>0$ in positive bending.

**RDM**

Damage-threshold rotation ${\theta}_{d}^{-}<0$ in negative bending.

**MYP**

Plasticity threshold ${M}_{y}^{+}⩾{K}_{e}{\theta}_{d}^{+}$ in positive bending.

**MYM**

Plasticity threshold ${M}_{y}^{-}⩽{K}_{e}{\theta}_{d}^{-}$ in negative bending.

### 12.16 Factor keyword INTERF_POU_CINE

This factor keyword makes it possible to specify the anelastic parameters of the soil-pile interaction law INTERF_POU_CINE (see [R7.01.50](doc:r7.01.50)) applicable to the elements 3D_INTSOPLIEU (see [R7.01.49](doc:r7.01.49)).

#### 12.16.1 Operands

**F_TY**

Elastic limit of the soil-pile interface along the axis of the pile

**F_NY**

Elastic limit of the soil-pile interface orthogonal to the axis of the pile

**G_T**

Plastic modulus of the kinematic hardening along the axis of the pile. By default, it is equal to 0 (perfectly plastic)

**G_N**

Plastic modulus of the kinematic hardening orthogonal to the axis of the pile. By default, it is equal to 0 (perfectly plastic)

## 13 Fluid material

Definitions of the constant fluid characteristics.

### 13.1 Operand RHO

```text
RHO =    rho
```

Density of the fluid. No check.

### 13.2 Operands CELE_R and CELE_C

```text
CELE_R =    celr
```

Celerity of propagation of the acoustic waves in the fluid medium (real type).

No check of the order of magnitude.

```text
CELE_I =    celi
```

Imaginary part of the celerity of propagation of the acoustic waves in the fluid medium (the celerity becomes complex in particular for a porous medium). No check of the order of magnitude.

Note: when one uses a modelling of fluid type (3D_FLUIDE for example) and one sets RHO=0. and CELE_R=0., one obtains mass and stiffness matrices which are truly zero by CALC_MATR_ELEM. (see [R4.02.02](doc:r4.02.02)).

### 13.3 Operand PESA_Z

```text
PESA_z = pz,
```

Acceleration of gravity along the axis $z$ , used only, and mandatory, if the modelling chosen in AFFE_MODELE is 2D_FLUI_PESA (gravity waves and sloshing modes in the fluid).

### 13.4 Operand COEF_AMOR

> COEF_AMOR= alpha,

This parameter is defined as the reflection coefficient (amplitude ratio of a reflected P wave). This coefficient is involved exclusively if one defines an absorbing fluid boundary. In fact, the impedance of an absorbing fluid boundary is defined as ${Z}_{C}=\rho c{q}_{\alpha}$ , where $\rho$ is the density of the fluid defined with the operand RHO, $c$ is the celerity of propagation of the acoustic waves defined by the operands CELE_R and CELE_I and ${q}_{\alpha}=(1+\alpha )/(1-\alpha )$ where $\alpha$ is defined with the operand COEF_AMOR. By default this value is $0$ . When $\alpha =0$ one has ${q}_{\alpha}=1$ , that is one is in the presence of a perfectly absorbing boundary. This model is relevant only if one considers the fluid to be compressible. One may choose the value $\alpha =0.2$ commonly accepted for the effect of the sediments at the bottom of a reservoir.

### 13.5 Operand LONG_CARA

> LONG_CARA= alpha,

In order to take into account the first-order radiation condition at infinity, one may define an impedance term ${Z}_{R}=\rho R$ where $R$ is defined with the operand LONG_CARA. In order to approximate asymptotically the constitutive behaviour of a wave by a cylindrical (spherical) wave propagating towards infinity, one generally truncates the fluid domain at a cylinder or half-cylinder (sphere or half-sphere) of radius $R$ . One notes that the first-order radiation condition is equivalent to the exact radiation condition for the propagation of a spherical wave. By default this value is $0$ (the first-order condition is not activated). This parameter has an impact exclusively if one defines an absorbing fluid boundary.

## 14 Material data associated with post-processing

### 14.1 Factor keyword FATIGUE

One may refer to [R7.04.01](doc:r7.04.01) and [R7.04.03](doc:r7.04.03) .

#### 14.1.1 Operand WOHLER

This operand makes it possible to introduce the Wöhler curve of the material in a form discretised point by point. This function gives the number of cycles to failure ${N}_{\mathit{rupt}}$ as a function of the half-amplitude of stress $\frac{\Delta \sigma }{2}.$ .

The Wöhler curve is a function for which the user chooses the interpolation mode:

- LOG LOG: logarithmic interpolation on the number of cycles to failure and on the half-amplitude of the stress (Basquin formula piecewise),
- LIN LIN: linear interpolation on the number of cycles to failure and on the half-amplitude of the stress (this interpolation is not recommended because the Wöhler curve is absolutely not linear in this frame),
- LIN LOG: linear interpolation on the half-amplitude of stress, and logarithmic on the number of cycles to failure, which corresponds to the expression given by Wöhler.

The user must also choose the type of extension of the function to the right and to the left.

#### 14.1.2 Operands A_BASQUIN / BETA_BASQUIN

```text
A_BASQUIN        = A

BETA_BASQUIN    = beta
```

These operands make it possible to introduce the Wöhler curve of the material in the analytical form of BASQUIN [R7.04.01](doc:r7.04.01).

$D=A{S}_{\mathit{alt}}^{\beta}$ where $A$ and $\beta$ are two constants of the material,

${S}_{\mathit{alt}}$ = alternating stress of the cycle = $\frac{\Delta \sigma }{2}$ and $D$ the elementary damage.

#### 14.1.3 Operands A0/ A1/ A2/ A3/ SL

```text
A0    = a0, A1    = a1,A2    = a2,A3    = a3,SL    = SL
```

These operands make it possible to define in analytical form the Wöhler curve in the "current zone" [R7.04.01](doc:r7.04.01).

${S}_{\mathit{alt}}$ = alternating stress = $\frac{1}{2}\frac{{E}_{C}}{E}\Delta \sigma$

$\begin{array}{c}X={log}_{10}\left({S}_{\mathit{alt}}\right)\hfill \\ {N}_{\mathit{rupt}}={10}^{\mathrm{a}0+\mathrm{a}1\mathrm{x}+\mathrm{a}2{\mathrm{x}}^{2}+\mathrm{a}3{\mathrm{x}}^{3}}\\ D=\lbrace \begin{array}{c}1/N\text{si}{S}_{\mathit{alt}}\ge {S}_{l}\\ 0.\text{sinon}\end{array}\hfill \end{array}$

This list of operands makes it possible to introduce the various parameters of this analytical form.

$\mathit{a0}$ , $\mathit{a1}$ , $\mathit{a2}$ and $\mathit{a3}$ constants of the material,

${S}_{l}$ endurance limit of the material.

Young's modulus $E$ is introduced in DEFI_MATERIAU (factor keyword ELAS, operand E).

The value of $\mathrm{Ec}$ , Young's modulus associated with the fatigue curve of the material, is also introduced in DEFI_MATERIAU under the factor keyword FATIGUE, operand E_REFE.

#### 14.1.4 Operand MANSON_COFFIN

```text
MANSON_COFFIN = f_mans
```

This operand makes it possible to introduce the Manson-Coffin curve of the material in a form discretised point by point. This function gives the number of cycles to failure as a function of the half-amplitude of strains $\frac{\Delta \varepsilon }{2}$ .

#### 14.1.5 Operand E_REFE

```text
E_REFE = Ec
```

This operand makes it possible to specify the value of Young's modulus associated with the fatigue curve of the material. This value makes it possible, among other things, to define the Wöhler curve in the "current zone" [R7.04.01](doc:r7.04.01).

#### 14.1.6 Operands D0/TAU0

```text
D0 = d0
```

Makes it possible to specify the value of the endurance limit in pure alternating tension-compression. This value is used in the computation of the criteria of Crossland and Dang Van Papadopoulos [R7.04.01](doc:r7.04.01) by the command POST_FATIGUE [U4.83.01](doc:u4.83.01).

```text
TAU0 = tau0
```

Makes it possible to specify the value of the endurance limit in pure alternating shear. This value is used in the computation of the criteria of Crossland and Dang Van Papadopoulos [R7.04.01](doc:r7.04.01) by the command POST_FATIGUE [U4.83.01](doc:u4.83.01).

### 14.2 Factor keyword DOMMA_LEMAITRE

Under this factor keyword are grouped all the material characteristics required for the computation of the Lemaitre damage and the Lemaitre-Sermage law (option ENDO_ELGA of CALC_CHAMP, [U4.81.04](doc:u4.81.04)).

#### 14.2.1 Operand S

```text
S = s
```

$S$ is a material parameter required for the computation of the Lemaitre damage. $S$ must be a function of the parameter $\mathrm{TEMP}$ .

#### 14.2.2 Operand EPSP_SEUIL

```text
EPSP_SEUIL = Pseuil
```

Makes it possible to specify the value of the damage threshold $\mathit{pd}$ , required for the computation of the Lemaitre damage.

#### 14.2.3 Operand EXP_S

```text
EXP_S = pd
```

Makes it possible to define the Lemaitre-Sermage law; the default value $(1.0)$ corresponds to the computation of the Lemaitre damage.

### 14.3 Factor keyword CISA_PLAN_CRIT

Under this factor keyword are grouped all the material characteristics required for the implementation of the criteria with critical planes [R7.04.04](doc:r7.04.04).

#### 14.3.1 Operand MATAKE_A

```text
MATAKE_A = a,
```

Makes it possible to specify the value of the dimensionless coefficient $a$, present in the criteria MATAKE_MODI_AC and MATAKE_MODI_AV, cf. [R7.04.01] and [U4.83.02](doc:u4.83.02).

#### 14.3.2 Operand MATAKE_B

```text
MATAKE_B = b,
```

Makes it possible to specify the value of the coefficient b, present in the criteria MATAKE_MODI_AC and MATAKE_MODI_AV, cf. [R7.04.01] and [U4.83.02](doc:u4.83.02).

#### 14.3.3 Operand COEF_FLEX_TORS

```text
COEF_FLEX_TORS = c_flex_tors,
```

Makes it possible to specify the value of the ratio of the endurance limits in alternating bending and torsion, cf. [R7.04.01] and [U4.83.02](doc:u4.83.02). This value must be greater than or equal to one and less than or equal to $\sqrt{3}$. This operand is to be used in the criteria: MATAKE_MODI_AC and MATAKE_MODI_AV.

#### 14.3.4 Operand D_VAN_A

```text
D_VAN_A = a,
```

Makes it possible to specify the value of the dimensionless coefficient $a$, present in the criteria DANG_VAN_MODI_AC and DANG_VAN_MODI_AV, cf. [R7.04.01] and [U4.83.02](doc:u4.83.02).

#### 14.3.5 Operand D_VAN_B

```text
D_VAN_B = b,
```

Makes it possible to specify the value of the coefficient $b$, present in the criteria DANG_VAN_MODI_AC and DANG_VAN_MODI_AV, cf. [R7.04.01] and [U4.83.02](doc:u4.83.02).

#### 14.3.6 Operand COEF_CISA_TRAC

```text
COEF_CISA_TRAC = c_cisa_trac,
```

Makes it possible to specify the value of the ratio of the endurance limits in alternating bending and torsion, cf. [R7.04.01] and [U4.83.02](doc:u4.83.02). This value must be greater than or equal to one and less than or equal to $\sqrt{3}$. This operand is to be used in the criteria: DANG_VAN_MODI_AC, DANG_VAN_MODI_AV and FATESOCI_MODI_AV, cf. [R7.04.01] and [U4.83.02](doc:u4.83.02).

#### 14.3.7 Operand FATSOC_A

```text
FATSOC_A = a,
```

Makes it possible to specify the value of the coefficient $a$, present in the criterion FATESOCI_MODI_AV, cf. [R7.04.01] and [U4.83.02](doc:u4.83.02).

### 14.4 Factor keyword HHO

One may refer to [R3.06.14](doc:r3.06.14) .

#### 14.4.1 Operand COEF_STAB

This operand makes it possible to give the stabilisation coefficient for the HHO method. If it is not given, an automatic coefficient is determined as a function of the physics of the problem.

### 14.5 Factor keyword WEIBULL,WEIBULL_FO

Definition of the coefficients of the Weibull model [R7.02.06](doc:r7.02.06).

Briefly, the cumulative probability of failure $P_r$ of a structure is written, in the case of a monotonic loading:

${P}_{r}=1-\exp\left[-\sum_{{V}_{p}}({(\frac{max({\sigma}_{I}-\sigma_{th},0)}{{\sigma}_{u}})}^{m}\frac{{V}_{p}}{{V}_{0}})\right]$

where the summation is carried over the mesh cells ${V}_{p}$ which have plastified (that is, cumulative plastic strain greater than a value chosen arbitrarily ${p}_{s}$ ) and $m,s_u,V_0,\sigma_{th}$ are the parameters of the Weibull model.

In the case of an arbitrary loading path:

${P}_{r}(t)=1-\exp\left[-{(\frac{{\sigma}_{w}}{{\sigma}_{u}})}^{m}\right]$

with:

${\sigma}_{{\omega}^{m}}=\sum_{V}{\left[\max(\underset{\left\lbrace u<t,\dot{p}(u)>0\right\rbrace }{\max}\left\lbrace \tilde{{\sigma}_{I}}(u)\right\rbrace -\sigma_{th}, 0)\right]}^{m}\frac{V}{{V}_{0}}$ ,

$\dot{p}$ denoting the cumulative plastic strain rate, $\tilde{{\sigma}_{I}}$ the largest principal stress at the time instant $t$ [R7.02.06](doc:r7.02.06).

Finally, if the cleavage stress depends on the temperature (WEIBULL_FO):

${P}_{r}(t)=1-\exp\left[-{(\frac{{\sigma}_{\omega}^{0}}{{\sigma}_{u}^{0}})}^{m}\right]$ ,

${\sigma}_{\omega}^{0}$ denoting the Weibull stress defined conventionally for a given ${\sigma}_{u}^{0}$ :

${\sigma}_{\omega}^{{0}^{m}}=\sum_{V}\left[\max(\underset{\left\lbrace u<t,\dot{p}(u)>0\right\rbrace }{max}{\frac{{\sigma}_{u}^{0}.{\sigma}_{I}(u)}{{\sigma}_{u}(\theta (u))}}-\sigma_{th}, 0)\right]^{m}\frac{V}{{V}_{0}}{A}^{{p}^{m}}$

$\theta (u)$ denoting the temperature in the element $\delta V$ .

#### 14.5.1 Operands

```text
M = m, SIGM_REFE = sigmu, SIGM_CNV = sigm0u, VOLU_REFE = V0, SIGM_SEUIL = sigth
```

Parameters associated with the Weibull model.

```text
SEUIL_EPSP_CUMU    =    ps
```

Threshold cumulative plastic strain.

### 14.6 Factor keywords RCCM,RCCM_FO

Definition of the quantities required for the use of the simplified methods defined in the RCC-M regulation [R7.04.03](doc:r7.04.03). These quantities are constant or a function of the parameter ’TEMP’.

#### 14.6.1 Operand SY_02

```text
SY_02    = sy
```

Elastic limit at 0.2% plastic strain at the analysis temperature. This operand may vary as a function of the temperature.

#### 14.6.2 Operands SM/SU/SH

```text
SM = sm
```

Allowable equivalent stress of the material at the analysis temperature. This operand may vary as a function of the temperature.

```text
SU    = su
```

Tensile strength of the material at the analysis temperature. This operand may vary as a function of the temperature.

```text
Sh    = sh
```

Allowable stress of the material at room temperature, cf. POST_RCCM [U4.83.11].

Allowable stress of the material at the maximum temperature, cf. POST_RCCM [U4.83.11].

#### 14.6.3 Operand SC

```text
SC    =    sc
```

Allowable stress of the material at room temperature, cf. POST_RCCM [U4.83.11](doc:u4.83.11).

#### 14.6.4 Operand S

```text
S =    s
```

Allowable stress of the material. This operand varies as a function of the temperature, cf. POST_RCCM [U4.83.11](doc:u4.83.11).

#### 14.6.5 Operands N_KE/M_KE

```text
N_KE = n,M_KE = m
```

These operands make it possible to define the values of $n$ and $m$, two constants of the material.

These characteristics are required for the computation of the elasto-plastic concentration coefficient ${K}_{e}$ , which is defined by the RCC-M as being the ratio between the real strain amplitude and the strain amplitude determined by the elastic analysis.

$\lbrace  \begin{array}{ccc}{K}_{e}=1& \text{si}& \Delta \sigma \le 3{S}_{m}\\ {K}_{e}=1+(1-n)(\frac{\Delta \sigma }{{\mathrm{3S}}_{m}}-1)(n(m-1))& \text{si}& {\mathrm{3S}}_{m}<\Delta \sigma \le 3{S}_{m}\\ {K}_{e}=\frac{1}{n}& \text{si}& {\mathrm{3mS}}_{m}\le \Delta \sigma \end{array}$

#### 14.6.6 Operands A_AMORC/B_AMORC

```text
A_AMORC = a, B_AMORC = b
```

Coefficients of the initiation law.

#### 14.6.7 Operand D_AMORC

```text
D_AMORC =    d
```

Distance of extraction of the stresses.

#### 14.6.8 Operand R_AMORC

```text
R_AMORC =    r
```

Parameter of the relation between stress and effective stress.

### 14.7 Factor keyword CRIT_RUPT

Definition of the quantities required for the critical-stress failure criterion implemented by the keyword POST_ITER/CRIT_RUPT under COMPORTEMENT. If the largest mean principal stress in an element exceeds a given threshold sigc, Young's modulus is divided by the coefficient coef.

This criterion is available for the constitutive laws VISCOCHAB, VMIS_ISOT_TRAC(_LINE) and VISC_ISOT_TRAC (_LINE) , and validated by the tests SSNV226A,B,C.

#### 14.7.1 Operands SIGM_C, COEF

Value of the threshold stress sigc (in units of stress) and of the coefficient coef (dimensionless).

### 14.8 Factor keyword REST_ECRO

Definition of the data required in order to take into account the phenomenon of hardening recovery implemented by the keyword `POST_INCR='REST_ECRO'` under `COMPORTEMENT` in the operators for non-linear mechanical analysis. At the end of each computation time step, the internal variables corresponding to the cumulative plastic strain (isotropic hardening) and/or to the back-stress tensor (kinematic hardening) are modified

This criterion is available for the constitutive laws `VMIS_ISOT_TRAC`, `VMIS_ISOT_LINE`, `VMIS_CINE_LINE`, `VMIS_ECMI_LINE`, `VMIS_CIN1_CHAB` and `VMIS_CIN2_CHAB`, and for the modellings `3D`, `AXIS`, `D_PLAN` and `C_PLAN`.

#### 14.8.1 Rate and form of the recovery

The function `COEF_ECRO` quantifies the rate of recovery.

The recovery is bounded by the parameter `TAU_INF`.
Indeed, one does not recover all of the hardening; the residual hardening is a function of the temperature $T$ and of the initial hardening (before recovery) ${r}_{0}$. One proceeds to the separation of the variables

$$
{r}_{\infty}(T,{r}_{0})={\tau}_{\infty}(T){r}_{0}
$$

The user supplies the function ${\tau}_{\infty}(T)$ by means of the parameter `TAU_INF`.

The parameters `COEF_ECRO` and `TAU_INF` are dimensionless.

#### 14.8.2 Triggering of the recovery

`TEMP_MINI` is the value of the temperature for the start of hardening recovery and `TEMP_MAXI` is the temperature for total hardening recovery.
`TEMP_MINI` and `TEMP_MAXI` have the dimension of a temperature (in °C).
`EPSQ_MINI` makes it possible to shift the triggering of the process by giving a minimum value to the cumulative plastic strain from which the recovery operates for the case of isotropic hardening.
For kinematic hardening it is the parameter `XCIN_MINI` which plays this role (component by component, because it is a tensor and not a scalar).

The parameter `EPSQ_MINI` is dimensionless (strain). The parameter `XCIN_MINI` has the unit of a pressure.

- In the case of a mixed hardening such as `VMIS_ECMI_LINE`, it is the isotropic part (and therefore `ESPQ_MINI`) which governs the minimum threshold for triggering of the recovery.
- For the constitutive behaviours `VMIS_CIN1_CHAB` and `VMIS_CIN2_CHAB`, only the *isotropic* part of the hardening is treated

**Notes**

### 14.9 Factor keyword VERI_BORNE

This keyword makes it possible to check the domain of validity of the parameters of a constitutive law. Indeed, the identification of the parameters of these laws is always carried out in a certain range of strain and of temperature. The aim is to warn the user if, in the study, one leaves this domain in which the parameters have been identified. These bounds are defined under the keyword VERI_BORNE. Exceeding the bounds in the course of the analysis results in the issuing of an alarm.

#### 14.9.1 Operands

Value of the bounds in terms of maximum total strain, strain rate, and extreme temperatures.

### 14.10 Factor keywords POST_ROCHE and POST_ROCHE_FO

Material dedicated solely to the macro-command POST_ROCHE. It includes material parameters linked to the Ramberg-Osgood plasticity law, as well as parameters originating from the RCCM-Rx.

Parameters of function type may depend on the temperature only. The temperature field is supplied via AFFE_MATERIAU/AFFE_VARC/CHAM_GD or EVOL. It is preferable to use the keyword CHAM_GD, but if one is constrained to use the keyword EVOL, it is then necessary to specify the time instant to be taken into account in POST_ROCHE by giving the keyword INST_TEMP.

#### 14.10.1 Operands related to the Ramberg-Osgood law

The Ramberg-Osgood plasticity law is defined by the strain curve as a function of the stress according to the following expression:

$\epsilon =\frac{\sigma}{E}+{\epsilon}_{M,p}(\sigma )$ with ${\epsilon}_{M,p}(\sigma )=K{(\frac{\sigma}{E})}^{1/n}$

$E$ corresponds to Young's modulus, $K$ and $n$ are the parameters of the keyword POST_ROCHE.

#### 14.10.2 Operands related to the RCCM-Rx codification

**RP02_MIN**: minimum conventional elastic limit at 0.2% strain

This parameter is mandatory:

- on the parts defined as "elbows" in the operator POST_ROCHE
- on the whole of the piping line if RCCM_RX=’OUI’ in POST_ROCHE

It is not used in the other cases.

**RM_MIN**: minimum tensile strength

This parameter is mandatory on the whole of the piping line if RCCM_RX=’OUI’ in POST_ROCHE. It is not used in the other cases.

**RP02_MOY**: mean conventional elastic limit at 0.2% strain

This parameter is necessary on the whole of the piping line if RCCM_RX=’OUI’ in POST_ROCHE; if it is not supplied in this case, it is considered equal to 1.25*rp02min. It is not used in the other cases.

**COEF**: Dimensionless coefficient

This parameter is used only if RCCM_RX=’OUI’ in POST_ROCHE.

## 15 Material data associated with external constitutive behaviours

### 15.1 Factor keywords MFRONT, MFRONT_FO

Definition of the parameters relating to a *user* constitutive law defined in the MFront formalism [U2.10.02](doc:u2.10.02). These quantities are constant or a function of the parameter 'TEMP'. It is possible to supply up to 197 parameters.

#### 15.1.1 Syntax

```
|    /    MFRONT = _F (    ♦    LISTE_COEF    =    (c1,c2,...)    [l_R]
                       )
     /    MFRONT_FO = _F (    ♦    LISTE_COEF    =    (c1,c2,...)    [l_fonction]
                          )
```

### 15.2 Factor keywords UMAT, UMAT_FO

Definition of the parameters relating to a *user* constitutive law, that is to say one whose behaviour-integration routine is supplied by the user [U2.10.01](doc:u2.10.01). These quantities are constant or a function of the parameter 'TEMP'. It is possible to supply up to 197 parameters.

#### 15.2.1 Syntax

```
|    /    UMAT = _F (    ♦    LISTE_COEF    =    (c1,c2,...)    [l_R]
                     )
     /    UMAT_FO = _F (    ♦    LISTE_COEF    =    (c1,c2,...)    [l_fonction]
                        )
```

## 16 Simple keyword MATER

The command DEFI_MATERIAU may be re-entrant, but each constitutive behaviour remains unique. One does not in fact allow a constitutive behaviour already present in the material to be replaced, but only the data structure to be enriched with additional material characteristics.

Example of use:

Only the thermal characteristics of the material are defined at first. Then, after the thermal solution, the mechanical properties are added under ELAS:

ACIER_TH=DEFI_MATERIAU(    THER=_F(    LAMBDA=54.6,

> RHO_CP=3710000.0,),);

CHM=AFFE_MATERIAU(        MAILLAGE=MAIL,

> AFFE=_F(    TOUT=’OUI’,

> MATER=ACIER_TH,
> TEMP_REF=20.0,),);

TEMPE=THER_LINEAIRE(    MODELE=MODETH, ...

ACIER_TH=DEFI_MATERIAU(    reuse=ACIER_TH,

> MATER=ACIER_TH,
> ELAS=_F(    E=204000000000.0,

> NU=0.3,
> ALPHA=1.092e-05,),);

RESUT=MECA_STATIQUE(MODELE=MODMECA, ...
